*Status:* done — evidence `tests/browser/customer-quotes.spec.ts` › “QUOTE-002 owner enters disclosure; middle option and add-on become a revised drawn signature and exact job; 390px typed approval works entirely by keyboard” (owner disclosure door, line descriptions, chosen option/add-on, change request, revised drawn approval, exact job, typed keyboard/reflow/contrast, signature record, downloaded signed artifact, named erasure preview and peer withdrawal end states at 1440/390); › “QUOTE-002 customer revision links, change requests, superseded documents, declines and expired updates at 1440 and 390; signing stays gated” (factory gate, stale link and 30-day/day-31 refusal/update queue); `tests/api/customer-quotes.test.mjs` › “QUOTE-002 / COMM-001 the owner accepts concrete disclosure, the customer chooses the middle option and one add-on, requests a revision and signs its immutable exact copy” (versioned consent, signer/capacity/authentication/time/IP/browser and document hashes, immutable embedded-image copy, exact job, confirmation provider outcomes and erasure; named shared-original withdrawal retains the peer’s accepted quote and unrelated original); › “QUOTE-002 concrete disclosure changes and disablement close stale signatures; typed signing competes atomically with a staff answer and never emails twice” (stale consent, typed signature, first-wins and replay); `tests/portable.mjs` › Glove portability (actual rer export/import retains signed HTML bytes and all evidence; old bearer refused, fresh read-only link, erasure and later-backup fence).

#### QUOTE-003 — Deposits on approval.
QUOTE-002 inheritance: charge only against the winning frozen revision and chosen total; the signing/consent gate and price approval still apply.
The owner sets a deposit per quote or per service (an amount or a percentage); approval online takes the deposit by card or ACH through `PAY-002`, the deposit is held against the job and applied to its invoice, and a refund of a deposit follows `PAY-004`.

*Acceptance:* a flow test approves a $1,850 quote with a $500 deposit (Stripe fake), finds the deposit on the job, and checks the final invoice shows $1,350 due.

*Semantics (23 October 2026 conservative default):* only the signed-in unscoped owner sets `service.deposit` for future quote revisions or `quote.deposit` on an unapproved draft: an amount or a percentage above zero and at most 100, with half-up cent rounding against the eventual chosen total. The rule is frozen in the linked revision, chosen-document hash and signed copy; approved/accepted revisions cannot change it. After the winning current price-approved revision is signed under QUOTE-002 and its exact job exists, that owner alone can prepare test card/ACH Checkout (`payment.depositCheckout`). Customer quote bearers cannot invoke a payment door or initiate a charge. A confirmed deposit stays on that job; invoice preparation links it and any prior original-method refund to the final invoice once, and only net confirmed funds reduce the balance. Pending ACH remains pending. Original-method refunds use PAY-004, including before invoice preparation. No public customer Checkout or automatic charge on signing is enabled.

*Status:* partial — evidence `tests/api/customer-quotes.test.mjs` › “QUOTE-003 frozen signed deposit stays owner-only, is held on the exact job, allocates to its final invoice and refunds through the original card” (service/quote rules, signing/price/choice gate, refused customer payment authority, $1,850 quote/$500 confirmed held deposit/$1,350 invoice due, $50 original-card refund); gap: ct customer-side Checkout ruling and customer-initiated payment on online approval; conservative default ADOPTED is deny customer Checkout, signed-in unscoped owner only, show once, no automatic charge. `tests/browser/customer-quotes.spec.ts` › “QUOTE-003 owner sets frozen deposit terms; customer sees the requirement and no Checkout authority at 1440 and 390” proves the owner/customer screens and the adopted gate.

#### QUOTE-004 — Automatic quote follow-up.
QUOTE-002 inheritance: stop on the winning staff or customer-link decision and the frozen validity date; an update request notifies the office and needs a newly approved revision.
The owner sets a follow-up sequence (for example day 2 text, day 5 email, day 10 call task); Glove sends it through `COMM-001`/`COMM-002` for every unanswered quote, stops the moment the customer answers, the quote is closed or it reaches its validity date (`QUOTE-002`), and shows which step each open quote is on.

*Acceptance:* a flow test advances a clock past each step for three quotes, one answered on day 3 and one whose validity date is day 7, and checks exactly which messages were sent, that the answered quote's sequence stopped, and that the expiring quote's day-10 step was never sent.

*Status:* partial — evidence `tests/api/reminders.test.mjs` › “QUOTE-004 three quote sequences stop on day-three answer and day-seven expiry without reusing customer links” (exact email and text steps for three quotes, stopped answer and validity, once-only internal call tasks and explicit completion, replay, immutable business and stop footer, postal-address guard, private explicit unsubscribe and subsequent suppression, no reused quote credential); `tests/browser/reminders.spec.ts` › “PAY-005, QUOTE-004 and COMM-003 owner templates, text consent, visit replies, report notices and stopped states at 1440 and 390” (Settings schedules and template preview, call task and completed state, customer withdrawal). Gap: ct approval of the shared consent, quiet-hours and withdrawal defaults remains. ADOPTED: unscoped owner only, off by default, explicit approved templates, separately recorded purpose consent bound to the current address or number; email follows branch-local 08:00–18:00 and texts the recorded customer zone, with immutable business and opt-out wording. Call steps create internal tasks only, never place calls. The private withdrawal link permits only an explicit stop of quote follow-up emails. COMM-001 retains its existing provider-matrix gaps; no live provider delivery is claimed.

#### QUOTE-005 — Branded quote, invoice and report documents.
Quotes, invoices, service reports and agreements render as branded pages and PDFs with the business identity of `MONEY-002` (logo, legal name and DBA, EIN, the license numbers and insurance a state requires, US address, phone and date formats), terms, photos, and each line's image and the customer-facing description its price-book item carries (`MONEY-003`), from templates the owner edits.

*Acceptance:* a browser journey edits the quote template, opens a quote as a customer and as a PDF, and finds logo, license number, terms, an attached photo and each price-book line's customer-facing description (`MONEY-003`) in both; it then opens an invoice and a service report and finds the EIN, two license numbers and logo set under `MONEY-002` printed on each.

FIELD-003 inheritance: before this capability ships, render the immutable service report as the branded PDF with every snapshot field and flagged form reading, asserted in the document journey.

*Semantics (QUOTE-005, 21 October 2026):* the signed-in owner edits plain-text heading, terms and footer per document kind, and explicitly attaches customer-facing photos. Issuing a quote link freezes the business identity, template, line descriptions/images and attachments in its hashed customer document; completing a report freezes the same identity/template with every report field. Later edits never change those snapshots. Invoices and agreements use their current desk-authorized projection and current template; invoice lines retain the approved job’s customer descriptions/images. Existing role, branch, revision and original-file guards apply. Dates use US calendar presentation and ten-digit phones use US punctuation; saved legacy addresses and time zones remain as recorded. Print / Save PDF invokes the browser’s print renderer after removing a bearer fragment, restores the link after printing, includes the whole document and hides response controls. No signing, send or delivery claim is added; QUOTE-002/006 retain their owner-disclosure gate and COMM-006 inherits the same document and original-file restrictions.

*Status:* done — evidence `tests/browser/documents.spec.ts` › QUOTE-005 branded templates, quote HTML and PDF, invoice, immutable report and agreement at 1440 and 390 (owner edit; quote HTML/PDF logo, licenses, terms, attached photo and each line description; invoice/report EIN, two licenses and logo; agreement; every report snapshot field/flagged reading and originals; extracted PDF text excludes bearer credentials); `tests/api/documents.test.mjs` › QUOTE-005 customer quote documents freeze branding, owner templates, line descriptions and only their scoped original images (immutability, exact originals, authority and erasure), and QUOTE-005 desk invoice and agreement projections use identity and descriptions without granting public or technician access.

#### QUOTE-006 — Field quotes and change orders, offline.
QUOTE-002 inheritance: field signatures retain claimed signer/capacity, versioned affirmed consent and document hashes, first-decision-wins freeze and the owner disclosure gate; no verified-identity or legal-validity claim.
A technician the owner allows builds a quote or a change order on the phone from the price book, including options, and prices the work they add on a `BOOK-005` service call, with or without signal; the customer signs on the phone; the change order adds its lines to the job and invoice; offline drafts send exactly once on reconnect under `GLOVE-06`. Prices outside the owner's allowed range wait for desk approval. A technician's close rate in the `REPORT-002` technician report is their field quotes accepted ÷ answered. A completed `BOOK-006` assessment can become a field quote on the phone, carrying its notes, photos and form results.

*Acceptance:* a browser journey with the network off builds a two-option field quote, captures the customer's signature, reconnects, and finds the approved change order on the job and its lines on the invoice draft; an out-of-range price waits for approval; and the `REPORT-002` technician report shows that technician's close rate from their field quotes; a completed assessment becomes a field quote carrying its photos.

*Implemented draft semantics (24 October 2026; online grant approved by ct at 14:50Z):* the unscoped signed-in owner explicitly enables online field pricing per technician, bound to that technician’s current sign-in. The technician reads selling prices and their own field quotes only while currently assigned to an open job or completed assessment. The ordinary state and offline day remain unpriced. Options use active price-book items, authoritative tax and descriptions, and the owner’s inclusive per-line unit-price bounds expressed as whole percentages of the current price-book price. A price outside those bounds is flagged for the desk; every draft remains subject to ordinary desk price approval. Drafting from a completed assessment retains its notes, photos and form results. Command replay returns only a receipt and never another copy of the priced document. Revocation and reassignment close the read and new-draft doors; sign-in relinking requires a fresh owner grant. A grant never approves a customer-facing price or creates a customer decision, signature, send or job amendment. The technician report counts accepted field quotes divided by accepted or declined field quotes answered in its inclusive business-local date period, to one decimal percent; unanswered drafts are excluded and an empty denominator is unavailable. Its JSON and CSV carry accepted and answered counts. A desk-recorded acceptance defaults its commission seller to the originating technician, retaining the existing explicit desk override. The remaining phone, signing and change-order work retains QUOTE-002, GLOVE-06, TEAM-003 and DATA-001 obligations before it ships. No acceptance point is added.


*Implemented change-order slice (24 October 2026):* a technician explicitly drafts additions with `fieldQuote.draft {changeOrder: true}`; an assessment can only produce a new quote. The signed-in unscoped owner explicitly invokes `fieldQuote.apply` with the current quote and source-job versions. The current revision needs its desk price approval, winning customer acceptance and frozen QUOTE-002 signature, disclosure and signed-copy evidence. The selected lines append once to the same open source job, with their applicable checklists and forms; its ordinary completion and invoice preparation carry those lines while invoice approval remains separate. A field change order cannot create another job. Replays return the receipt only. Activity and export retain the quote revision, signed-copy hash and application actor and time. Existing invoices, recurring billing, discounts, deposits, mismatched frozen tax or a combined rounding difference refuse for explicit desk reconciliation; they are named engineering gaps, never silently repriced. Field drafts without the explicit change-order flag retain the ordinary new-job path. Authority approved by ct 24 October 2026 16:35Z: explicit signed-in unscoped owner application only; scoped or non-owner sessions and unsigned or desk-unapproved changes refuse, and signing alone never amends a job.

*Status:* partial — evidence `tests/api/options.test.mjs` › “QUOTE-006 owner-bound field pricing drafts two options from an assigned assessment, retains evidence once, and withdraws access immediately” (current owner grant, assigned-visit isolation, two options, inclusive price bounds and out-of-range desk approval, assessment notes and photo carry-over, field close-rate JSON and CSV, default seller credit, exact replay, cost redaction, Activity and export, next-request WITHDRAWAL and reassignment; explicit owner application of the signed second option exactly once to its source job and ordinary unapproved invoice draft); `tests/browser/customer-quotes.spec.ts` › “QUOTE-002 owner enters disclosure; middle option and add-on become a revised drawn signature and exact job; 390px typed approval works entirely by keyboard” (signed field option, owner-applied job lines and ordinary invoice draft at 1440 and 390). Gap: phone controls and offline pricing custody, offline two-option signing with QUOTE-002 consent and document evidence, change-order application for existing invoices, recurring billing, discounts, deposits or differing tax snapshots, and full phone browser proof remain. Owner ruling (24 October 2026 14:50Z) approves the online grant: off by default, signed-in unscoped owner only, bound to the technician’s current sign-in and assignment, selling prices only, no margins, immediate withdrawal and desk price approval for every draft. WITHDRAWAL is asserted on the very next request. Application approved by ct 24 October 2026 16:35Z: only the signed-in unscoped owner explicitly applies an already desk-approved and QUOTE-002-signed change order; scoped and non-owner sessions refuse, and signing alone never amends the job. The existing field-pricing HTTP flow asserts these gates.

#### QUOTE-007 — Discounts on quotes and invoices.
A quote option and an invoice take a document discount (a percentage of the subtotal or a fixed dollar amount, with a reason such as senior, military or first-time customer) shown below the subtotal, and discount lines (negative-price lines, from the price book or custom, each marked taxable or non-taxable). Discount lines count in the subtotal under their own taxable flag. The document discount is allocated to non-taxable lines first, then to taxable lines in line order, and the allocation is shown; tax under `MONEY-001` is then computed on the discounted taxable amount and rounded half up once per document. A discount is part of the priced revision approved under `GLOVE-03`, carries unchanged from the accepted quote to the job and the invoice, and appears in the invoice CSV, the `LEDGER-*` payload (as the provider's discount line with its taxable and non-taxable amounts) and reports. The owner limits who may give a discount and its maximum (`TEAM-001`). `PLAN-002`'s member discount follows the same rule.

*Acceptance:* a flow test quotes parts of $400.00 (taxable at 8.25%) and labor of $300.00 (non-taxable) with a $50.00 non-taxable first-time-customer discount line and a 10% document discount, and checks subtotal $650.00, discount $65.00 taken from labor, tax $33.00 and total $618.00; a 50% document discount on the same lines spills $75.00 onto the parts and gives tax $26.81 and total $351.81; the accepted figures are identical to the cent on the job, the invoice, the CSV and the explicitly mapped QuickBooks inspection payload (LEDGER-001 and LEDGER-004 carry the provider-fake assertion before they ship, pattern 1).

*Semantics (QUOTE-007, 19 October 2026; ct approved 18:39Z):* a discount line is a line whose unit price is negative (`-50.00`), custom or from a price-book item of kind `discount` (a fixed negative price, no cost); its line total rounds half a cent away from zero. Each line is taxable or non-taxable as `MONEY-001` decides (item flag, labor rule, exemption); discount lines may not take either pool below zero. The document discount is a percent of the subtotal (above 0 to 100, two places, amount rounded half up to the cent) or a fixed dollar amount, never above the subtotal, with a reason up to 100 characters. It comes off the non-taxable lines first (line order, each line at most its own amount), then taxable lines in line order; tax is the jurisdiction rate on the remaining taxable cents, rounded half up once, and the total is subtotal − discount + tax. Each quote option carries its own discount; add-ons take discount lines only; on acceptance the chosen option's discount applies to the accepted lines (a percent re-applies to their subtotal, an amount stays fixed). The job and invoice carry it; an invoice revision or correction keeps it unless the payload sets another. The CSV writes the discount as up to two rows (non-taxable and taxable amounts); the QuickBooks inspection payload writes a `DiscountLineDetail` with tax after discount and both amounts in its description; revenue reports and job costing use subtotal less discount. Limits: the owner is unlimited; `business.save` `discountLimits` gives each built-in or custom role a maximum percent of the document's gross (positive-line) subtotal, counting discount lines and the document discount together; an unlisted role gives none (`DISCOUNT_LIMIT`), checked on person and agent quote drafts and revisions and invoice preparations, revisions and corrections; carrying an accepted quote's discount unchanged is not giving one; GLOVE-03 approval still decides every price.

*Status:* done — evidence `src/glove/money.ts`, `src/glove/sales-tax.ts`, `src/glove/discounts.ts`, `src/glove/commands/quotes.ts`, `src/glove/commands/invoices.ts`, `src/glove/qbo-invoice.ts`, `src/ui/discount.tsx`, `tests/api/discounts.test.mjs`, `tests/browser/discounts.spec.ts`.

### Scheduling and dispatch

#### DISP-001 — Drag-and-drop dispatch board.
Day, week and month views by technician and by crew, an unscheduled-jobs list, drag-and-drop to book, move, reassign or resize a visit (each drop is a `GLOVE-07` change with conflict review), color by job type or status, and a keyboard path for every drag.

*Acceptance:* a browser journey at 1440 px drags an unscheduled job onto a technician, moves it to another day, triggers and confirms a conflict review, and repeats the move with the keyboard.

*Semantics (DISP-001, 19 October 2026):* the board shows a day (one row per technician across the hours), a week (Sunday to Saturday, technicians by day) or a month (weeks of days, all technicians or one), in the business time zone; inactive technicians show only where they have visits in the shown range. The unscheduled list is the "Waiting to book" card: accepted quotes still to become jobs and open jobs with no booked or started visit. Dragging an unscheduled job onto a technician books it (`visit.book`) for one hour at the dropped quarter hour in the day, or at 9:00 on the dropped day in the week and month (in the month for the chosen technician, else the first active one). Dragging a booked or started visit moves it to the dropped quarter hour, day and technician keeping its length, and dragging its right edge in the day changes its end; each drop asks for the reason and is a `GLOVE-07` change (`visit.change`) with its conflict review. The keyboard path on a focused visit: left and right move it a quarter hour in the day and a day in the week and month, up and down change technician (a week in the month), Shift with left or right changes its end by a quarter hour in the day, Enter reviews the change and Escape drops it; booking by keyboard is the "Book a visit" form. Visits are colored by status (booked, arrived, completed, late evidence, conflict) or by job type (the category of the job's first price-book line, else Uncategorized, as in `COST-004`), with a legend. Views by crew are `DISP-007`'s Rows choice on the same board.

*Status:* done — evidence `src/ui/dispatch.tsx`, `src/ui/dispatch-board.tsx`, `src/glove/commands/dispatch.ts`, `tests/browser/journey.spec.ts`.

#### DISP-002 — Map board and travel time.
The dispatch board shows today's visits and technicians on a map (Google Maps), drive time between consecutive visits from a routing provider, and a directions handoff to the technician's navigation app (Apple Maps, Google Maps, Waze). With the map and site coordinates, the owner draws service-area zones for technicians beside their ZIP codes (`DISP-004`), and booking applies the same warn-or-refuse area rule to a site outside every zone the technician serves (moved here from `DISP-004`, ct 19 October 2026).

*Acceptance:* a browser journey opens the map board with a fixture day, sees pins and drive times (provider fake), and the technician's phone offers a directions link with the site's address; a flow test draws a zone for a technician and checks a booking at a site outside it gets the area warning. It also carries `DISP-006`: travel time from the previous visit joins the automatic-assignment ranking.

*Semantics (DISP-002, 22 October 2026):* only the owner connects Google Maps Platform with their own key, kept as an installation secret (never in state, Activity, a command result, the business export, the installation archive or a log; a restored installation needs it entered again). While it is connected, saving a site with a new or changed address places it with Google's Geocoding API (the street address only goes to Google) and keeps its latitude and longitude on the site, or says Google found no such place; the desk places listed sites again with `site.locate`. The day board shows a route map over the board (`GET /api/glove/map?date=`, desk roles only): Google's base map (Static Maps API, asked for by center and zoom only) under Glove's own numbered pins for each technician's stops in order, the legs between consecutive stops with the drive time and distance from Google's Routes API (driving, traffic-unaware; only the stops' coordinates go to Google), and the zones drawn for each technician; a stop whose site is not on the map is listed with **Place on the map**. On the technician's phone each visit offers directions to the site's address in Apple Maps, Google Maps or Waze. The owner draws a technician's zones on the map (`technician.zones`: up to 20 named polygons of 3 to 100 corners, by clicking corners or typing them). A technician's service area is their ZIP codes and drawn zones together: with zones drawn, a site whose ZIP is not listed and which is not inside any zone (a site not on the map is inside none) falls under the owner's area rule, warned for review or refused, exactly as `DISP-004`'s ZIP rule. With Google Maps connected, `DISP-006`'s automatic assignment adds to each candidate's booked minutes their drive minutes from their previous visit that day (the latest ending by the new start; none, no drive) to the job's site, and picks the fewest booked-plus-drive minutes, then by name, naming each one's booked and drive minutes in the ranking; a site Google cannot place, or a drive with no route, refuses the automatic booking so the dispatcher chooses. Live technician positions are `DISP-005`'s, and route optimization `DISP-003`'s, each carrying the map board in its text and acceptance.

*Status:* done — evidence `src/glove/maps.ts`, `src/glove/mercator.ts`, `src/glove/commands/maps.ts`, `src/glove/eligibility.ts`, `src/glove/commands/dispatch.ts`, `src/ui/dispatch-map.tsx`, `src/ui/technician.tsx`, `tests/api/eligibility.test.mjs`, `tests/browser/dispatch-map.spec.ts`.

#### DISP-003 — Route optimization.
For a day or a technician, Glove proposes the visit order and assignment that respects arrival windows, skills and service areas and minimizes drive time, shows the time saved, and applies it only when the dispatcher accepts; it runs as a bounded background job.

*Acceptance:* a flow test optimizes a fixture day of 12 visits for 3 technicians (routing provider fake), checks no arrival window or skill is broken and the drive time falls, and checks nothing moves until accepted.

*Semantics (DISP-003, 22 October 2026):* a signed-in owner or dispatcher requests a day or one technician with `route.optimize`; the existing 15-minute Worker cron claims one durable job per pass. A job covers 2–20 visits and up to 8 assigned technicians. It requests coordinate-only, traffic-unaware Google Routes driving times with a ten-second abort, then performs at most 1,200 appointment exchanges within twenty seconds. It retains appointment duration and arrival windows, validates skills, working availability, service areas, overlaps and driving time against the whole proposed schedule, and keeps crews and visits with field evidence fixed. It saves the original and proposed slots and driving comparison; no visit moves in the background. The board shows the proposal and time saved. `route.accept` requires a person's reason, fresh live dispatch/branch authority, unchanged day/eligibility dependencies and a fresh provider matrix, then commits every move and assignment history together. Discard, provider refusal, expiration and stale proposals move nothing. The comparison measures driving between visits, with no depot legs; the bounded improvement search does not claim a global optimum. Restore retains proposal metadata but requires Google Maps to be reconnected; customer erasure discards affected undecided proposals and redacts their erased references.

*Status:* done — evidence `tests/api/routing.test.mjs` › “DISP-003: 12 visits for 3 technicians improve through a bounded background proposal, respect windows and skills, and move only on dispatcher acceptance” proves the 12/3 day, lower driving time, windows/skills/areas, unchanged visits before explicit acceptance and exactly-once moves; › “DISP-003: stale eligibility, discard and a routing-provider refusal never change appointments” proves stale/provider refusal and discard; `tests/browser/routing.spec.ts` › “DISP-003 the dispatcher compares driving saved and explicitly accepts the route, at 1440 and 390” proves the proposal/comparison and accepted board at both widths.

#### DISP-004 — Skills, availability, time off and service areas.
Technicians carry skills and certifications (for example EPA 608, licensed electrician), working hours, time off and service areas (ZIP codes; drawn zones arrive with the map under `DISP-002`); booking warns or refuses (by owner setting) when the technician lacks a required skill, is off or outside the area; availability can be read from Lanyard or Google Calendar.

*Semantics (DISP-004, 19 October 2026):* calendar availability is read from the calendar's secret iCal address (Google Calendar, Outlook and most calendars publish one). The address is a credential: the owner or that technician sets it, it is stored apart from Glove's records (`glove_secrets`), only they can read it back, and it never appears in state, the timeline, a command result, the business export, the installation archive or a log; a restored installation needs it entered again. Reading it keeps only busy spans (start and end, no titles) for the next 90 days with the time they were read; an event marked free or cancelled is not busy; UTC and TZID times are exact; floating times and all-day dates are read in the business time zone; DAILY and WEEKLY repeats are expanded and other repeats are read at their first occurrence only and counted. A booking overlapping a busy span falls under the owner's availability rule (warn or refuse) and names when the calendar was read.

*Semantics (DISP-004 Lanyard availability, 19 October 2026; ct approved 23:45Z and 23:58Z):* only the owner connects Lanyard, one technician at a time, by entering that technician's Lanyard staff ID explicitly (Glove never matches staff by name or email) and a Lanyard bearer key. The key is sealed as an installation secret, apart from Glove's records, and never appears in state, Activity, a command result, the business export, the installation archive or a log; a restored installation needs it entered again. Glove only sends GET requests to Lanyard's fixed availability path for that staff ID, refuses redirects, and sends nothing about customers, sites or jobs. From the answer it keeps only that staff member's normalized availability windows and the start and end instants of their approved leave for the next 90 days, with the time they were read; recurring patterns keep Lanyard's own semantics, and pending or declined leave is ignored. Glove never keeps names, payroll, preferences, leave reasons or any other staff member's data. A booking outside an availability window or over approved leave falls under the owner's existing availability rule (warn or refuse) and names when Lanyard was read. A failed read shows a generic message, keeps the last snapshot and labels it with the time it was read; disconnecting clears the key and the snapshot; an expired key shows Reconnect Lanyard to the owner.

*Egress (DISP-004, ct's EGRESS RULE):* the Lanyard origin is entered by the owner, so Glove accepts only an https origin on a public host (no IP literal, private, loopback, link-local or local name) with no username, password or key in the URL; redirects are refused, and the key travels only in the request's Authorization header. This read is Glove's declared outbound connection to Lanyard under its privacy and outbound rules (Operating rules: provider credentials are installation secrets that never travel in an export). It ships only once Glove adopts Base's egress door, which checks the host on its resolved address with no DNS-rebinding gap (gr-base-285).

*Acceptance:* a flow test books a gas job for a technician without the gas skill (refused), on their day off (refused) and outside their area (warning), then books it for an eligible technician; a flow test reads a technician's calendar (provider fake), refuses a booking over a busy span and books a free one, and checks the address is readable only by the owner and that technician and absent from state, timeline, command results and export. A flow test (Lanyard fake) has the owner connect a technician with an explicit staff ID and key, checks the key is absent from state, Activity, command results, the export and the archive, checks Glove sent only GETs to the fixed availability path with nothing about customers or jobs and refused a redirect, checks only that staff member's availability windows and approved-leave instants for the next 90 days are kept (Lanyard's recurring patterns as Lanyard defines them; pending and declined leave ignored; no names, payroll, preferences, reasons or other staff), warns or refuses a booking by the owner's setting, keeps and labels the last snapshot with its read time after a failed read with a generic message, clears it on disconnect, shows reconnect for an expired key, and refuses an http, non-public-host or credential-bearing origin.

*Status:* partial — evidence `src/glove/eligibility.ts`, `src/glove/calendar.ts`, `src/glove/commands/calendar.ts`, `src/glove/commands/dispatch.ts`, `src/ui/availability.tsx`, `tests/api/eligibility.test.mjs`, `tests/portable.mjs`; gap: availability is not read from Lanyard (Lanyard has no read door for availability yet); Base egress door: `DISP-004` stays partial until Glove adopts Base's egress door for the owner-entered Lanyard origin (gr-base-285).

#### DISP-005 — Live technician location and ETA.
While clocked in and on the job, a technician's phone shares location (with their consent, never off the clock), or Glove reads a fleet GPS provider (Linxup, Samsara, Verizon Connect); the board shows each technician's position and an ETA to the next visit, which feeds `COMM-004`.

*Acceptance:* a flow test posts phone locations and a fleet-provider feed (fake), checks positions and ETAs on the board, and checks no location is recorded after clock-out. It also carries `AGENT-003`: the delay watch adds a late technician's position and ETA to its at-risk reasons and same-time options.

*Semantics (DISP-005, ct approved 25 October 2026 04:20Z with the delegated-view rider in DISP-009):* location sharing starts off. Only the technician's current person session can record or withdraw their explicit consent; the phone requests browser permission and captures only while visible, online, clocked in and running a travel or job timer on its current assigned visit. Locations never enter the offline outbox. The owner can explicitly connect one Samsara vehicle per technician and request its current GPS snapshot from the fixed documented host; that read requires the same consent, shift and visit checks. Coordinates captured before the shift or timer, more than two minutes ago, in the future beyond ten seconds, or after assignment, consent or sign-in withdrawal are refused. A current position goes only to the owner within their branch scope, and its coordinates go to the already-connected Google Routes API for a traffic-unaware driving estimate to the next booked visit. This is a driving estimate from the moment of reading, not a departure promise; the delay watch retains its existing overrun and promised-window rules. Missing routes and stale positions never invent an ETA. The most recent position replaces the previous one in transient storage; it is hidden after two minutes and removed by the next product command, clock-out, timer stop, reassignment or consent withdrawal. Coordinates and fleet keys are absent from state, Activity values, command results, business exports and installation archives. Consent changes and generic capture outcomes are audited without coordinates. Dispatcher and agent location reads remain denied; delegated views belong to `DISP-009`; the existing agent delay proposals keep their current authority. `COMM-004` inherits customer notices and `FIELD-005` inherits native background capture before those capabilities ship.

*Status:* done — evidence `tests/api/eligibility.test.mjs` › “DATA-002 / DISP-002 map and location flow” (consent off by default and refused capture; consented capture refused without a shift or assigned timer; two uninterrupted phone fixes leave exactly one stored row with the second coordinates and the owner's map reads it; owner positions and ETAs with Office, Dispatcher and agent views denied and storage unchanged; explicit owner-only Samsara request and audited outcome with no polling on board reads; simultaneous phone and fleet sources; stale and unavailable states; AGENT-003 delay reasons and same-time options; clock-out, withdrawal, reassignment and revoked-session stops; no coordinates or keys in durable records or business export); `tests/portable.mjs` › installation export and restore journey (populated phone position and fleet credential excluded from the archive; restore has no position and requires fleet reconnection); `tests/browser/dispatch-map.spec.ts` › “DISP-002 the map board shows the day's pins and drive times, the owner draws a zone, and the technician's phone hands directions to their navigation app, at 1440 and 390” (consent, fresh capture, both sources and ETAs, delay reasons and options, keyboard tooltips, phone Clock out and withdrawal at both widths).

#### DISP-006 — Arrival windows and automatic assignment.
Visits, `BOOK-005` service calls' included, are booked with a customer arrival window (for example 8–10 am) separate from the planned time; when a booking comes from `BOOK-002` or `AGENT-001`, Glove assigns the best eligible technician (skills, area, load, travel) under the owner's rules, and the dispatcher can override. `BOOK-006` assessments are booked with an arrival window too.

*Acceptance:* a flow test books three online requests and checks each lands on an eligible technician inside its window, then the dispatcher reassigns one with a `GLOVE-07` review; an assessment on a request is booked with its arrival window.

*Semantics (DISP-006 arrival windows, 20 October 2026):* `visit.book`, crew bookings, `BOOK-005` service-call visits, `BOOK-006` `assessment.book` and approved dispatch proposals take an optional `window {start, end}`: wall-clock HH:MM on the visit's local date in its booking zone, start before end, kept on the visit as `arrivalWindow` beside the planned start and end. The planned start must fall inside the window, both ends included; otherwise the booking is refused (`WINDOW`). `visit.change` keeps the visit's window unless it names a new one, and `window: null` removes it; a move whose planned start leaves the kept window is refused. The window shows in the booking event ("9:00 AM – 10:30 AM (arrival window 8:00 AM – 10:00 AM)"), on the dispatcher's visit panel and on the technician's phone. Recurring series occurrences (`PLAN-001`) carry no window. `visit.book` (service calls included) and `assessment.book` also take `technicianId: "auto"`: Glove picks, among active technicians in the job's branch with no conflict at that time (skills, areas, time off, calendars, other visits and the owner's warn/refuse rules; an out-of-area warning is a conflict, so every candidate is in-area), the one with the fewest booked minutes on that local date, then by name; the assignment records "Assigned automatically" with the ranking, `NO_TECHNICIAN` answers when nobody is free, and the dispatcher overrides with `visit.change` under `GLOVE-07` review. With Google Maps connected, each candidate's drive from their previous visit that day joins their booked minutes (`DISP-002`'s semantics), and online bookings (`BOOK-002`) and the front office (`AGENT-001`) call this assignment, each naming `DISP-006` in its text and acceptance (ct approved as pattern-1 DONE, 20 October).

*Status:* done — evidence `src/glove/commands/dispatch.ts`, `src/glove/commands/assessments.ts`, `src/ui/dispatch-cards.tsx`, `src/ui/assessment.tsx`, `src/ui/visit-panel.tsx`, `tests/api/assessments.test.mjs`.

#### DISP-007 — Multi-day jobs, crews and subcontractors.
A job can span several days and several technicians at once; a crew is booked as one, and the `DISP-001` board shows its day, week and month by crew, a crew dragged as one; subcontractors sign in with their own limited role, see only their visits, and their 1099 totals are kept (`COST-002`).

*Acceptance:* a flow test books a three-day install for a two-person crew and a subcontractor, checks each sees only their visits, and checks the subcontractor's total in the 1099 export; a browser journey drags the crew's visit to another day on the `DISP-001` week board by crew.

*Semantics (DISP-007, 19 October 2026; the role, crews and 1099 totals as ct approved 19 October 11:36Z):* a job holds any number of visits over any days. The owner (acting for "admin") names crews of 2 to 10 active technicians or subcontractors; changing a crew changes only later bookings. Booking a crew books one visit per member at the same date and times in one command, after one review of every member's conflicts. Moving any visit of that booking moves every member's visit in it that is not completed or canceled to the same date and times, never to another technician, after one review. Canceling cancels them all, and is refused while a member has recorded evidence on theirs. The dispatch board's Rows choice shows crews instead of technicians in the day, week and month, one chip per crew booking, dragged or moved by keyboard as one. Subcontractor is a built-in role, invited and signed in like staff. A subcontractor sees only the visits now assigned to them and, for those, the job title, checklist and forms, the site's address and access notes, the customer's name and phone, and their own captures. They never see prices, costs, quotes, invoices, the price book, equipment, the timeline, other people's work or other visits. They record arrival, checklist items, notes, photos, materials by description, forms and completion on their visits. They cannot clock in, run timers, record refrigerant or expenses, or add priced work, and their phone keeps no offline day and gets no pushes (`FIELD-001` and `FIELD-004` are the Technician's). The owner marks a technician record as a subcontractor with a 1099 payee name and the last 4 digits of the TIN; a full TIN is refused and never stored. The owner sets an agreed amount on each of their visits. The 1099-NEC totals for a calendar year, which the owner and office read as JSON or CSV, sum each subcontractor's agreed amounts on their visits completed that year (dated by the completion Glove received, in the business time zone), with the visit count and whether the total is $600 or more. They are labelled "Totals for your filing, not a filed 1099"; Glove files nothing. Timesheets and payroll exports stay `COST-002`'s.

*Status:* done — evidence `src/glove/commands/dispatch.ts`, `src/glove/subcontract.ts`, `src/glove/view.ts`, `src/ui/dispatch.tsx`, `src/ui/subcontract.tsx`, `tests/api/subcontract.test.mjs`, `tests/browser/journey.spec.ts`, `src/glove/commands/field.ts`.

#### DISP-008 — Google Calendar and Outlook sync.
Each technician's visits appear in their Google or Microsoft 365 calendar (one way, updated on change, customer details limited by owner setting), and busy blocks from that calendar count against availability.

*Acceptance:* a flow test books, moves and cancels a visit and checks the calendar provider fake receives create, update and delete, and that a busy block refuses an overlapping booking.

*Semantics (24 October 2026):* the unscoped owner explicitly connects one delegated Google or Microsoft calendar per technician and approves one-way visit writes. Customer details default to hidden; the owner may include customer name and service address only. No attendees or contact channels are sent. Credentials, pending delivery work and event mappings are excluded from archives; restore requires explicit reconnection. All committed visit changes enqueue provider work, including technician reassignment. Booking refreshes busy time and refuses an unreadable provider; only busy instants are retained. Uncertain writes remain visible and stop later work on that connection, without automatic replay. Disconnect stops writes and retains existing provider events for owner review. The existing private iCal connection remains independent.

*Status:* done — `tests/api/eligibility.test.mjs` proves Google and Microsoft create, update and delete, overlapping busy refusal, owner detail limits, command replay, credential exclusion and read failure refusal through the production HTTP door with provider-edge fakes. `tests/browser/dispatch-map.spec.ts` proves the owner connects, reads and disconnects both providers through Team at 1440 and 390, with explicit customer-detail consent and no horizontal page overflow. No live provider proof is claimed.

#### DISP-009 — Delegated dispatcher position view.
The owner may grant a Dispatcher position view per technician and shift. The technician is told the view is on, every read is audited, and custody remains latest-only. Agent reads remain denied unless the owner grants the same view to a named agent. This is ct's 25 October 2026 rider to `DISP-005`, assigned the next free numeric ID.

*Semantics:* The unscoped owner explicitly grants an active Dispatcher or named agent a view of one technician’s exact open shift. The phone status names viewers. Current recipient authority, owner authority and branch scope are rechecked on reads. Every delegated map read commits a coordinate-free audit before returning data. Revocation and shift closure stop access; another shift never inherits it. The grant table is transient and excluded from archives. Latest-only position custody and the existing owner-only delay explanation remain unchanged.

*Status:* done — `tests/api/eligibility.test.mjs` › “DATA-002 / DISP-002 map and location flow” (owner-only current-shift grants; separate named-agent grant; ungranted reads denied; every delegated map read audited without coordinates; phone status names viewers; withdrawal, branch scope and next-shift refusal; latest position unchanged). `tests/browser/dispatch-map.spec.ts` › “DISP-002 the map board shows the day's pins and drive times, the owner draws a zone, and the technician's phone hands directions to their navigation app, at 1440 and 390” (owner grants to Dispatcher and named agent, technician notice and delegated map at both widths, keyboard tooltips, clock-out and withdrawal). Latest-only coordinates and archive exclusions under DISP-005 remain unchanged; transient grants are separately excluded by `scripts/installer/local.mjs`.

### Customer communication

#### COMM-001 — Customer email with delivery status.
Quotes, invoices, receipts, confirmations, reports and purchase orders to suppliers (`STOCK-002`) are emailed from Glove through the owner's provider (SendGrid, Postmark, Amazon SES or the Base mail adapter) from the business's verified domain; each message records queued, delivered, bounced or complained from provider events, and a failure shows on the record with what to do next. CAN-SPAM rules apply to anything not strictly transactional. This clause also inherits morning email delivery of the owner's `AGENT-008` brief; it is off until the owner turns it on, and nothing is ever pushed.

DATA-002 inheritance: before this capability ships, every new command, communication, payment or sync action and provider outcome keeps its actor/provider, time, references and actual effect in Activity and the business export; credentials and secret payloads never enter the trail.

*Acceptance:* a flow test sends a quote, an invoice and a purchase order to its supplier (provider fake), replays delivered and bounced events, and checks each message's state on the timeline and the bounce warning on the customer. It also delivers the owner's `AGENT-008` brief in the morning and asserts the provider delivery outcome without a duplicate delivery for that business-local date.

The flow also asserts each new DATA-002 event in Activity and the business export, including provider outcomes and references without secrets.

COMM-007 inheritance: its sent and received messages (or agent conversations) appear in the COMM-007 stream in order, with direction, channel and author.

*Semantics (COMM-001 partial slice, 21 October 2026):* the owner connects customer email (`mail.connect`): SendGrid or Resend through Base's mail adapter, or Postmark or Amazon SES through fixed provider endpoints, a from-address and optional name, the provider API key and, for SendGrid/Resend, its event-webhook verification key (SendGrid's Signed Event Webhook public key or Resend's `whsec_` signing secret), kept as installation secrets that are never shown again, exported or archived; `{provider: null}` disconnects. Glove does not check the sending domain itself: the provider refuses mail from a domain it has not verified, and that refusal shows on the message. The desk emails what each person may already record as sent (`email.send`): an approved quote's current revision to the customer's email (owner, dispatcher, office; the quote is then sent, naming the email), an approved or exported invoice to the customer's accounts-payable address, else their email (owner, office), and a draft purchase order to its supplier's address (owner, office; the order is then sent); `agreement.offer` with `email: true` emails a `PLAN-004` booking link to the customer's own email address. Every email Glove sends today is transactional; no marketing email is sent. Replies go to the business email when one is set. A message record keeps its kind, subject, recipient, from-address, provider and who sent it, never the body, so a booking link's bearer exists only in the email and the one-time command response. After the command commits, Glove makes one send attempt with the message id as the delivery id. The provider's answer (accepted, refused, rate limited or not confirmed) and every authenticated event the provider posts to `POST /api/glove/mail/events` (delivered, deferred, bounced, complained, failed, rejected) are provider facts on the message's timeline, in Activity and in the business export with the provider's reference; Glove authenticates each callback under the rules below (using Base's verifier for SendGrid/Resend), and Base's durable store keeps each matched event once, and applies an event only to a message Glove sent through that provider to exactly that recipient; authenticated unmatched events return 200 without effect. A message's state is the latest of its facts in Base's order: a final fact (delivered, bounced, complained, failed, rejected) is never replaced by a non-final one, and a later final fact (a complaint after delivery) replaces an earlier one. "Delivered" means the recipient's mail server accepted it, as the provider reported; Glove never claims a person read it. Each failure shows on the message with what to do next. A customer whose latest finished email to an address on their record bounced or was marked as spam carries that warning, with what to do next, until a later email to that address finishes delivered or the address changes. Each email is in the `COMM-007` stream as an outbound email with its author, recipient and state. Nothing is retried automatically: the desk sends again, or for a booking link revokes it and makes a new offer. QUOTE-002 inherits a confirmation copy of the winning signed revision to the on-record email, with the same provider outcomes, before online signing ships. Inheritance (pattern 1): receipts join with `PAY-002` and `PAY-004`, appointment confirmations with `COMM-003` (which also carries `FIELD-003` report links), and review requests with `COMM-008`; each emails through this door with the same provider outcomes before it ships, and any email that is not strictly transactional also carries the CAN-SPAM unsubscribe link and postal address.

*Semantics (COMM-001 morning brief email, 21 October 2026):* only the owner turns it on or off (`mail.morning {enabled, time}`, with customer email connected first; `time` is a business-local `HH:MM`, 07:00 by default); it is off until then, and opening the brief never sends anything. Glove's cron trigger runs every 15 minutes; the first run whose business-local time (in the business time zone) is at or past `time` on a local date emails that date's brief, as `AGENT-008` prepares it for that instant, to the owner's active sign-in address (the owner account's email, not the business email) through the connected provider; a business-local date gets at most one send, claimed once as a `queued` fact in the timeline before the provider is called, so a repeated or simultaneous run sends nothing more and a crash between claim and send loses that date's email rather than sending it twice. A run before `time`, with email off or disconnected, or without an active owner address sends nothing. The provider's answer (accepted, refused, rate limited or not confirmed) and its signed delivery events (`POST /api/glove/mail/events`, accepted only for that recipient and that date's delivery id) are provider facts on that date in Activity and the business export, with the provider's reference and no key or brief body; the Today screen and `GET /api/glove/brief` (`emailed`) show the date's state exactly as those facts fold, with what to do next, and "delivered" means the recipient's mail server accepted it. Nothing is retried: a failed date stays failed and the next local date sends its own brief. The email carries the brief's own text and, being a transactional notice to the owner of their own records, no marketing content.

*Callback authentication (COMM-001, ct scope-review-66 approved 21 October 2026):* authentication precedes delivery-payload parsing, message lookup, database writes and outbound effects. SNS necessarily decodes its bounded signed envelope and fetches only its pinned certificate to verify the signature; its SES Message remains unparsed until verification. Disconnected email returns 404. Every failed authentication/schema/recipient check returns the same generic 401 body, writes no Activity, durable counter, receipt/event ID or other business record, and logs only a generic Worker warning without secrets. After 20 rejections from one IP in a minute, further rejected requests return 429; verified traffic is never counted or refused by that rejection allowance. Authenticated events for messages Glove did not send through that provider return the same opaque 200 acknowledgement as matched events, with no effect and no consumed event ID; a wrong recipient for a known message refuses the entire batch. The owner's email settings show the last verified matched event time and any SNS cancellation warning. Unauthenticated and unmatched events never change delivery state, bounce/spam warnings, suppression/opt-outs, subscriptions or inbound email, request, conversation or customer records; never write Activity/export or reveal message/customer/recipient existence. Every future inbound-email receiver inherits this authentication before shipping.

Postmark: HTTPS Basic user `glove`, with a per-installation password generated from 32 random bytes and shown only in the first owner response. Store only its SHA-256 hash and compare in constant time. `mail.callback {action: rotate}` keeps the previous hash valid for exactly 24 hours (exclusive expiry), `revokePrevious` ends that overlap immediately, and disconnect revokes both. Rotation, revocation and allowlist changes are Activity facts without secrets. The optional source-IP allowlist is off by default, pinned in source with a dated provider reference, fails closed when on and never replaces Basic authentication; reconnecting credentials preserves this owner option unless explicitly changed. Plain-HTTP Postmark callbacks are refused without writes. Replay identity is event type plus Postmark ID, or message ID + type + recipient + time when no ID exists; refuse events over 7 days old. Sends include Glove's delivery ID in Postmark metadata. Delivery maps to delivered, hard bounce to bounced, soft bounce to deferred or failed when final, and SpamComplaint to complained.

Amazon SES via SNS: pin region, TopicArn and configuration set to the owner's connection. Require the message-type header to equal the envelope Type, accept SNS signature versions 1 and 2 (setup asks for version 2), and require the signing certificate at `https://sns.<region>.amazonaws.com/SimpleNotificationService-*.pem` with no query, credentials, fragment, alternate port or redirects; cache the certificate within its validity for at most one hour. SNS timestamps are at most 1 hour old and at most 5 minutes ahead. Require a signed SNS envelope (raw delivery off), the pinned SES configuration set and Glove delivery tag on each SES event; sends carry the configuration set and a base64url-encoded delivery ID tag. Deduplicate SNS MessageId and the SES event. A SubscriptionConfirmation URL is fetched only after all source, URL, timestamp and signature checks; require HTTPS on the same pinned regional SNS host with the exact ConfirmSubscription action, TopicArn and Token, and follow no redirects. An UnsubscribeConfirmation warns the owner and never fetches its URL. Delivery maps to delivered; Permanent bounce to bounced; Transient bounce to failed; DeliveryDelay to deferred; Complaint to complained; Reject and RenderingFailure (including AWS's `Rendering Failure` spelling) to rejected. SendGrid and Resend retain their signatures, with the same shared no-effect unmatched rule. Callback credentials, hashes and AWS/API keys are excluded from exports and archives, and restored email must be reconnected.

*Proven acceptance (COMM-001, 24 October 2026):* `tests/api/email.test.mjs` › “COMM-001 a quote, an invoice and a purchase order emailed through the owner's provider record delivered and bounced from signed provider events” (SendGrid, Resend, Postmark and Amazon SES routing and outcomes; Postmark and SES document delivery and bounce replay, exact job facts, customer warning and recovery, conversation order and authors, refusal, rate limiting, malformed answers, uncertainty, explicit resend and Activity and export with each provider reference); › “COMM-001 the owner's morning brief email is sent once per business-local date and records the provider outcome” (Postmark and SES concurrent separate-isolate date claims, complete brief including overdue invoices, delivered and bounced facts, refusal, rate limiting and uncertainty without same-date retry, next-date recovery and provider references); › “COMM-001 callback boundary authenticates before lookup, ignores unmatched without IDs, refuses whole wrong-recipient batches and limits only rejected IP traffic” (one 20-request allowance across two Worker isolates, 21st rejection, independent IP, minute reset, verified bypass and generic no-write rejection); › “COMM-001 Postmark generates hash-only Basic credentials, rotates with 24h overlap and revoke, enforces optional pinned IPs, age and replay, and maps delivery outcomes”; › “COMM-001 SES and SNS pins topic, region, configuration set and tag, verifies signatures v1 and v2, certificates and freshness before effects or subscription fetches, and deduplicates both IDs” (approved provider callback boundaries); `tests/api/customer-quotes.test.mjs` › “QUOTE-002 / COMM-001 the owner accepts concrete disclosure, the customer chooses the middle option and one add-on, requests a revision and signs its immutable exact copy” (SendGrid, Postmark and SES immutable confirmation copies and delivered outcomes); `tests/api/agreements.test.mjs` › PLAN-004 forty due entitlements flow (eight working emailed offers through each provider); `tests/api/payments.test.mjs` › PAY-004 three installments flow (payment and refund receipts through each provider); `tests/api/reminders.test.mjs` › COMM-003 owner-approved email templates flow (each provider's existing notices and report links under the retained authority and consent limits); `tests/browser/email.spec.ts` › “COMM-001 owner connects email and morning settings; the desk emails quotes, invoices and supplier orders with provider outcomes at 1440 and 390” (settings, document outcomes, both providers' failure and next-step screens, explicit recovery, unknown morning outcome and next-date delivered state); `tests/portable.mjs` › Glove portability (real archive and restore, callback password and hash exclusion, old credential refused and reconnection required). No live provider delivery is claimed; the clause permits provider-edge fakes. Rejection accounting uses a shared transient actor keyed by a hashed source address, with no durable counter or business write. The approved no-automatic-retry, callback, custody and future-clause inheritance rules above are unchanged.

#### COMM-002 — Texts from a registered US number.
The owner connects Twilio (or another provider) with a US number registered for A2P 10DLC or toll-free verification; Glove records each customer's SMS consent (source, time, wording), honors STOP, HELP and START in every thread, keeps quiet hours by the customer's time zone, and records sent, delivered and failed per message. The owner keeps their existing business number: it is ported to Twilio, or text-enabled in place as a hosted number while voice stays with the current carrier, with the port or hosting status shown until it is live; a new number is the owner's choice, never the only path. A customer's recorded opt-out also keeps them out of every `CUST-004` segment push.

DATA-002 inheritance: before this capability ships, every new command, communication, payment or sync action and provider outcome keeps its actor/provider, time, references and actual effect in Activity and the business export; credentials and secret payloads never enter the trail.

*Acceptance:* a flow test texts a consenting customer (provider fake), replays delivered and failed callbacks, receives STOP and checks the next send is refused with the reason, and checks no automated text is sent in quiet hours; a hosted-number order and a port request (provider fake) show their status until the number is live, and sends then use it; a customer who replied STOP is left out of a `CUST-004` segment push. It also carries `AGENT-003`: a texted customer's reply confirming or refusing a visit moved around a delay lands on that visit, replacing "customer not yet asked", and an `AGENT-009`-granted agent move texts the customer.

The flow also asserts each new DATA-002 event in Activity and the business export, including provider outcomes and references without secrets.

COMM-007 inheritance: its sent and received messages (or agent conversations) appear in the COMM-007 stream in order, with direction, channel and author.

*Status:* partial — evidence `tests/api/reminders.test.mjs` › COMM-003 owner-approved email templates flow (registered connection starts disabled, separate explicit enable, source and wording consent, exact immutable footer, quiet hours, signed callback and replay, reschedule request, STOP and START, segment exclusion and export without credentials); › Erasure during an automatic provider attempt flow (text erasure); `tests/browser/reminders.spec.ts` › “PAY-005, QUOTE-004 and COMM-003 owner templates, text consent, visit replies, report notices and stopped states at 1440 and 390” (owner connection and purpose consent); `tests/portable.mjs` (secret exclusion and reconnection after restore). Gap: hosted-number orders and port requests with live status remain unbuilt; connecting an already registered live number records only an explicit owner affirmation. Delay-specific confirmation and refusal and granted agent-move sends under AGENT-003 remain unproved. ct approval of the owner-only off-by-default channel, purpose consent and customer-zone quiet-hours defaults remains. No live registration or provider receipt is claimed.

#### COMM-003 — Appointment confirmations and reminders.
Booking, moving or canceling a visit (a `BOOK-005` service call's included) sends the customer a confirmation by text and email (by preference); reminders go at owner-set times before the window (for example 24 hours and 2 hours); the customer can confirm or ask to reschedule from the message. The wording of every automated message (confirmations, reminders, on-my-way, quote follow-ups under `QUOTE-004`, payment reminders under `PAY-005`, review requests) is an owner-edited template with merge fields and a preview; the opt-out wording and business name cannot be removed. An assessment visit (`BOOK-006`) sends the same confirmation and reminders.

*Acceptance:* a flow test books, moves and cancels a visit and checks the exact messages and their timing, and a reply "reschedule" opens a request on the job; an owner-edited reminder template with merge fields is exactly what the customer receives, and its opt-out wording cannot be removed; booking an assessment on a request sends its confirmation.

FIELD-003 inheritance: before this capability ships, the customer receives its service-report link through the configured notice channel with the provider outcome asserted.

COMM-001 inheritance: before this capability ships, every confirmation, reminder and report-link notice it emails goes through `COMM-001`'s door with the same provider outcomes: one send attempt per message, recorded with the provider's answer and reference on the message, the visit's timeline, Activity and the business export without secrets; state changes only from authenticated provider callbacks; delivery is never claimed without the provider's event. Any email that is not strictly transactional also carries the CAN-SPAM unsubscribe link and postal address.

*Implemented email semantics (23 October 2026; conservative default pending ct):* the owner previews plain-text templates through `reminder.preview`, then explicitly approves templates and the schedule through `reminder.configure`. The immutable footer adds the business name and office-handled stop instructions. No links or markup are accepted in templates. The owner records consent or withdrawal separately for visits, invoices and quotes through `reminder.consent`; changing the recorded recipient address invalidates consent. Automatic email is off by default. The scheduled Worker sends only between 08:00 inclusive and 18:00 exclusive in the record branch zone, rechecking current owner authority, connection, consent and business state. Due steps are claimed durably before one provider attempt; retries and concurrent passes never resend a claimed step. Unknown outcomes remain unknown. Historical messages are never a resend source. A changed visit supersedes its unsent notice and old reminder times; cancelled visits have no later visit reminder. Every visit creation door uses the shared commit path, including assessments and recurring or customer-booked visits. The pass reserves at most 50 messages. PAY-005 uses frozen invoice due dates and current confirmed balances, stops on disputes and zero, and exposes actual state in aging. QUOTE-004 uses elapsed 24-hour days after the revision was recorded sent and stops on any current-revision customer response, closure or the frozen expiry instant. Erasure removes recipient consent, visit intent and message records through DATA-001. The subsequent bounded text channel, explicit customer response and report-link doors, and quote-purpose unsubscribe are described in `docs/sms-channel.md`. The defaults remain pending ct approval; future notice kinds inherit the same immutable footer and explicit template approval before shipping.

*Status:* partial — evidence `tests/api/reminders.test.mjs` › “COMM-003 owner-approved email templates confirm and move visits including assessments, respect consent and quiet hours, and never replay an attempt” (exact approved wording and timing, booking, move, cancellation and assessment notices, authenticated provider outcomes, customer confirm and reschedule with unchanged booking, explicit emailed report link, consent withdrawal, text quiet hours, authenticated text replies, STOP and segment exclusion, privacy erasure); the same COMM-003 template flow also proves address change, unknown outcome and the owner switch through its retained refusal assertions; › “Erasure during an automatic provider attempt never restores recipient addresses in the outcome” (email and text erasure while provider response is pending); `tests/browser/reminders.spec.ts` › “PAY-005, QUOTE-004 and COMM-003 owner templates, text consent, visit replies, report notices and stopped states at 1440 and 390” (template editor and previews, disabled and enabled connection, purpose consent, customer responses and desk request, received report, stop states). Gap: ct approval of consent and quiet hours, existing-number affirmation, explicit visit response credential and explicit report-link email defaults remains. ADOPTED: unscoped owner only, automation off by default, explicit approved templates and purpose consent bound to the current address or number; texts additionally record source, wording and customer time zone. Only an explicitly owner-issued, expiring response link reveals the visit title and window, never contact or address; it records the first confirm or reschedule request without changing the booking or verifying identity. The owner explicitly emails a completed report through the configured provider. Automatic email confirmations and reminders still direct replies to the office rather than including the response link. COMM-004 and COMM-008 retain on-my-way and review-template obligations before shipping. COMM-001 retains its provider-matrix gaps; COMM-002 retains number ordering and delay-agent inheritance.

#### COMM-004 — On-my-way and job status texts.
The technician taps "on my way" (or Glove detects departure under `DISP-005`) and the customer gets a text with the technician's name, photo and ETA; arrival, completion and report-ready notices follow by owner setting.

*Acceptance:* a browser journey taps on-my-way on the phone and checks the customer text (provider fake) carries name and ETA; completion sends the report link.

FIELD-003 inheritance: before this capability ships, the report-ready notice carries its exact service-report link, with the provider delivery outcome asserted.

*Adopted status-notice boundary (26 October 2026; pending ct):* All three notice settings start off. The unscoped owner previews and explicitly approves exact plain-text templates and each setting, including seven-day report sharing; the business name and STOP wording cannot be removed. Only the current assigned technician explicitly taps on my way, online, with a consented on-clock travel timer and fresh position for that visit. The server privately derives a driving estimate; the customer receives the technician’s first name and ETA plus a two-hour link to their photo, never coordinates or a movement history. Only the technician can upload their own photo and explicitly consent; metadata is stripped, replacement or withdrawal deletes the original and links even after profile deactivation, and expiry, assignment change, customer consent withdrawal or changed owner authority closes public reads. The photo bearer is hash-only in transient storage, omitted from archives, and grants only first name and sanitized image. The tap implements the clause’s explicit-departure alternative; no detected-departure send is added. Arrival follows its separate exact owner-approved template only when the assigned technician’s fresh arrival reaches the server within two minutes; stale offline evidence still records arrival without a misleading text. Completion can issue the immutable report’s seven-day link only under the approved report setting, current customer consent, quiet hours and current configuring-owner authority. Only a hash persists; the bearer exists in the one provider attempt. Replay never resends, uncertain delivery is not called delivered, and revoked or expired report links stop reading. COMM-002, COMM-003 and COMM-005 retain their existing consent and channel obligations; DISP-009 retains delegated location views.

*Status:* partial — evidence `tests/browser/reminders.spec.ts` › “COMM-004 owner previews status templates; technician shares photo and ETA, arrival and the exact completed report at 1440 and 390” (explicit owner settings and exact previews, phone tap with technician first name, consented photo and current driving ETA, arrival text and completed report text, exact report-link content and authenticated delivery outcome at both widths); `tests/api/reminders.test.mjs` › “COMM-004 owner-approved tap sends first name, photo and live ETA; arrival and completion follow owner settings once” (off-by-default refusal, personal photo consent and sanitized image, two-hour bearer isolation and withdrawal even after profile deactivation, fresh arrival and stale offline suppression, current assignment and consent, no coordinates in texts, one attempt on replay, exact seven-day report bearer, signed delivery, revocation, quiet-hours suppression and Activity with business export without credentials). Gap: ct approval of adopted technician-triggered first-name, photo and ETA disclosure, owner-configured arrival texts and automatic seven-day report-link issuance remains. COMM-002 retains registration, number ordering and its pending messaging-policy obligations; no live provider receipt is claimed.

#### COMM-005 — Two-way texting inbox.
Replies land in a shared inbox, threaded by customer and linked to the open job, with assignment, unread state and canned replies; technicians text customers from the job through the business number, never their personal number.

DATA-002 inheritance: before this capability ships, every new command, communication, payment or sync action and provider outcome keeps its actor/provider, time, references and actual effect in Activity and the business export; credentials and secret payloads never enter the trail.

*Acceptance:* a browser journey receives a customer reply (provider fake), assigns it, answers with a canned reply, and finds the thread on the customer and the job. It also carries `AGENT-003`: a customer's confirmation reply to a moved visit is threaded on the customer, the job and that visit.

The flow also asserts each new DATA-002 event in Activity and the business export, including provider outcomes and references without secrets.

COMM-007 inheritance: its sent and received messages (or agent conversations) appear in the COMM-007 stream in order, with direction, channel and author.

*Status:* partial — evidence `tests/api/reminders.test.mjs` › “The desk explicitly retains, assigns and answers a customer text on its job; STOP and role withdrawal close the send door” (owner opt-in retention, desk assignment, explicit same-customer job link, canned-text reply, provider outcome, customer and job conversation, Activity and business export, STOP and revoked-role refusal); `tests/browser/journey.spec.ts` › “The Owner runs the whole phone desk and Office repeats its permitted steps with exact role refusals at 390 and 1440” (Owner and Office text inbox, assignment and canned reply on a phone and desktop). Gap: Technician arbitrary-text sending and delay-move confirmation threading on the visit remain unbuilt. Adopted explicit owner opt-in body retention, desk-only body audience and person-triggered replies require ct approval. The channel retains current-number visit consent and customer-zone quiet hours, including STOP; shared-number senders reveal no retained body and cannot be answered until resolved.

#### COMM-006 — Customer hub.
A customer opens their hub from a sign-in link sent to their email or phone and sees their quotes (approve and sign; each price-book line shows the customer-facing description its item carries, `MONEY-003`), upcoming visits (confirm, reschedule within policy), invoices and receipts (pay), service reports, equipment, agreements (renew, update card) and can request new work; they see only their own records.

DATA-002 inheritance: before this capability ships, every new command, communication, payment or sync action and provider outcome keeps its actor/provider, time, references and actual effect in Activity and the business export; credentials and secret payloads never enter the trail.

*Acceptance:* a browser journey signs a customer in by a link, finds a quote's price-book line with its item's customer-facing description (`MONEY-003`), approves the quote, pays an invoice (Stripe fake), requests work, and checks another customer's link reads none of it.

The flow also asserts each new DATA-002 event in Activity and the business export, including provider outcomes and references without secrets.

FIELD-003 inheritance: before this capability ships, the customer finds and reads their service reports in the hub, with report/file isolation asserted.

COMM-007 inheritance: its sent and received messages (or agent conversations) appear in the COMM-007 stream in order, with direction, channel and author.

**CT preparatory policy — 26 October 2026; APPROVED WITH RIDERS.** Only a signed-in Owner without branch limits may explicitly inspect one customer’s proposed customer-facing content. The projection includes customer document fields of sent or decided quotes, customer-facing price-book descriptions, approved invoices, confirmed-payment summaries, visits, completed-report summaries, equipment and agreements. It shows proposed customer communications content, not proof that any content was sent, received or read.

Exclude draft documents, private notes, access notes, internal conversations, credentials, payment authorization evidence and report files. Report summaries provide no original-file access. Existing document, customer, record and original-file restrictions remain binding. The projection does not authenticate a customer, assert control of their email or phone, issue a credential or link, grant customer access, approve or sign a quote, request work, change a visit or agreement, send a message, update a payment method or authorize a payment.

Enforce the Owner restriction at the command boundary. Signed-out people, branch-limited Owners, other staff roles, custom roles, integration tokens and agents gain no projection access through this policy. Refusal reads “An Owner without branch limits must sign in to inspect this customer preview” and reveals no customer content. An unavailable customer reads “Customer preview unavailable” without exposing another customer’s records.

Each successful inspection records the actual Owner, time, selected customer reference and inspection effect in Activity and business export, without copying the projection body, private fields or credentials into the trail. Inspection creates no communication or payment effect. Restore reconstructs the projection only from permitted restored records under current authority; it restores no session, customer credential, link or sending or payment authority. DATA-001 erasure removes underlying personal content and personal saved outcomes or timeline copies, and the projection must not reconstruct erased content. The trusted later erasure journal prevents an older backup from reviving erased content; an offline destination cannot discover a later erasure without that journal.

COMM-006 remains PARTIAL. Preserve the complete customer-hub target and every original acceptance point. Gaps include customer sign-in and credential issuance, expiry and revocation; customer-facing screens and cross-customer isolation; quote approval and signing; invoice payment and receipts; work requests; visit confirmation and policy-bound rescheduling; report discovery, reading and original-file isolation; agreement renewal and card updates; and COMM-007 portal communications. Existing staff send, receive, delivery-outcome and conversation-history targets remain with their owning communication clauses. Complete customer browser acceptance at 1440 and 390 remains required. This inspection policy proves no customer authentication, delivery, payment authority or legal compliance.
