*Status:* partial — `tests/api/customer-quotes.test.mjs` › “COMM-006 Owner hub preview isolates one customer and excludes drafts, access notes, payment credentials and internal conversations” reports proof of the owner-only projection, price-book customer description, approved invoices, completed-report summaries, equipment, separation from another customer, draft and secret exclusion, signed-out and role refusals, branch-scoped Owner refusal and attributable Activity and export. This is repository-reported evidence, not independently executed by CT. It proves no customer sign-in or browser acceptance. Gap: Customer sign-in and credential lifecycle, customer-facing screens, quote approval and signing, invoice payment, work requests, report originals and isolation, visit responses, agreement renewal and card updates, and COMM-007 portal conversations remain unbuilt; the complete 1440 and 390 browser acceptance remains unproved. Approved with riders: the CT preparatory policy above governs; implementation of every rider is not established. COMM-006 stays PARTIAL.

#### COMM-007 — One conversation history per customer.
Calls, texts, emails, notes, portal actions and agent conversations appear on the customer and job timeline in one stream with their direction, channel, author and delivery state.

*Semantics (COMM-007, 21 October 2026, under ct's ruling of 20 October):* the stream is read from `GET /api/glove/conversation?customerId=` or `?jobId=` and shown as Conversation on the customer and on the job. The desk (owner, dispatcher, office) logs a call (inbound or outbound; answered, voicemail or no answer; optional whole minutes from 0 to 600 and what was said) or a note (text required) on a customer and, optionally, one of that customer's jobs (`conversation.log`); a merged-away customer takes no new entries. Every message Glove sends or receives today is in the stream, each with its direction (inbound, outbound or internal), channel, author, recipient, who recorded it and its delivery outcome: requests as they arrived (channel = the request's source), request follow-ups, quotes recorded as sent and the customer's recorded acceptance or decline (`GLOVE-04`), purchase orders recorded as sent to the supplier for the job (`STOCK-002`), Web Push visit notices to technicians about the job with the push service's answer (`FIELD-004`), and every agent action on the customer's records (`GLOVE-10`, `AGENT-*`). A send made outside Glove reads "Not confirmed: sent outside Glove"; a push the service accepted reads "Handed to the push service (HTTP n); arrival on the phone is not confirmed"; a note reads "Internal note: not sent to the customer"; Glove claims no delivery a provider did not report. Entries are ordered oldest first by the moment Glove recorded them (UTC, millisecond); entries recorded in the same millisecond keep a fixed order. The customer's stream holds every entry for the customer, their jobs and any customer merged into them; a job's stream holds the entries on that job, its quote and the request the quote answered. Viewers and agents read the stream like the desk; technicians and subcontractors do not. Sign-in emails to staff and segment pushes to Mango or Mailchimp are not customer messages and are not in the stream. A property's history (`CUST-001`, `GET /api/glove/sites/<id>/history`) carries the same entries as `message` rows for the desk roles and viewers, newest first with the rest of that history: those on the jobs at the property and on the requests and quotes made for it; a call or note logged on the customer without a job belongs to no property and stays in the customer's Conversation; field roles read no message there. Inheritance: texts, emails, calls on the business number, portal actions and agent conversations join the stream as `COMM-001`, `COMM-002`, `COMM-005`, `COMM-006`, `COMM-009` and `AGENT-001` ship; each carries that obligation in its own acceptance.

*Acceptance:* a flow test logs a call and a note on one job, reads them with today's real sends (a request and its follow-up, a quote recorded as sent and its signed acceptance, a purchase order recorded as sent, a delivered and a refused Web Push notice) and an agent's quote draft, in order, from the customer and job streams with direction, channel, author and delivery; field roles and agents cannot log, field roles cannot read, a merged customer's entries read on the survivor, and the logged call and note are in Activity and the business export. Inheritance: `COMM-001`, `COMM-002`, `COMM-005`, `COMM-006`, `COMM-009` and `AGENT-001` each assert that its sent and received messages (or agent conversations) appear in the COMM-007 stream in order, with direction, channel and author.

*Status:* done — evidence `src/glove/conversation.ts`, `src/ui/conversation.tsx`, `tests/api/conversation.test.mjs`, `src/glove/history.ts`, `src/glove/api.ts`, `tests/browser/journey.spec.ts`.

#### COMM-008 — Review requests after paid jobs.
After a job is complete and paid (owner chooses the trigger), Glove asks the customer for a Google review by text or email, at most once per owner-set period per customer, never asks a customer who opted out, and records the request and any review that the Google Business Profile connection reports. Every customer gets the same request: Glove never filters requests by sentiment.

*Acceptance:* a flow test completes and pays two jobs for one customer within the period and checks one request was sent; an opted-out customer gets none. COMM-001: assert each emailed review request goes through the owner's provider (fake) with its provider outcome on the message, customer and job timelines, Activity and export without secrets, and that any email not strictly transactional carries the CAN-SPAM unsubscribe link and postal address.

COMM-001 inheritance: before this capability ships, every review request it emails goes through `COMM-001`'s door with the same provider outcomes: one send attempt per message, recorded with the provider's answer and reference on the message, the customer and job timelines, Activity and the business export without secrets; state changes only from authenticated provider callbacks; delivery is never claimed without the provider's event. Any email that is not strictly transactional also carries the CAN-SPAM unsubscribe link and postal address.

**Approved current default — explicit review email.** Review email is off by default. The current signed-in owner explicitly configures one uniform request and explicitly requests every email; enabling this preparatory and manual capability does not enable automatic execution or retries. “Unscoped owner” means the owner's established current installation authority, without a branch-scoped or application-token credential; it never bypasses existing session, role, customer, job, branch or recipient boundaries.

Review-email permission is separate from visit notices, quote follow-ups and SMS permission. The owner records the customer's affirmative permission with its source or reference and time against the current email address; a changed address requires permission for that address. An address on file or owner configuration approval is not customer consent. Existing do-not-contact, marketing and review opt-outs prevail. Every eligible customer receives the same configured request, without sentiment filtering, with the business name, postal address and a purpose-specific unsubscribe.

The period is per customer across jobs. Every provider attempt consumes it, including refusal, rate limiting, failure and uncertain delivery; changing the job or message identity cannot bypass it. An uncertain result remains unknown, never presumed unsent and never automatically retried. Queueing, provider acceptance and authenticated delivery are distinct facts. Configuration, consent and withdrawal, explicit requests or skipped reasons, and provider outcomes and references retain their existing attributable Activity, timeline and export custody without credentials or unsubscribe bearers.

This approves only the current manual-email boundary. COMM-008 still requires owner-selected automatic trigger execution, owner controls, text requests under COMM-002, and recording reviews reported by Google Business Profile. Those capabilities inherit the same eligibility, consent, uniform wording, period and outcome controls; COMM-003 retains owner-editable template obligations. DISP-009 is unchanged.

*Status:* partial — `tests/api/reminders.test.mjs` › “COMM-008 explicit uniform email requests require completed paid jobs, current consent and a per-customer period” (unpaid refusal; two completed and paid jobs produce one explicit request; provider acceptance and signed delivery on message, job timeline, customer history, Activity and export; mandatory postal address and unsubscribe; separate withdrawal prevents a later request; changed-address and Office-send refusal; refused, rate-limited, uncertain and authenticated bounced attempts consume the shared period despite job and template changes, with no automatic retry; no bearer in export). Gap: Automatic trigger execution, owner browser controls, text requests and Google Business Profile review retrieval remain unbuilt. Approved with riders: the current capability is off-by-default, explicit owner-triggered email under the owner's established current authority; unscoped does not bypass access or recipient boundaries. Separate source and time-recorded permission must match the current email, existing opt-outs prevail, and every request uses the uniform configured wording, postal address and purpose-specific unsubscribe. Assert address-change and unauthorized-send refusal, the shared customer period across jobs, period consumption for every provider attempt including refusal, rate limiting, failure and uncertainty, no automatic send or retry, authenticated outcome distinctions and attributable secret-free history. The cited test proves its named manual-email slice, not every rider. Automatic triggers, owner browser controls, texts under COMM-002 and Google review retrieval remain required and unbuilt; COMM-003 retains owner-editable review templates. Status remains PARTIAL. `tests/browser/reminders.spec.ts` › “PAY-005, QUOTE-004 and COMM-003 owner templates, text consent, visit replies, report notices and stopped states at 1440 and 390” proves separate review withdrawal and changing between private preference links at both widths.

#### COMM-009 — Calls on the business number.
Inbound calls to the business's Glove number ring the office and on-call phones in order, forward to the owner's existing phone system if set, play a recording notice where the owner records calls, text back a missed caller with a booking link, and log every call (caller, duration, recording, outcome) on the customer; once the existing number is ported (`COMM-002`), it is the business number.

DATA-002 inheritance: before this capability ships, every new command, communication, payment or sync action and provider outcome keeps its actor/provider, time, references and actual effect in Activity and the business export; credentials and secret payloads never enter the trail.

*Acceptance:* a flow test replays Twilio voice webhooks (fake) for an answered call and a missed call, checks the call log, the recording notice and the missed-call text.

The flow also asserts each new DATA-002 event in Activity and the business export, including provider outcomes and references without secrets.

COMM-007 inheritance: its sent and received messages (or agent conversations) appear in the COMM-007 stream in order, with direction, channel and author.

**Adopted preparatory call boundary (27 October 2026; pending ct):** Call routing starts off and only a signed-in Owner without branch limits explicitly configures the connected existing Twilio number, its ordered office and on-call destinations and optional final forwarding destination. The present slice accepts only an unambiguous current customer number; unknown and shared-number callers remain a Gap, without creating customer identities. Signed callbacks retain exact provider call references, durations and outcomes. Only that Owner authority reads native call metadata and routing configuration. Recording is refused and no recording notice or recording access is claimed. Missed-call webhooks never send automatically. The Owner may explicitly request one booking-link text for an unreplied missed call, using their still-valid offer for that customer, separate source and wording consent for the current number, STOP protection and customer-zone quiet hours. The body and booking bearer remain transient; a durable attempt permits one provider request, never an automatic retry. Provider acceptance and authenticated delivery remain distinct. Activity and business export retain actual effects and provider references without credentials or bearers. DATA-001 erasure removes call records, replies, saved outcomes and personal timeline copies; a saved opaque callback identity prevents replay from reconstructing erased calls even when the number is reused. Restore retains permitted history but imports no provider key or booking credential, and reconnection changes the callback connection identity. This preparatory boundary does not replace the complete original target.

*Status:* partial — evidence `tests/api/reminders.test.mjs` › “COMM-009 signed known-customer calls route in order and retain outcomes; Owner explicitly texts one consented booking reply”, implemented in `tests/api/voice-flow.mjs`: ordered office and on-call routing and final forwarding, answered and missed call replay, exact caller and duration, off-by-default recording refusal, explicit booking text, separate consent, quiet hours, one attempt, authenticated delivery, role and branch refusals, ordered COMM-007 stream, Activity and business export with provider references and no credentials or bearer, and erasure without webhook resurrection. Gate correction evidence: the same HTTP flow asserts Owner retention and staff refusal of native call identities and call-to-offer associations through state and business export, including audit change metadata; signed inbound and completed-leg replays and a fresh next-leg callback hang up after the customer number changes, without writes. `tests/browser/reminders.spec.ts` › “COMM-009 native call history is Owner-only while the desk retains text history”, implemented in `tests/browser/voice-journey.ts`, asserts Owner call outcomes and Office exclusion at 1440, with screen proof at 1440 and 390. Gap: CT approval of the adopted routing, Owner-only call custody and explicit missed-call booking-text defaults remains pending. Recording consent, recording notice, provider recording custody and access, automatic missed-call texts, unknown and shared-number callers, inbound caller-disconnect outcomes, browser configuration and reply controls, complete archive restoration proof and COMM-002 number hosting and port activation remain unbuilt or unproved. The existing-number connection is an Owner affirmation; no live provider receipt is claimed.

### Field work on the phone

#### FIELD-001 — Offline day of jobs that survives a reload.
The technician's phone keeps the day's assigned visits (customer, site, access notes, equipment, history, checklists, forms, photos already taken) cached, so the app opens, reloads and works with no signal; everything captured offline sends exactly once on reconnect under `GLOVE-06`, and a conflict with a desk change is shown, never silently overwritten.

*Acceptance:* a browser journey loads the day, goes offline, reloads the app, completes a visit with photos and a form, reconnects, and checks everything landed once; a visit moved at the desk meanwhile shows the late-evidence review.

*Semantics (FIELD-001, 19 October 2026, data at rest as ct approved):* only a signed-in Technician's phone keeps the day: that technician's own view (the visits ever assigned to them with those jobs' customers, sites, access notes, equipment, history, checklists, forms and captures; never prices), replaced on every successful refresh, kept in this browser's storage, and cleared on sign-out and whenever a different person or a desk role signs in on it. A service worker keeps the app shell this phone loaded and that technician's photos, answering from them only when the network fails; it never keeps records or other `/api/` or `/auth/` answers, and desk roles never keep records. With no signal the app opens, reloads and works from the kept day, saying how old it is; everything recorded (arrival, checklist, forms, notes, photos, materials, completion) waits in the phone's outbox, in order, tied to the person who recorded it (another sign-in never sends or shows it), and sends exactly once on reconnect under `GLOVE-06`; a visit can be completed once its arrival is waiting to send. Evidence for a visit the desk moved to someone else meanwhile is kept, flagged and reviewed under `GLOVE-07`, never silently overwritten.

*Status:* done — evidence `src/ui/offline-day.ts`, `public/glove-sw.js`, `src/ui/api.ts`, `src/ui/outbox.ts`, `src/ui/technician.tsx`, `tests/browser/journey.spec.ts`.

#### FIELD-002 — Custom forms and inspections.
The owner builds forms (yes/no, choice, number with units, reading with range, photo, signature, notes, conditional questions) and attaches them to services, equipment types or agreements; technicians fill them offline; results appear on the job, the report and the equipment history.

*Acceptance:* a flow test builds an HVAC inspection form with a conditional question and a reading range, fills it on a visit with one out-of-range reading, and finds the flagged reading on the job and the equipment.

*Semantics (FIELD-002, 19 October 2026):* the owner builds a form of up to 60 questions (yes/no, choice, number with an optional unit, reading with a unit and an inclusive range, photo, signature, notes); a question can be asked only when an earlier yes/no or choice question has a given answer. A form attaches to price-book services and to equipment types (matched without regard to case). A new job takes a copy of each form that applies: one per unit the job names whose type the form lists, otherwise one for the job when a quoted service lists it; editing or archiving the form never changes a job's copy, and the per-service checklists stay as they are. The technician fills each copy once on the phone, through the outbox, so it works without signal (`GLOVE-06`): every question asked and required must be answered, a question not asked must be left empty, and a reading below its lowest or above its highest value is flagged. The filled form is a field record on the job and its timeline (naming how many readings are out of range), on the unit's service history when the copy is for a unit, and in the property history. Agreements carry forms when `PLAN-002` ships, and the customer's report prints form results under `FIELD-003`.

*Status:* done — evidence `src/glove/forms.ts`, `src/glove/commands/forms.ts`, `src/glove/commands/field.ts`, `src/ui/forms.tsx`, `tests/api/forms.test.mjs`, `tests/browser/journey.spec.ts`.

#### FIELD-003 — Shareable service report.
Completing a visit produces an immutable service report: work summary, findings, `FIELD-002` form results with out-of-range readings marked, photos, materials, customer signature (or the recorded reason it is absent), and recommendations. The owner, dispatcher or office creates a seven-day link with **Copy report link** and shares it with the customer by hand today; Glove makes no delivery claim. Recommendations become a quote draft in one step under ordinary human price approval. COMM-003/004 inherit customer notices, COMM-006 the hub, and QUOTE-005 branded PDF documents, in their text and acceptance before shipping.

*Semantics (20 October 2026, ct approved 23:58Z, pattern 1/3):* the exact customer-facing snapshot is frozen in the same guarded transaction as completion, with only the completing technician's first name. A link contains 32 random bytes, shown once, with only its SHA-256 retained separately from domain records; the bearer stays in the page fragment and request header, never a URL query, record, saved command result, export or log. The same desk roles revoke links. Each read rechecks expiry and revocation and permits only that report and the original files it named when frozen; other records and later evidence remain private. Link secrets are excluded from the installation archive, so a restored installation issues new links. Branch limits apply to creating/revoking a link and drafting a recommendation. A technician records findings and recommendations through the normal offline completion outbox; the owner or dispatcher prices a recommendation into an ordinary quote draft, linked to the source report and still requiring human price approval. Reports appear in Activity, property history and business export without link secrets. Existing completed or imported visits without a snapshot do not claim a report was issued.

*Acceptance:* `tests/api/service-reports.test.mjs` › `FIELD-003 / FIELD-002 / DATA-002 …` completes a visit, opens its customer report and every original with only the bearer, asserts all fields and marked readings, first-name-only technician, immutable contents, other-record/file refusal, one-step recommendation draft with normal price approval, expiry/revocation and live role/branch refusal, Activity/property history/export without secrets. `tests/browser/service-reports.spec.ts` completes from the technician screen, previews, copies, reads, drafts a recommendation and revokes at 1440/390. `tests/portable.mjs` restores exact snapshot/link metadata while refusing the old bearer and accepting a newly issued link. COMM-003/004 assert the actual report notice and delivery outcome, COMM-006 hub discovery, and QUOTE-005 branded PDF rendering before they ship.

*Status:* done — evidence `src/glove/service-reports.ts`, `src/glove/commands/service-reports.ts`, `src/glove/commands/field.ts`, `src/ui/service-report.tsx`, `src/ui/service-report-links.tsx`, `tests/api/service-reports.test.mjs`, `tests/browser/service-reports.spec.ts`.

#### FIELD-004 — Installable technician app with push.
The technician app installs to the home screen on iPhone and Android, uses the camera directly, sends push notifications for new, moved and canceled visits, and offers tap-to-call and tap-to-navigate.

*Acceptance:* a browser journey installs the app manifest, subscribes to push, and receives a notification (push service fake) when the dispatcher moves the visit; from the visit, tap-to-call opens a `tel:` link to the customer's number and tap-to-navigate a maps link to the site's address.

*Semantics (FIELD-004, 19 October 2026; privacy and security as ct approved 19 October 10:50Z):* `/app` installs to the home screen from its web app manifest (Glove, starting at `/app`, standalone, 192 and 512 px icons) and the home-screen tags iPhone reads; an iPhone shows web pushes only once Glove is added to its Home Screen (iOS 16.4 and later), which the phone screen says. Field photos open the rear camera directly. The owner turns phone notifications on under More → Team: Glove makes a VAPID key pair whose private key is an owner-only installation secret (never in records, state, exports, logs or the archive); turning them on again replaces the key, and turning them off deletes the key and every subscription. A Technician turns notifications on for each phone. A subscription (the push service address and the phone's keys) belongs to that person, that device and that signed-in session; only that person reads it back, as their list of phones (a label, the last outcome and when, never the address or keys); and it is deleted when they remove it, sign out on that phone, the session is ended elsewhere or expires, they stop being an active Technician (a role change or deactivation), the push service no longer knows it (HTTP 404 or 410), or the owner turns notifications off. It is never in records, exports or the archive, so a restored installation needs fresh subscriptions. After every committed change, whoever or whatever made it (a person, a rule, a recurring series, an agent under the owner's grant, a move-in), each technician with a visit that has not ended and was booked to them, moved (its start or end changed), canceled, or handed to another technician gets one push on each subscribed phone: "New visit on your schedule" (also for the technician who receives a handed-over visit), "Visit moved", "Visit canceled" or "Visit moved off your schedule", or "N changes to your schedule" when one change touches several of their visits. A push carries only that line and, when one visit changed, the job's opaque id; never the customer, site, time or a note. Each push is encrypted for the phone (RFC 8291, aes128gcm) and signed with the VAPID key (RFC 8292: a 12-hour token naming the business email, else the installation address), kept by the push service for at most a day, and sent once, never retried. The phone list says "Handed to the push service" (not "delivered", which Glove cannot see), "Not sent: the push service answered HTTP <status>" or "Not sent: the push service could not be reached". Only a Technician subscribes, and no role, agent included, has a door that sends a push. Tapping a notification opens the signed-in app on that job's visit. On a visit, Call customer opens a `tel:` link to the customer's phone (its digits and a leading +) and Navigate to site opens Google Maps directions to the site's address (the Maps app where installed). A local installation served from this computer also accepts a push service on this computer (`http://127.0.0.1` or `localhost`), so its own browser journey can run one; a hosted installation accepts only https push services. `FIELD-005`'s native apps carry push in their own text.

*Status:* done — evidence `src/glove/push.ts`, `src/glove/commands/push.ts`, `src/ui/push.tsx`, `public/manifest.webmanifest`, `public/glove-sw.js`, `tests/api/push.test.mjs`, `tests/browser/journey.spec.ts`.

#### FIELD-005 — Native iOS and Android apps.
Glove ships technician apps in the App Store and Google Play, signed in to the owner's installation, with the offline cache of `FIELD-001`, background photo upload, push, location under `DISP-005` and Tap to Pay under `PAY-003`.

*Acceptance:* a device-farm journey on a current iPhone and Android phone completes a visit offline, reconnects and takes a Tap to Pay test payment (Stripe test mode).

*Status:* missing — gap: no native apps.

#### FIELD-006 — Equipment nameplate capture.
The technician photographs an equipment nameplate; Glove reads make, model, serial and manufacture date into the equipment record for the technician to confirm, and attaches the manufacturer's manual where one is available.

*Acceptance:* a flow test submits three fixture nameplate photos (model provider fake), checks the proposed fields, and checks nothing is saved until the technician confirms.

*Semantics (FIELD-006, 19 October 2026):* the model provider is the owner's own: an OpenAI-compatible chat-completions address (https), one model id and the owner's key, connected by the owner only and kept like the marketing keys (never in records, exports, logs or the archive); without it nothing is read. The technician reads a nameplate on a visit they are working, for a unit already at the site or one not recorded yet; only the photo goes to the provider (no customer, site or job details), asking for JSON with storage off. The reading is a proposal on the visit, labelled as read by the model: no equipment record changes until the technician who took it confirms the fields, correcting any first (the reading keeps the proposal and the corrected fields), or discards it. A manufacture date is kept as printed to its precision (YYYY, YYYY-MM or YYYY-MM-DD); anything else is left unread, as is any field the model returns empty. "Where one is available" means the manual library the desk keeps (make, model, title, https address): on confirmation the manual whose make and model match, ignoring case and surrounding spaces, is attached; Glove never invents, searches for or fetches a manual. Reading needs a signal; the confirmed unit and its manual are then in the phone's offline day (`FIELD-001`). The provider flow and exactly these fields (and `AGENT-006`'s) are listed in the declared data flows, the docs page `docs/data-flows.md`.

*Status:* done — evidence `src/glove/commands/nameplates.ts`, `src/glove/provider.ts`, `src/ui/nameplates.tsx`, `tests/api/nameplates.test.mjs`.

#### FIELD-007 — Refrigerant tracking for HVAC.
On HVAC visits the technician records refrigerant type and the amounts added and recovered per equipment item, with their EPA Section 608 certification number; the owner gets a per-equipment and per-period refrigerant log for their own recordkeeping.

*Acceptance:* a flow test records refrigerant added and recovered on two visits to one condenser and reads the equipment log and the period total.

*Status:* done — evidence `src/glove/commands/field.ts`, `src/glove/equipment.ts`, `src/ui/equipment.tsx`, `tests/api/equipment.test.mjs`.

#### FIELD-008 — The whole office on the owner's phone.
Every desk role does its daily work from a phone, 390 px wide and up, in the installable app (`FIELD-004`) and the native apps (`FIELD-005`), under the same role checks as the desk: create a request, a customer or a job (`BOOK-005`), build, approve and send a quote, book or move a visit with conflict review, prepare, approve and send an invoice, take a payment (key in a card read out on the phone or charge a saved one under `PAY-009`, or record a check or cash), answer a text in the inbox (`COMM-005`) and read the `REPORT-001` dashboard. No screen scrolls sideways, and every action is the same public command as at the desk.

*Acceptance:* a browser journey at 390 px signed in as Owner creates a job from a phoned-in request, sends a quote, books the visit, sends the invoice, records a check payment, charges a saved card on a second invoice (Stripe fake), answers a customer text and opens the dashboard, asserting no horizontal scroll on any screen; the same steps signed in as Office are refused exactly where the role table says.

*Adopted phone desk boundary (26 October 2026; pending ct):* the existing desk commands run at both widths. The signed-in unscoped owner records an existing Stripe test method only after Stripe verifies its attachment to the mapped customer and the owner records source, wording and authorization scope. Each Owner or Office charge explicitly confirms the displayed method and amount, reserves before a single provider attempt and counts funds only after the authenticated Stripe event. Unknown attempts block another charge. Registration and withdrawal are audited. The shared inbox retains consented incoming text only after the owner opts in, exposes bodies to native desk people only, and sends each reply only on their explicit action under current consent and quiet hours. Customer erasure removes message records. PAY-008 inherits method collection and autopay; PAY-009 retains keyed cards, other charge subjects and custom roles; COMM-005 retains technician texting and moved-visit threading; FIELD-005 retains native delivery.

*Status:* partial — evidence `tests/browser/journey.spec.ts` › “The Owner runs the whole phone desk and Office repeats its permitted steps with exact role refusals at 390 and 1440” (phoned request to quote and job, provider-accepted quote and invoice email, booking, check payment, saved-card charge settled by a signed Stripe event, customer text reply and dashboard; every screen checks horizontal scroll; Office service-call creation, allowed sends and payments, and quote approval, customer acceptance and ordinary booking refusals); `tests/api/payments.test.mjs` › “FIELD-008 saved test cards require customer authorization and desk authority, reserve once, and pay only on authenticated Stripe confirmation” (verified attachment, scope and role refusals, exact-once reservation, unknown outcome, signed settlement, decline, withdrawal and Activity with business export); `tests/api/reminders.test.mjs` › “The desk explicitly retains, assigns and answers a customer text on its job; STOP and role withdrawal close the send door” (inbox assignment, same-customer threading, provider delivery, conversation history, STOP and role withdrawal). Gap: FIELD-005 native apps still require the owner’s Apple Developer and Google Play publisher and signing access and device proof. Keyed-card entry, discretionary deposit charges, saved bank methods and custom-role charges remain under PAY-009 and PAY-008. Adopted saved-card authorization, desk inbox retention and reply policy await ct approval; FIELD-008 stays partial.

#### COST-001 — Clock-ins and job timers.
Technicians clock in and out for the day and run travel and on-job timers per visit, with or without signal; time is attached to the job; the desk sees who is on the clock (the `REPORT-001` dashboard's on-the-clock tile) and corrects time with a reason.

*Acceptance:* a browser journey clocks in, starts travel and job timers on two visits with one offline stretch, clocks out, and checks the time on each job; a desk correction keeps the original; the `REPORT-001` on-the-clock tile lists exactly the technicians clocked in at each step.

*Semantics (COST-001, 19 October 2026):* a technician clocks in and out on the phone and, only while clocked in, runs one timer at a time, travel or on the job, on a visit currently assigned to them; starting a timer stops the running one at the same phone time, and clocking out or completing the visit stops it too. Every start and stop carries the phone's clock time and goes through the phone's outbox with the identity it was given, so it works without signal and a resend lands once (`GLOVE-06`). An entry counts end − start in whole minutes, rounded half up; a job's time is the sum of its entries per kind. The desk (owner, dispatcher, office) corrects a stopped entry's start and end, as local dates and times in the business timezone, with a reason; the entry keeps the phone's original times and every correction (who, when, reason, from and to), and the job's timeline records it. Technicians read only their own entries. The `REPORT-001` on-the-clock tile lists the technicians with a shift clocked in and not out, with the timer each is running.

*Status:* done — evidence `tests/api/payroll.test.mjs`, `src/glove/commands/time.ts`, `src/ui/clock.tsx`, `src/glove/dashboard.ts`, `src/ui/today.tsx`, `tests/browser/journey.spec.ts`, `tests/api/security.test.mjs`, `src/glove/view.ts`, `src/ui/technician.tsx`.

#### COST-002 — Timesheets and payroll export.
Weekly timesheets per person roll up clock and job time, flag overtime past 40 hours a week (and daily overtime where the owner sets a state rule), are approved by the owner, and export in the import shape of Gusto, ADP RUN and QuickBooks Payroll; W-2 employees and 1099 subcontractors are kept apart, with subcontractor payment totals for 1099-NEC.

*Acceptance:* a flow test builds a 44-hour week, approves it, and checks the overtime split and the Gusto and QuickBooks Payroll exports; a subcontractor's total appears only in the 1099 export.

*Semantics (COST-002, 20 October 2026):* a workweek is seven local days in the business time zone from the owner's week-start day (Monday unless the owner sets another; fixed once any timesheet is approved). Each closed shift counts its whole minutes (`COST-001` rounding) on the local day it started, including a shift that runs past midnight; job and travel timer minutes are shown beside it, and paid hours are the shift (clock) time. When the owner sets a state rule, minutes past the "after" hours in a day are overtime and minutes past the optional "double after" hours are double time (quarter-hour settings, double after overtime); then, day by day in order, regular minutes beyond 40 hours in the workweek become overtime. Seventh-day and other state rules are not applied. Hours are each bucket's minutes / 60, rounded half up to two decimals, so rounded buckets can differ from the rounded total by 0.01. A timesheet is flagged when it holds any overtime or double time. Whoever reads time entries reads the week's timesheets (a technician only their own; a subcontractor none). Only the owner (acting for "admin") approves a week, and only once no shift in it is still open; an approval stands while the week's entries and their split are unchanged. A later correction, late entry or overtime-rule change marks it "changed since approval" until the owner approves it again. The payroll files are for a pay period of 1 to 6 whole workweeks, read by the owner and office like the other exports. They hold only people whose every week with time in the period is approved and unchanged, and they list everyone left out with the reason. A technician record marked as a 1099 subcontractor (`DISP-007`) is never in the payroll files or approvable; their agreed-amount totals are only in the 1099-NEC export. The files follow what each provider publishes, checked against the provider's own help on 21 October 2026. RUN Powered by ADP: ADP's own "Time Sheet Import and RUN Powered by ADP" guide (GTSG V0418A) defines the layout, and the file follows it exactly: a `##GENERIC## V1.0` version row, the column titles IID, Pay Frequency, Pay Period Start Date, Pay Period End Date, Employee ID, Earnings Code, Pay Hours, Dollars, Separate Check, Worked In Dept and Rate Code, then one row per person and earnings code with the owner's company code (up to 10 letters and digits), pay frequency, the period's start and end as MM/DD/YYYY, the hours to two decimals, Separate Check `0` (the regular check) and Rate Code `BASE` (the first pay rate on the employee), with the owner's earnings codes, by default REG, OTP and DBL. Gusto: Gusto publishes no fixed employee hours layout outside an account (its template is downloaded inside Gusto, and its Smart Import matches columns on upload); Glove's column names (last name, first name, Gusto employee ID, regular, overtime and double-overtime hours; the first word of the technician's name is the first name) are therefore not confirmed against a Gusto-published layout. The one column Gusto's help names, `workweeks`, is followed: one row per person and approved workweek, holding that week's date range as `YYYY-MM-DD - YYYY-MM-DD` beside that week's hours, so Gusto places overtime in the right workweek. Glove keeps no SSN and writes no SSN column. QuickBooks Payroll: Intuit publishes no fixed timesheet columns, only that the custom upload takes one entry per line with the employee and a date plus units, the date format and each column's meaning chosen on upload; Glove's date-and-units file (employee display name, MM/DD/YYYY date, pay item Regular, Overtime or Double time, hours) is a file the owner maps on upload, not a confirmed Intuit layout. The employee ID is the payroll ID the owner keeps on each technician record. None of these files has yet been uploaded to a live Gusto, ADP RUN or QuickBooks account; Glove pays no one and files nothing. Inheritance: `TEAM-003` commissions and spiffs and `PAY-002` technician tips add their amounts to the same payroll files (Gusto's commission and paycheck-tips columns, an ADP RUN earnings code with dollars) for the same approved weeks before either ships.

*Status:* done — evidence `tests/browser/journey.spec.ts`, `src/glove/payroll.ts`, `src/ui/timesheets.tsx`, `tests/api/payroll.test.mjs`, `src/glove/api.ts`, `tests/api/security.test.mjs`, `src/glove/view.ts`.

#### COST-003 — Job expenses and receipts.
Technicians and the office record expenses on a job (supply-house purchases, permits, equipment rental, subcontractor bills) with a receipt photo, vendor, amount and whether it is billable; billable expenses can be added to the invoice at cost or with markup.

*Acceptance:* a flow test records a supply-house receipt as billable with 20% markup and a permit fee as non-billable, and checks the invoice draft and job cost.

*Status:* done — evidence `src/glove/commands/expenses.ts`, `src/glove/commands/invoices.ts`, `src/ui/expenses.tsx`, `tests/api/expenses.test.mjs`.

#### COST-004 — Estimate versus actual job costing.
Every job shows its quoted price and estimated cost against actual labor (time × each person's loaded cost rate), materials at cost, expenses and subcontractors, giving actual margin and the difference from estimate; reports roll this up by job type, technician and period.

*Acceptance:* a flow test runs a job quoted at $1,850 with estimated 41% margin through recorded time, materials and an expense, and checks actual cost and a 46% margin to the cent.

*Semantics (COST-004, 19 October 2026):* revenue is the latest person-approved invoice revision's subtotal before tax, else the accepted quote's subtotal. The estimated cost is each accepted line's unit cost (kept when the line was priced from the price book, `MONEY-003`) × its quantity; a line without one counts at $0.00 and is named. Actual labor is every travel and on-job entry (`COST-001`) at its whole minutes × that technician's loaded cost per hour ÷ 60; materials are the price-book materials recorded on the job at the item's unit cost now, beyond what the job's own purchase orders received (`STOCK-002`), whose received lines count at the order's unit cost; expenses and subcontractors are `COST-003`'s amounts, subcontractor bills apart. Each product is rounded half up to the cent; a margin is (revenue − cost) ÷ revenue as a percentage to one decimal, rounded half up, and the difference from estimate is in dollars and margin points. The owner sets each technician's loaded cost per hour; like every cost it is a margin figure, read only by roles that read reports (`MONEY-003`). Reports take the jobs completed in a period (by local completion date, bounds inclusive) and roll them up by job type (the category of the job's first price-book line, else Uncategorized) and by technician (their minutes, labor cost and the jobs they recorded time on).

*Status:* done — evidence `tests/api/costing.test.mjs` › COST-004 a $1,850 job estimated at 41% margin comes in at 46% through time, materials, an expense and a subcontractor (cent-exact estimate/actual, reports and named uncosted lines); `tests/browser/journey.spec.ts` › COST-004 uncosted estimate lines are named on the job at 1440 and 390 (each missing-cost line and zero-cost notice).

#### COST-005 — Live margin warnings.
When actual cost on an open job crosses an owner-set threshold of its estimate, or projected margin falls below a floor, Glove warns the dispatcher and the owner while the job is still open.

*Acceptance:* a flow test records time past the threshold and checks one warning to each of the two people, and no repeat for the same crossing.

*Semantics (COST-005, 19 October 2026):* the owner sets, on the business, a cost threshold (warn when actual cost reaches this percentage of the estimate) and a margin floor (warn when projected margin falls below this percentage); either can be blank, meaning off. After every command, each open job is checked with `COST-004`'s figures; its projected cost is the larger of actual and estimated cost, and a job with no estimated cost never crosses the threshold. A crossing is one warning shown in Glove to the owner and to every dispatcher, on their desk screens and on the job's timeline; nothing is sent (no email or text). A warning is kept once per job, kind and setting, so the same crossing never repeats; changing a setting can warn again. Like every cost, warnings are read only by the owner and dispatchers they are addressed to, and their timeline entries only by roles that read reports.

*Status:* done — evidence `src/glove/margins.ts`, `src/glove/api.ts`, `src/glove/view.ts`, `src/ui/costing.tsx`, `src/ui/team.tsx`, `tests/api/margins.test.mjs`.

### Invoicing and payments

#### PAY-001 — USD invoices sent from Glove.
An approved invoice is sent by email and text with a pay link, or presented on the technician's phone on site; a technician the owner allows prepares the invoice on the phone, offline if needed, and it sends exactly once on reconnect; invoices support net terms and due dates, progress invoicing on long jobs, batch invoicing of completed jobs, and statements per customer; completed jobs not yet invoiced are listed and the office is reminded daily; `GLOVE-09`'s revision and correction trail applies to every one; an invoice carries the customer's PO number (approval is refused when the customer requires one and it is missing) and goes to the customer's billing contact and accounts-payable address.

*Acceptance:* a flow test approves and sends an invoice (provider fakes), batch-invoices three completed jobs, and opens a customer statement with the right balances; a commercial customer that requires a PO refuses invoice approval without one, and its invoice goes to the accounts-payable address with the PO printed.

*Semantics (PAY-001 batch preparation, 21 October 2026):* the existing invoice-preparation permission also prepares selected completed jobs together (`invoice.prepareBatch`, 1–50 distinct job IDs). Each job uses the same accepted lines, priced materials, billable expenses, discount and tax rules as a single preparation and produces a draft requiring its own existing approval. Results stay in selection order and name each prepared invoice or refusal; a refused item consumes no invoice number or expense and does not prevent the other items. Branch limits apply to each job; an out-of-branch job returns no record details. Replaying the same command returns the same result; a fresh request for an already invoiced job refuses it rather than duplicating it. Activity and business export retain the individual preparations and the batch counts. The Invoices screen offers selection, each result and links to review drafts.

*Semantics (26 October 2026, statement and collection groundwork):* the desk's existing report authority opens a customer's dated statement as JSON or downloads it as CSV from Customers. Statements include only that customer's approved revisions and retained opening balances through the chosen date, subtract confirmed payment effects through each record's branch-local date, and keep currencies separate. Unapproved corrections and pending, failed or returned payments do not reduce a balance; tips and fees are excluded. Credits stay separate until explicitly allocated. Dates remain frozen under GLOVE-09 and PLAN-005. Branch scope, customer erasure and existing report permissions apply at the HTTP boundary. Statements issue no payment credential and send no message. A scheduled internal Activity reminder records completed work still awaiting invoice preparation or approval once per job and branch-local date, on the first pass at or after 08:00; approval and erasure are rechecked atomically. This reminder sends no customer or office email.

*Semantics (26 October 2026, progress invoices):* existing invoice preparers explicitly name each stage and enter its priced lines for an accepted quoted job. Every stage is a distinct draft under the existing exact-revision human approval, PO, terms, tax and correction controls. Earlier stages require approval before the next stage; names are unique within the job. The first stage freezes the accepted gross contract cap. Drafts and corrections reserve their current gross amounts within that cap, and a final stage requires a completed job and exactly the remaining amount including tax. Only a final stage export finishes invoicing the completed job. No percentage, material, expense, discount allocation or charge is inferred; the preparer reconciles the explicit stage lines, including agreed discounts, against the accepted scope. Job-held deposits attach only to the final stage. Changed scope and automatic allocations remain separate unfinished work.

*Adopted field invoice default (26 October 2026; pending ct):* a signed-in Owner without branch limits explicitly allows or withdraws invoice preparation and approved invoice presentation for one active technician linked to their current sign-in. This separate permission is off by default and grants no field price-book authority, approval, sending, payment, custom-role or integration-token authority. A technician requests a draft only for their currently assigned, uncancelled visit on a completed job. The phone's existing ordered outbox keeps the visit, capture time, actor and command identity through offline reload and a lost response; reconnection prepares one office-review draft without an external message. Current authority and assignment are rechecked on execution and replay. An unapproved draft reveals no prices to the technician.

With that permission, the technician state and kept offline day include only the customer-facing fields of an exact approved invoice revision on their current assigned job: document identity, lines, approved totals, PO, issue and due dates and approval basis. Costs, credentials, private accounting details and payment balances stay excluded. An unapproved replacement does not replace the last approved revision. The phone labels the kept time and inability to discover later changes while disconnected; reconnection hides a kept presentation until a fresh authority and approval read. Sign-out, changed identity, withdrawal on fresh read and DATA-001's received erasure fences clear retained access through the existing offline custody. An offline destination cannot discover a later withdrawal or erasure without reconnecting to trusted current state. Restore supplies no session or permission to a different identity. CT approval of this added field permission and offline invoice custody remains a Gap; this preparatory policy establishes no new customer authentication, sending or collection authority.

PLAN-005 fixed-monthly invoices inherit its ct-approved period-end issue date, separate approval time, later-of-period/approval due-date basis, approval-gated aging and frozen report/export dates; preexisting documents keep their original basis. This is proven with PLAN-005’s named monthly-date flow before that capability ships.

*Status:* partial — evidence `src/glove/commands/invoices.ts`, `src/glove/statements.ts`, `src/glove/unbilled-reminders.ts`, `src/ui/customers.tsx`, `tests/api/flows.test.mjs` — “PAY-001 customer statements retain approved dated balances, confirmed payments, credits and corrections without cross-customer or branch disclosure”; `tests/browser/invoice-batch.spec.ts` — “PAY-001 the office prepares three selected completed jobs together, sees a refused item and reviews each draft and downloads a customer statement at 1440 and 390”; existing batch and approved email evidence remains in `tests/api/flows.test.mjs` and `tests/api/email.test.mjs`. `tests/api/flows.test.mjs` › “PAY-001 named progress drafts preserve the contract cap, exact final remainder, tax, approval and correction trail” proves named stages, replay, over-cap and premature-stage refusal, PO, frozen terms, tax cents, final residual, export, immutable correction history, customer statement totals and branch isolation; › “PAY-001 owner-granted technician draft requests replay once and expose only approved invoices under current assignment and permission” proves separate explicit Owner permission, role and assignment refusal, concurrent replay once, no draft prices, office-only approval, exact prior approved revision, withdrawal including replay, customer erasure and refused access to erased visits, Activity and export without automatic sending. `tests/browser/invoice-batch.spec.ts` › “PAY-001 office stages a long job, sees the remaining contract amount and approves the exact final invoice at 1440 and 390” proves stage controls, refused overbilling, remaining amount, final review and approved stage history; › “PAY-001 owner allows a technician to queue one invoice draft offline and present its approved revision after reconnect and reload at 1440 and 390” proves the owner permission screen, offline queue and reload, lost response after server commit with exactly-once replay, office approval, approved presentation online and offline, kept-time warning and withdrawal on reconnect. Gap: Text and customer pay links remain under the ct-held PAY-002 customer-side ruling. External invoice delivery on reconnect remains unbuilt; the preparatory reconnect action creates a draft only. CT approval of the separate field permission and offline approved-invoice custody remains pending. Current progress invoices use explicitly priced stages within the frozen accepted gross contract amount; changed scope requires reviewed reconciliation before further stages. Approved email sends and accounts-payable delivery use COMM-001.

#### PAY-002 — Card, ACH and wallet payments through Stripe.
The owner connects their own Stripe account; customers pay invoices, deposits and agreements by card, Apple Pay, Google Pay or ACH bank debit; Glove records each payment only when Stripe confirms it (ACH as pending until settled), shows fees, and never touches raw card numbers. Card surcharging is off by default and, if the owner turns it on, carries the disclosure they configure. A customer can add a tip when paying online or on the `PAY-003` screen (owner-set choices); each tip is recorded against the payment and the visit's technicians, kept out of revenue and sales tax, and carried to the payroll export (`COST-002`, `TEAM-003`). The `REPORT-001` dashboard's "Paid today" tile sums the payments Stripe confirmed on the business's local today, tips apart; an ACH payment counts on the day it settles. The `REPORT-002` sales-tax report then counts tax collected from payments (until then its basis is tax on approved invoices, labelled so).

DATA-002 inheritance: before this capability ships, every new command, communication, payment or sync action and provider outcome keeps its actor/provider, time, references and actual effect in Activity and the business export; credentials and secret payloads never enter the trail.

*Acceptance:* a flow test pays one invoice by card and one by ACH (Stripe fake with webhooks), checks the card payment is paid at once, the ACH payment pending until the settlement event, and a failed ACH return reopens the balance; a card payment with a $15.00 tip records the tip for the visit's technician, untaxed, and the tip appears in the payroll export; the `REPORT-001` "Paid today" tile counts the card payment at once and the ACH payment only after its settlement event; the `REPORT-002` sales-tax report counts the tax on the two paid invoices as collected. COMM-001: assert each emailed receipt goes through the owner's provider (fake) with its provider outcome on the message, payment's timeline, Activity and export without secrets, and that any email not strictly transactional carries the CAN-SPAM unsubscribe link and postal address.

The flow also asserts each new DATA-002 event in Activity and the business export, including provider outcomes and references without secrets.

REPORT-003 inheritance: confirmed payments update the aging report's open balances before this capability ships; assert the exact bucket/customer totals and invoice links in its flow.

COMM-001 inheritance: before this capability ships, every receipt it emails goes through `COMM-001`'s door with the same provider outcomes: one send attempt per message, recorded with the provider's answer and reference on the message, the payment's timeline, Activity and the business export without secrets; state changes only from authenticated provider callbacks; delivery is never claimed without the provider's event. Any email that is not strictly transactional also carries the CAN-SPAM unsubscribe link and postal address.

*Semantics (PAY-002 partial slice, 23 October 2026):* the signed-in unscoped owner connects Stripe test mode with `payment.connect`, which requires Stripe’s balance API to confirm the key is not live; `payment.checkout` reserves an approved USD invoice payment before one fixed-endpoint Stripe Checkout attempt. Card-hosted entry and ACH never pass account or card numbers to Glove. A returned hosted URL is bearer material, shown only in the first response, excluded from records, saved outcomes and archives; a replay recovers the reservation without another attempt. The public `/payment-return` acknowledgement never treats a redirect or its query parameters as payment confirmation. Restored installations must reconnect their excluded private keys before another reservation is allowed. Checkout facts expose only an irreversible reference hash; the provider session reference stays in the private archive-excluded attempt store. Unresolved requests block a second request for that invoice. `payment.receipt` is an explicit Owner/Office transactional email to the on-record accounts-payable or customer address through COMM-001. Webhooks verify the raw-body Stripe signature and five-minute freshness before parsing; current intent retrieval verifies the connection, currency, gross amount, payment identity and test mode. Confirmed effects reduce aging and feed paid-today and collected-tax totals, with tips apart; a failed charge or ACH return reverses them. Each tip splits equally in exact cents among the completed visits’ distinct technicians (ID order receives remainder cents), and joins approved payroll weeks only. Test mode is named in payment facts, receipts, aging and collected-tax basis; these are not live funds. Provider outcomes and receipts read on the payment timeline, Activity, conversation stream and business export. Property history includes each payment and its current provider state. Duplicate-customer merge and undo carry payment ownership with the invoice. After erasure, anonymous financial effects remain and provider/session references are removed. The owner sets 1–20 distinct fixed-dollar tip choices, including zero, with `payment.preferences`; new connections default to zero only. Preference edits affect future requests and keep existing reservations unchanged. A rejected receipt leaves the payment confirmed; replay never sends again, and the office can explicitly send a new receipt after correction. These conservative custody/authority defaults await ct confirmation and do not complete the clause.

*Status:* partial — evidence `tests/api/payments.test.mjs` › Stripe-hosted card and ACH settle once (settlement/return, fees, tips/payroll, dashboard, collected tax, aging, receipt/provider timeline, Activity/export); rejects invalid callbacks and unauthorized requests (no effects, reconnect fence, uncertain Checkout replay); payment custody follows customer merge and undo and erasure (financial totals retained, provider/session references removed); `tests/browser/receivables.spec.ts` › confirmed payment and ACH return (Today, aging, tax, invoice and property-history screens at 1440/390); gap: test-mode invoice Checkout only; customer-facing payment controls and customer-selected tips, wallet proof, deposits and agreement payments, surcharge/disclosure, uncertain Checkout reconciliation, archive/restore proof remain. Adopted default pending ct: signed-in owner-only show-once hosted Checkout links, no automatic charge or receipt, and keys excluded from archives; live activation is disabled.

#### PAY-003 — Tap to Pay and card readers on site.
Technicians take card-present payments on the phone (Tap to Pay on iPhone and Android through Stripe Terminal) or a paired Stripe reader, with the tip choices of `PAY-002`, the receipt texted or emailed and the payment on the job before they leave.

*Acceptance:* the `FIELD-005` device journey takes a Tap to Pay test payment on a $412.00 invoice with an 18% tip chosen on the phone's tip screen and the receipt sent by text, then a second test payment with no tip and the receipt by email. It finds both payments on the job, the $74.16 tip recorded for the visit's technician outside revenue and sales tax, and each receipt on the timeline with the channel the customer chose and the provider's delivery outcome.

*Status:* missing — gap: no payments or native app.

#### PAY-004 — Partial payments, balances, refunds and credits.
An invoice accepts several payments (deposit, progress, final, check and cash recorded by the office, cards the office keys in or charges on file under `PAY-009`); balances, overpayments and credits carry on the customer; the owner refunds all or part through the original method, and every refund is its own record linked to the payment. The `REPORT-001` dashboard's receivables tile is the sum of every invoice's unpaid balance.

DATA-002 inheritance: before this capability ships, every new command, communication, payment or sync action and provider outcome keeps its actor/provider, time, references and actual effect in Activity and the business export; credentials and secret payloads never enter the trail.

*Acceptance:* a flow test pays an invoice in three parts (one by check), refunds $50 to the card (Stripe fake), and checks the invoice, customer balance and refund record, and that the `REPORT-001` receivables tile equals the open balances after each step. COMM-001: assert each emailed receipt goes through the owner's provider (fake) with its provider outcome on the message, payment's timeline, Activity and export without secrets, and that any email not strictly transactional carries the CAN-SPAM unsubscribe link and postal address.

The flow also asserts each new DATA-002 event in Activity and the business export, including provider outcomes and references without secrets.

AGENT-008 inheritance: before this capability ships, the morning brief lists overdue invoices (due date passed, balance unpaid) with their invoice link and a proposed action, asserted in the AGENT-008 flow.

REPORT-003 inheritance: allocations, credits, overpayments and refunds update the aging report's open balances before this capability ships; assert the exact bucket/customer totals and invoice links in its flow.

COMM-001 inheritance: before this capability ships, every receipt it emails goes through `COMM-001`'s door with the same provider outcomes: one send attempt per message, recorded with the provider's answer and reference on the message, the payment's timeline, Activity and the business export without secrets; state changes only from authenticated provider callbacks; delivery is never claimed without the provider's event. Any email that is not strictly transactional also carries the CAN-SPAM unsubscribe link and postal address.

*Semantics (23 October 2026):* `payment.checkout` accepts an optional positive installment amount up to the invoice’s unpaid balance; the owner-only, show-once test Checkout boundary of PAY-002 remains. The signed-in office or owner records cash/check receipts (`payment.record`) with a reference; these are staff records, never provider-confirmed claims. Overpayments remain customer credit on their invoice. `payment.allocate` explicitly moves available credit to an unpaid invoice of the same customer, currency and branch; it never transfers money. The owner records a reasoned non-cash credit (`payment.credit`). Only the signed-in owner refunds (`payment.refund`), at most the original confirmed amount less earlier confirmed or unresolved refunds, using the original method. Refund `amount` is invoice principal and optional `tip` is separately bounded by the remaining original tip; a tip-only refund changes tips/payroll, never invoice balance or tax. A subsequent return reverses only the remaining principal/tip, not a second copy of a prior refund. Cash/check refunds record money already returned; Stripe refunds reserve durably before one provider attempt with an idempotency key. Each refund has its own payment-linked record; pending/unknown refunds have no balance effect and are never automatically resent. A pending provider reference can be read again (`payment.refundRefresh`); only provider-confirmed success reopens a balance. Invoice/customer balances (`GET /api/glove/balances`), aging, Today’s receivables and the owner’s linked overdue brief use the same cent ledger. Stripe test revenue/tips remain separate from office cash/check records. Transactional receipts for payments and refunds use COMM-001 with actual delivery facts. QUOTE-003 inherits job-held deposits and final-invoice allocation; PAY-009 inherits keyed/saved-method entry without bypassing these allocation/refund rules.

*Status:* done — evidence `tests/api/payments.test.mjs` › “PAY-004 three installments, original-card refund, cash/check overpayment and customer credit keep invoice, aging, brief and receipts exact” (three installments including check, $50 original-card refund, invoice/customer/dashboard/aging totals after each step, overdue action link, credit transfer, receipt provider outcomes, Activity/export); › “PAY-004 refunds reserve uncertain funds once, refuse excess and stale writes, and confirm pending original-method outcomes explicitly” (uncertainty, replay, limits, pending refresh, cash refund and authority); `tests/browser/receivables.spec.ts` › “PAY-004 office payment and original-method refund controls, customer credit, receivables and overdue brief at 1440 and 390” (invoice cash/check/refund/credit controls, aging and Today/brief screens and action links, no sideways scroll or icon-only controls). Job-held deposits and their final invoice allocation, including a refund before invoice preparation, are proven by QUOTE-003’s two named HTTP flows; keyed card entry/saved methods remain PAY-009’s entry obligations.

#### PAY-005 — Payment reminders that stop at zero.
The owner sets a reminder schedule (before due, on due, after due); Glove sends it by text and email with the pay link, stops the moment the balance is zero or the invoice is disputed, and shows each invoice's reminder state.

*Acceptance:* a flow test advances a clock through the schedule for two invoices, pays one after the first reminder, and checks it received nothing more.

REPORT-003 inheritance: each invoice in the aging report displays its actual reminder state before this capability ships, asserted in the reminder flow.

*Status:* partial — evidence `tests/api/reminders.test.mjs` › “PAY-005 due-date reminders stop after payment or dispute and aging exposes their actual state” (schedule, zero-balance stop, dispute stop, no Checkout links, aging state and Activity); `tests/browser/reminders.spec.ts` › “PAY-005 and QUOTE-004 show automatic email progress, provider acceptance and stopped states at 1440 and 390” (invoice and aging screens). Gap: customer pay links await the customer-side Checkout ruling under PAY-002; existing Checkout stays owner-only and shown once, and reminder emails contain no link. SMS under COMM-002, owner schedule and dispute controls in Settings and invoices, live provider proof under COMM-001, and ct approval of the shared consent and quiet-hours default remain.

#### PAY-006 — Consumer financing through Wisetack.
On quotes above an owner-set amount, the customer can apply for financing through Wisetack from the quote or hub; Glove shows the provider's decision and offer, and records the payout when the provider funds; Glove never underwrites or lends.

*Acceptance:* a flow test applies for financing on a $9,800 quote (provider fake), approves, funds, and checks the invoice is paid by the financing payout.

*Semantics (23 October 2026 conservative default):* the signed-in unscoped owner configures only Wisetack's fixed test endpoint (`financing.connect`), an owner-only private auth token, merchant reference and minimum amount. For a current price-approved owner-issued quote link, the customer chooses the exact work, enters an expected completion date and explicitly consents before requesting an application. Glove sends only the chosen USD amount, completion date, generic service purpose and opaque purchase reference to Wisetack; no name, contact, address, SSN, income or other credit data is forwarded. The provider-hosted application link is returned once, never retained in records, saved outcomes, logs, exports or archives; personal and credit information is entered only on Wisetack. A durable reservation precedes the one provider request; an unknown outcome is never automatically resent. The owner explicitly refreshes from Wisetack's authenticated transaction read door; the quote shows only its own provider status and reported APR/month ranges, labelled provider facts with final terms on Wisetack. Approval is not money. Funding is allocated only when the full provider-reported amount matches the winning signed chosen-document hash and exact job; it is held on that job until its invoice is prepared. Duplicate reads create no duplicate payment. Wisetack's fee stays separate; original-method refunds use PAY-004's reservation and separate linked refund record. Activity, property history and export keep only whitelisted outcomes and references; DATA-001 erases the application and retains anonymous financial totals. Glove never underwrites or lends. COMM-006 inherits the same isolation, explicit consent, minimal data and provider-outcome rules before financing appears in the hub. Production activation remains disabled; a live merchant account is needed only to activate a future live connection.

*Status:* partial — evidence `tests/api/customer-quotes.test.mjs` › “PAY-006 customer explicitly applies for a $9,800 chosen quote; Wisetack alone approves and funds its exact signed job and original-method refunds remain separate” (explicit consent and minimal outbound fields, one-time link/replay, provider decision/offer, refusal before exact signing, $9,800 funded invoice, separate original-method refund, Activity/export, property history and erasure); gap: ct approval of the adopted customer consent/authority default (owner-issued current quote link; explicit consent; amount/date/opaque reference only, no customer credit data) only. `tests/browser/customer-quotes.spec.ts` › “PAY-006 explicit customer financing consent and provider offers/funding appear on the quote and exact invoice at 1440 and 390” proves connection/auth-input custody, consent/application, provider offers and the funded invoice at both widths. The live merchant account gates activation only, not this fake-provider engineering proof.

#### PAY-007 — Payouts and reconciliation.
Each Stripe payout is matched to the payments, refunds and fees inside it; checks and cash are grouped into deposit batches; anything unmatched is listed with the reason, so the bank deposit, Glove and the books agree. Where Stripe offers Instant Payouts on the owner's account, the owner requests one to an eligible debit card with Stripe's fee shown before confirming, and it reconciles the same way.

DATA-002 inheritance: before this capability ships, every new command, communication, payment or sync action and provider outcome keeps its actor/provider, time, references and actual effect in Activity and the business export; credentials and secret payloads never enter the trail.

*Acceptance:* a flow test replays a payout of six payments, one refund and fees (Stripe fake) and checks the payout reconciles to the cent, with a deliberately missing payment listed as unmatched; an instant payout (Stripe fake) with its fee reconciles the same way.

The flow also asserts each new DATA-002 event in Activity and the business export, including provider outcomes and references without secrets.

*Semantics (23 October 2026):* the owner inspects a USD Stripe test payout (`payout.reconcile`). Automatic payouts use Stripe's paginated source balance transactions (up to 1,000; a longer/incomplete list refuses reconciliation) and match confirmed payment/refund references, gross cents and fees; missing, unconfirmed, wrong-amount, wrong-fee/currency and unsupported sources are named. Bank amount plus any payout fee must equal source net exactly, and only provider-paid payouts reconcile. An unavailable payout fee refuses reconciliation; zero is never inferred. Stripe does not attribute source payments to manual payouts. Those require an explicit owner-reviewed books allocation (`payout.allocate`), never claimed as Stripe source attribution; unreviewed sources remain unmatched. Cash/check deposits (`payment.depositBatch`) group 1–100 confirmed same-currency/branch records once, net of prior recorded refunds, as staff-recorded deposits without a bank-confirmation claim. For Instant Payouts, the owner records their account-specific fee schedule (basis points and minimum, labelled owner-recorded and not verified by Glove); a preview checks an existing Stripe debit card's instant eligibility and funds, computes the half-up fee, and requires selected source net to equal the proposed bank amount plus that fee. The owner explicitly confirms both displayed amounts within ten minutes; source availability, current connection, schedule, eligibility and funds are checked again. A durable reservation and Stripe idempotency key precede one request. Replay never resends an unknown outcome. Sources cannot be allocated to another payout. Reconciliation checks the actual payout debit, source allocation and actual fee against the confirmed preview; a difference stays unmatched. Only the owner reads provider-wide payout and fee records; the office sees its branch's cash/check batches. DATA-001 removes erased customer references from shared payout source detail while retaining anonymous aggregate bank history.

*Status:* done — evidence `tests/api/payments.test.mjs` › “PAY-007 six confirmed payments, refund and fees reconcile exactly; missing source is named; explicit Instant Payout fee and cash/check batches never duplicate” (six payments, refund/fees and cent totals, missing source, manual allocation, bank batches, eligible debit card, explicit fee confirmation, actual-fee mismatch, replay/duplicate-source refusal, Activity/export and erasure); `tests/browser/receivables.spec.ts` › “PAY-007 owner inspects unmatched payouts, confirms the visible instant fee and groups cash/check deposits at 1440 and 390” proves the real controls, provider-paid/unmatched states, explicit $15 fee preview/confirmation, cash/check batches, reflow and absence of icon-only controls at both widths.

#### PAY-008 — Saved payment methods and autopay with consent.
A customer saves a card or bank account in the hub or on site, with the authorization text and time recorded (for ACH, the recurring debit authorization the Nacha rules require); saved methods pay agreements and approved invoices automatically when the customer chose autopay, the office charges them on demand under `PAY-009` when the recorded authorization covers charges the business starts, and a failed charge sends an update link.

*Acceptance:* a flow test saves a card with consent, charges an agreement renewal, fails the next charge (Stripe fake) and checks the update-link message and the retained authorization record.

PLAN-005 fixed-monthly invoices inherit its ct-approved period-end issue date, separate approval time, later-of-period/approval due-date basis, approval-gated aging and frozen report/export dates; preexisting documents keep their original basis. This is proven with PLAN-005’s named monthly-date flow before that capability ships.


*Acceptance inherited from PLAN-005 (ct carve, 22 October 2026 22:10Z):* When the customer chose autopay, the invoice is charged to the saved method under `PAY-008`, with the receipt sent, and a failed charge follows `PLAN-003`'s dunning. a flow test runs two series for three months (Stripe fake): a per-visit series with one visit in month 1, two in month 2 (a third skipped) and none in month 3, and a quarterly commercial PM job billed $240.00 fixed monthly on autopay; it checks exactly three per-visit invoices with each visit's lines, exactly three $240.00 invoices dated the last day of each month, each charged once, with one failed charge entering dunning and recovered; an on-close series invoices once when the job closes.

**CT preparatory policy — 26 October 2026; APPROVED WITH RIDERS.** Only a signed-in Owner without branch limits may record customer permission or explicitly execute this preparatory payment flow. Permission is an Owner-recorded account of the customer’s consent, not customer authentication or proof of consent obtained through Glove. Retain its source, exact authorization wording, recorded time, recording Owner, customer, verified saved test-card reference, fixed-monthly rule identity and revision, cadence, maximum USD amount, and active or withdrawn state. A replacement permission creates a distinct version and does not overwrite the earlier record; withdrawal remains attributable and timed.

The permission covers only the named card and fixed-monthly rule revision within the stated maximum. Each full unpaid approved invoice requires the Owner’s explicit confirmation of its displayed invoice, card and USD amount. Confirmation does not authorize another invoice or a retry. Billing, schedules, agents and restore never invoke a charge automatically in this preparatory flow. Provider requests are test mode only; live charges are refused.

Recheck current Owner authority, customer and card association, provider-verified card attachment, active permission, exact rule revision, running billing state, invoice approval, unpaid amount and cap before reserving one provider attempt. Existing durable reservation, idempotency and authenticated settlement boundaries apply. An unknown attempt blocks a further attempt; a decline does not trigger an automatic retry. Withdrawal, paused billing or a changed card, rule revision, cadence or maximum refuses further requests until fresh permission is recorded. Withdrawal does not erase the actual outcome of an already-authorized request in flight.

Refusals identify the required correction without exposing credentials or another customer’s records: “Owner access required”; “Test mode only; live charges are unavailable”; “Fresh customer permission is required for this card and billing rule”; “Billing is paused; no charge was requested”; “Approve the invoice before charging”; “The invoice must be fully unpaid”; “The invoice exceeds the authorized USD maximum”; or “A prior payment attempt is unresolved; no further charge was requested”, as applicable. A refused request makes no payment-provider request and changes no invoice balance.

Record permission versions, withdrawal, explicit execution and provider outcomes with actor, time, permitted references and actual effect in Activity and business export. Keep authorization evidence Owner-only and exclude raw payment data and credentials. Customer erasure covers personal consent evidence, saved-method associations, personal command results and timeline copies under DATA-001 while preserving required anonymous financial totals. Restore retains permitted business history but imports no provider credentials, creates no payment authority and resumes no charge. Reconnection alone does not replace current permission or explicit invoice confirmation. The trusted later erasure journal prevents an older backup from reviving erased evidence; an offline destination cannot discover a later erasure without that journal.

PAY-008 remains PARTIAL. All original acceptance and PLAN-005 inheritance remain binding.
