Gaps include customer-side card collection and consent, bank authorization, automatic agreement renewals and recurring collection, retries, PLAN-003 dunning and recovery, receipts, failed-charge update links and card updates, per-visit and on-close collection proof, and complete customer-facing journeys at 1440 and 390. This policy establishes no payment-network or legal compliance.

*Status:* partial — `tests/api/recurring-billing.test.mjs` › “PAY-008 explicit Owner test charges bind customer permission to a monthly rule and cap, settle once, and stop on withdrawal or rule change” reports proof of retained source, exact wording, time and rule-specific consent, three frozen $240 monthly invoices, explicit test charges once each, authenticated settlement, a declined second charge without retry, cap and role refusals, withdrawal, changed-rule refusal, owner-only consent evidence and attributable Activity and business export. This is repository-reported evidence, not independently executed by CT. This is preparatory explicit execution, not automatic renewal, receipt, update-link or dunning completion. Gap: Customer-side saved-card setup and bank authorization; automatic agreement renewals and recurring charges; receipts, failed-charge update links and PLAN-003 dunning with recovery; per-visit and on-close charge proof; and customer-facing 1440 and 390 journeys remain unbuilt. Approved with riders: the CT preparatory policy above governs; implementation of every rider is not established. PAY-008 stays PARTIAL.

#### PAY-009 — Office charges a card by phone or on file.
The Owner, the Office role and any custom role granted "take card payments" (`TEAM-001`) open an invoice, a deposit (`QUOTE-003`), a cancellation fee (`BOOK-002`) or an agreement charge and press **Charge card**. They either key in the card the customer reads out on the phone, or pick one of the customer's saved methods (`PAY-008`) and charge it at once.
- **Keyed cards never touch Glove.** The card number, expiry and security code are typed into Stripe's own card-entry element inside Glove's page and go straight to Stripe; Glove's server, records, logs and exports never hold them. A keyed card is sent as a mail or telephone order (MOTO) where the owner's Stripe account allows it. Where Stripe demands customer authentication instead, Glove texts or emails the customer a pay link for the same amount.
- **Saved methods follow their authorization.** A saved card is charged on demand only when its `PAY-008` authorization covers charges the business starts; a saved bank account is debited only under an authorization that covers it (a Nacha recurring or single-entry authorization). Otherwise Glove refuses and offers the pay link. While keying a card, the office can save it for later charges only after reading the customer the consent text, which is stored with the method as its `PAY-008` authorization.
- **Paid only on Stripe's word.** Before confirming, the screen shows the amount, the card brand and last four digits, any surcharge under `PAY-002`'s settings and an optional tip. The payment is recorded only when Stripe confirms it, and lands on the invoice, the customer balance and the timeline with who charged it. A decline leaves the balance open and shows Stripe's decline reason.
- **Desk and phone alike.** The same command runs at the desk and on a phone 390 px wide (`FIELD-008`), under the same role checks. Anyone without the permission (Dispatcher, Technician, Agent, Viewer) is refused through every door; a technician takes card-present payments on their own visits under `PAY-003`.

DATA-002 inheritance: before this capability ships, every new command, communication, payment or sync action and provider outcome keeps its actor/provider, time, references and actual effect in Activity and the business export; credentials and secret payloads never enter the trail.

*Acceptance:* a flow test (Stripe fake with webhooks) signs in as Office and charges a keyed card against a $412.00 invoice and a saved card against a $500.00 deposit. Each payment is recorded only on Stripe's confirmation event, with the Office person on the timeline. A declined keyed card leaves the $412.00 balance open with the decline reason. A saved card whose authorization covers only autopay is refused, with the pay link offered. A Technician, a Dispatcher and an Agent are refused the charge through the command and through HTTP. No request body, record, log line or business export contains a card number. A browser journey at 390 px signed in as Owner charges a saved card on an invoice from the phone with no horizontal scroll.

The flow also asserts each new DATA-002 event in Activity and the business export, including provider outcomes and references without secrets.

*Status:* partial — evidence `tests/api/payments.test.mjs` › “FIELD-008 saved test cards require customer authorization and desk authority, reserve once, and pay only on authenticated Stripe confirmation” (Office saved-card charge, verified customer and attachment, explicit business-charge authorization, autopay-only and role refusals, uncertain attempt blocking, authenticated Stripe settlement and decline, authorization withdrawal, Activity and business export); `tests/browser/journey.spec.ts` › “The Owner runs the whole phone desk and Office repeats its permitted steps with exact role refusals at 390 and 1440” (Owner and Office saved-card invoice controls with amount, brand and last four digits, pending and paid states, no horizontal scroll). Gap: Keyed Stripe card entry and MOTO entitlement, saved-card deposits and other charge subjects, custom-role charges, saved bank authorization, optional tips and customer authentication or payment-link activation remain unbuilt. Existing Checkout remains owner-only under PAY-002. Adopted owner-registered test-card authorization and explicit Owner or Office charges require ct approval; no live activation is supplied.

#### LEDGER-001 — QuickBooks Online sync.
The owner connects QuickBooks Online; the first connection to a company that already holds records links them under `LEDGER-004` before anything posts. Glove keeps customers, items, invoices, payments, refunds, Stripe fees and deposits in sync, maps income accounts, tax codes and classes or locations once, and reads back payments recorded in QuickBooks; every sync error is shown on the record with the QuickBooks message and can be retried after a fix; nothing is posted twice. A customer with many properties (a property manager or landlord) can be mapped as a QuickBooks parent customer with a sub-customer per property: each property's invoices post to its sub-customer, the parent's statement rolls them up, and payments the parent makes against several properties are applied to each sub-customer's invoices.

DATA-002 inheritance: before this capability ships, every new command, communication, payment or sync action and provider outcome keeps its actor/provider, time, references and actual effect in Activity and the business export; credentials and secret payloads never enter the trail.

MONEY-001 inheritance: before this capability ships, post the frozen USD jurisdiction/tax snapshot with explicit existing-record mappings and assert the provider receives it to the cent; the current downloadable payload is inspection only, never a sync.

QUOTE-007 inheritance: before this capability ships, post each QUOTE-007 discount as the provider's discount line (tax after discount) with its non-taxable and taxable amounts and assert the provider fake receives the accepted figures to the cent.

*Acceptance:* a flow test syncs a customer, invoice, payment and refund (QuickBooks fake), replays the same sync without duplicates, receives a rejected item with its message, fixes and retries it, and reads a payment entered in QuickBooks back onto the invoice. A property manager with two properties mapped as sub-customers posts each property's invoice to its own sub-customer under the parent, and one payment covering both is applied to both invoices.

The flow also asserts each new DATA-002 event in Activity and the business export, including provider outcomes and references without secrets.

PLAN-005 fixed-monthly invoices inherit its ct-approved period-end issue date, separate approval time, later-of-period/approval due-date basis, approval-gated aging and frozen report/export dates; preexisting documents keep their original basis. This is proven with PLAN-005’s named monthly-date flow before that capability ships.

*Preparatory sync semantics (26 October 2026):* only a signed-in unscoped Owner explicitly connects a QuickBooks sandbox company, verifies existing record IDs, confirms the pre-existing links, posts one record or reads one provider payment. The access token is an installation secret, excluded from browser command storage, domain state, Activity, business exports and archives. Same-company token replacement retains link identities; another company requires fresh links and confirmation. Each mapped sales item retains its existing income account; optional class and department references and the transaction tax code are verified against that company. Parent-billed properties must be actual sub-customers of their mapped parent. An existing invoice number refuses creation until the owner links the matching customer, number and total. A durable reservation precedes each provider write; replay retains the provider identity, a definite rejection permits explicit retry after repair, and an uncertain or interrupted operation blocks another send. The owner may reconcile an uncertain result only to an existing record whose returned fields match the exact reserved content; this performs no new post. The provider-confirmed tax and total must equal the frozen approved cents; a mismatch retains its reference for reconciliation. Readback applies only exact, fully allocated USD payments to their mapped invoices and never counts Glove-origin payments twice. Each allocation retains its invoice tax snapshot. Provider effects already authorized in flight remain recorded if the initiating person loses authority; this grants no later send authority. Full-invoice refunds preserve the approved tax and discount snapshot. There is no automatic sync or retry, production connection, new customer payment authority or live-provider proof. These preparatory semantics do not narrow LEDGER-001 or LEDGER-004.

*Status:* partial — `tests/api/quickbooks.test.mjs` › “QuickBooks sync posts mapped customer, frozen invoice, payment and refund once; a rejected item is repaired and retried with secret-free history” proves verified existing customer and item mappings, customer and item updates, approved invoice transport, payment and full refund transport, replay on a later date without another post, repeated customer edits including return to an earlier value, provider rejection shown on the record, repair and retry, frozen branch tax of $11.32 and total $164.83 after settings changes, a 30% discount with $30.30 non-taxable and $35.49 taxable allocation, payment echo suppression, role refusal, disconnect, and attributable provider references in Activity and business export without the access token. › “QuickBooks property sub-customers receive separate invoices and one parent payment settles both without replay or unknown-send duplicates” proves two mapped sub-customers billed through their parent, each invoice posted to its property, one parent payment read back onto both invoice balances, exact collected tax, property history, readback replay, uncertain-send refusal and exact-reference reconciliation without another post. › “QuickBooks first-post review refuses unconfirmed links and duplicate invoices; provider facts survive owner withdrawal without allowing another send” proves explicit first-post confirmation, duplicate document refusal, linking an existing invoice, payment against that reference, and recording the authorized in-flight provider result after the initiating person loses Owner authority. `tests/browser/quickbooks.spec.ts` › “QuickBooks owner connects and maps existing records, repairs a rejected invoice, syncs once and reads its payment at 1440 and 390” proves the owner settings, verified maps, confirmation, rejection, explicit retry, replay, uncertain-send reconciliation and one parent payment displayed as the correct allocation on each property invoice in the built application at both widths. `tests/api/quickbooks.test.mjs` › “QuickBooks mapped fixed discount item and document discount preserve accepted taxable and non-taxable cents at the provider edge” proves the mapped negative discount item, document discount and frozen $15.59 tax with a $230.69 total. › “QuickBooks rejection messages follow customer export and erasure without credentials or a surviving personal outbox payload” proves access-token redaction, customer export, erasure during an in-flight provider refusal, suppression of late personal outcomes and post-erasure send refusal. Existing PLAN-005 date inheritance remains proved by `tests/api/recurring-billing.test.mjs` and `tests/browser/recurring-billing.spec.ts`. Gap: Production OAuth authorization and token refresh, owner account activation, automatic customer and item discovery and the complete LEDGER-004 reviewed matching plan, creating approved unmatched records, Stripe fees and deposits, partial refunds, refund readback, changed imported payments, posted-invoice corrections, and recovery after an interrupted in-flight send remain unbuilt or unproved. Live automated-sales-tax jurisdiction and taxable-basis reconciliation remain unverified. Only sandbox companies with manually verified existing records are supported. The owner must authorize the production Intuit app and company after production connection support ships; no live provider call or activation is claimed. LEDGER-001 remains PARTIAL.

#### LEDGER-002 — Xero sync.
The same as `LEDGER-001` for Xero; the existing sales-invoice CSV remains for owners who import by file.

DATA-002 inheritance: before this capability ships, every new command, communication, payment or sync action and provider outcome keeps its actor/provider, time, references and actual effect in Activity and the business export; credentials and secret payloads never enter the trail.

*Acceptance:* the `LEDGER-001` flow against a Xero fake.

The flow also asserts each new DATA-002 event in Activity and the business export, including provider outcomes and references without secrets.

PLAN-005 fixed-monthly invoices inherit its ct-approved period-end issue date, separate approval time, later-of-period/approval due-date basis, approval-gated aging and frozen report/export dates; preexisting documents keep their original basis. This is proven with PLAN-005’s named monthly-date flow before that capability ships.

*Preparatory semantics (26 October 2026):* Xero inspection is off by default. Only a signed-in Owner without branch limits explicitly connects and reads the fixed Xero Accounting API. Custom roles and integration tokens are refused. The tenant identifier and OAuth access token remain installation secrets, outside domain state, Activity, business export and archives. The connection is verified with a read; replacement for the same tenant retains its local connection identity. Inspection returns only whitelisted contact, item and sales-invoice summaries in the immediate response, not saved command outcomes or Activity bodies. Provider errors refuse inspection, redirects are refused, and potentially incomplete lists refuse rather than claiming company-wide discovery. Disconnect removes credentials; restored domain history grants no read or write access. Inspection itself grants no posting, automatic execution, mapping confirmation or customer payment authority. Xero has no QuickBooks-style sub-customer; this preparatory slice makes no property allocation claim. CT approved preparatory default: Owner-only read inspection; the future connector must review an explicit parent-contact and property representation before any post, preserving all inherited allocation obligations.

CT scope ruling, 26 October 2026: Owner-only Xero inspection is approved as a preparatory default, with no posting, mapping confirmation, payment allocation or money-movement authority. The complete inherited LEDGER-001 target and Xero-fake acceptance remain binding, including existing-record review, two property invoices, the parent roll-up and one parent remittance allocated to both invoices. The product representation uses one reviewed Xero billing contact and distinct Glove properties, with each property invoice linked to its property and that contact. A documented supported invoice reference or tracking dimension identifies the property without discarding the purchase-order reference; no QuickBooks-style contact hierarchy is assumed. One parent remittance retains explicit invoice allocations; the proposed transport uses invoice-linked Xero payment applications grouped locally under that remittance, with provider identities and exact allocated cents, subject to official capability verification. Readback must preserve each property’s balance and frozen tax and discount amounts without duplicate cash or payment echoes. Existing branch, role, reviewed-link, approval, replay, uncertainty, audit, export, archive and erasure obligations continue to apply. This representation supplies neither provider proof nor new write authority and does not narrow the target to read-only. LEDGER-002 remains PARTIAL.

**Historical CT assessment, 26 October 2026 (retained verbatim):** Gap retained after CT ruling: Owner-only inspection and CSV do not prove verified mapping, complete paginated discovery and first-post review, customer and item sync, frozen invoice, tax, discount and monthly-date transport, payment and refund sync and readback, durable replay, correction and uncertain-send recovery, fees and deposits, inherited parent and property allocations, owner screens at 1440 and 390, OAuth refresh or live connection acceptance. Xero’s contact, property-reference, multi-invoice remittance and reconciliation capabilities require official primary-source verification; this ruling supplies no provider proof.

*Local mapping semantics (27 October 2026):* A separate explicit Owner command now verifies and records existing billing-contact and sales-item links, and a fresh fingerprint-bound command records their local review. Neither command authorizes a provider write, first post or customer payment. Provider identifiers are Owner-only on current reads, command outcomes and error records. Credentials remain installation-private; customer erasure removes customer links and personal outcomes. See [XERO.md](XERO.md) for the supported commands and retrieval limits.

*Status:* partial — `tests/api/xero.test.mjs` › “Xero Owner discovers every page, reviews verified existing links and preserves authority and customer privacy without accounting writes” proves bounded complete-page reads for contacts and invoices, whole-list items, rejection of duplicate or malformed pages and later-page failure, explicit existing billing-contact and sales-item links with verified income accounts and revenue tax types, duplicate-link and stale-review refusals, same-tenant retention and different-tenant review, Owner-only commands and read custody, customer export and erasure, and attributable provider references without credentials. This is local link review, not accounting sync or first-post authorization. Existing CSV evidence and the earlier read-only inspection test remain applicable. Gap: Property mappings, accounting dimensions, complete first-post review, customer and item sync, frozen invoice, tax, discount and monthly-date transport, payment and refund sync and readback, durable replay, correction and uncertain-send recovery, fees and deposits, parent remittance allocation and bank reconciliation, owner screens at 1440 and 390, OAuth refresh and live connection acceptance remain unbuilt or unproved. Official contact roll-up, property reference and purchase-order coexistence, multi-invoice remittance and bank-reconciliation capability verification remain outstanding. Bounded paginated retrieval does not prove a transactionally frozen company snapshot. LEDGER-002 remains PARTIAL.

The inspection regression in `tests/api/quickbooks.test.mjs` also proves active-only contacts (including GDPRREQUEST, absent and unknown-status refusals), attributed inspection, token replacement, exact existing sales-invoice customer/number/USD-total/state validation, duplicate links, confirmation-time revalidation, retained verifying and confirming Owner/time, and Owner-only link custody. Invoice links use main's version-bound explicit mapping and fingerprint review; replacing a billing contact invalidates its dependent invoice links. No provider write is authorized.

#### LEDGER-003 — Pantry sync.
For an owner who keeps their books in Pantry, Run Edit Run's accounting product, Glove posts the same records through Pantry's API with the same error and retry behavior.

DATA-002 inheritance: before this capability ships, every new command, communication, payment or sync action and provider outcome keeps its actor/provider, time, references and actual effect in Activity and the business export; credentials and secret payloads never enter the trail.

*Acceptance:* the `LEDGER-001` flow against Pantry's documented API (fake).

The flow also asserts each new DATA-002 event in Activity and the business export, including provider outcomes and references without secrets.

*Status:* missing — gap: no Pantry connection.

#### LEDGER-004 — First QuickBooks connection linked to existing records.
An owner moving in usually connects a QuickBooks Online company that already holds their customers, products and services and years of invoices, synced there by Jobber or Housecall Pro. On that first connection Glove posts nothing until the owner has reviewed and confirmed a link plan.
- **Proposed links.** Glove reads the company's customers (with sub-customers), items and open and recent invoices, and proposes a link for each Glove customer, price-book item and moved-in invoice. Customers match first by the QuickBooks customer ID the move-in carried where the source kept one (`MOVE-005`), then by display name together with email or phone. Items match by SKU, then by name. Moved-in invoices match by the carried QuickBooks ID, then by invoice number with the same customer and total.
- **Owner review.** The review lists four groups: matched (with the rule that matched), ambiguous (two or more candidates; the owner picks one or none), Glove records with no match (the owner approves creating each in QuickBooks or links it by hand), and QuickBooks records with no Glove counterpart (left alone, or brought into Glove through the `CUST-003` review). A QuickBooks name clash ("another customer already uses this name") appears in the review before anything posts, never as a sync error afterwards.
- **Only confirmed writes.** Confirming writes only the confirmed links and the new records the owner approved. A linked moved-in invoice is never posted again; later payments and refunds on it post against the QuickBooks invoice it links to. Each link records who confirmed it and can be changed until the first post that uses it. Reconnecting the same company keeps the links; connecting a different company starts a new review.

DATA-002 inheritance: before this capability ships, every new command, communication, payment or sync action and provider outcome keeps its actor/provider, time, references and actual effect in Activity and the business export; credentials and secret payloads never enter the trail.

MONEY-001 inheritance: before this capability ships, post the frozen USD jurisdiction/tax snapshot with explicit existing-record mappings and assert the provider receives it to the cent; the current downloadable payload is inspection only, never a sync.

QUOTE-007 inheritance: before this capability ships, post each QUOTE-007 discount as the provider's discount line (tax after discount) with its non-taxable and taxable amounts and assert the provider fake receives the accepted figures to the cent.

*Current command semantics (26 October 2026):* discovery reads every paginated customer (including inactive records and sub-customers), sales item and invoice, refusing an invalid or overlapping page or a company beyond 100,000 records per kind rather than offering a partial plan. Matching folds case and whitespace for names and emails and removes phone punctuation; it never matches on name alone. Existing verified same-company customer and item choices take precedence. Item SKU precedes name; an invoice uses its original imported number, linked customer and original total, not its remaining balance. Remote review names are returned transiently; Activity retains the read fingerprint, local and provider references and matching rules. An explicit customer creation must name the current review fingerprint, customer version and affirmative approval; it refuses matches, name clashes and unresolved earlier sends. Its durable reservation and provider reference use the existing sync custody and reconciliation. Confirmation records the actor on each link. A dependent reserved or completed post prevents reassignment of its accounting identities. The owner review uses these same commands. Carried source-ID matching remains unbuilt because the documented source shapes do not define those accounting fields.

*Owner review semantics (26 October 2026):* Team and settings groups matched records with their rules, ambiguous choices, unmatched Glove records and untouched provider-only records. Candidate customer names and contact fields, item names and SKUs, and invoice numbers and original totals are transient whitelisted comparison data. They stay in screen memory and are excluded from retained command results and audit bodies. Verifying matched groups uses each existing verified-link command and creates no provider record. Choosing none leaves an ambiguity unresolved; manual linking remains available. Each unmatched customer needs its own affirmative creation checkbox and the current review fingerprint. A visible name clash prevents creation, and the command rechecks the same boundary. Refresh follows each change. Final link confirmation refuses any unresolved pre-existing records and retains the confirming actor. Linked imported balances expose later payment sync without allowing the old invoice to be posted again.

*Acceptance:* a flow test moves in a Jobber fixture (`MOVE-001`) with 50 customers, 20 price-book items and their open invoices, then connects a QuickBooks fake that already holds 48 of those customers (10 matched by the carried QuickBooks ID, 37 by name with email or phone, and one name with two QuickBooks candidates), all 20 items and the open invoices. The review lists exactly 47 matched customers, one ambiguous pair, two Glove customers with no match and 20 matched items, and the fake receives no write before confirmation. After the owner picks the ambiguous match and approves creating the two unmatched customers, the fake holds no duplicate customer or item, the two new customers exist once each, the moved-in invoices are linked and not re-created, a payment on one of them posts against its existing QuickBooks invoice, and the next new Glove invoice posts against the linked customer.

The flow also asserts each new DATA-002 event in Activity and the business export, including provider outcomes and references without secrets.

*Status:* partial — `tests/api/quickbooks.test.mjs` › “QuickBooks company discovery proposes imported customer, item and original invoice links without a write or a partial company review” proves a saved-shape 50-customer and 20-item Jobber move-in; 47 contact matches, one ambiguous pair and two unmatched customers; SKU before name for items; original invoice number and total despite a partially paid opening balance; provider-only sub-customers; pre-write name clashes; complete paginated reads and refusal of overlapping pages; owner-only access; same-company link retention and different-company confirmation refusal; and secret-free Activity and export references. › “QuickBooks reviewed migration creates only two explicitly approved unmatched customers, links old invoices and posts later money once with locked confirmed links” proves review-bound explicit approval, stale-review and name-clash refusal, creation replay, 48 old invoices linked without recreation, later payment against its existing invoice, one new invoice against the linked customer with frozen tax and discount cents, per-link confirmer identity, dependent-post link locks, definite-refusal repair and uncertain-creation reconciliation without another send. `tests/api/quickbooks.test.mjs` › “QuickBooks first-post review refuses unconfirmed links and duplicate invoices; provider facts survive owner withdrawal without allowing another send” proves the manual prerequisite: verified existing customer, item and invoice links, explicit confirmation before posting, duplicate-number refusal, and payment against the linked existing invoice. `tests/browser/quickbooks.spec.ts` proves the manual mapping and confirmation controls at 1440 and 390. `tests/browser/quickbooks.spec.ts` › “QuickBooks company review resolves ambiguous links, approves two creations and preserves moved invoices at 1440 and 390” proves the matched groups and rules, one-or-none ambiguity choice, per-customer creation approval, pre-write name-clash refusal, untouched provider-only records, refused premature confirmation, verified existing links, two creations exactly once, all 48 old invoices retained without posting, later payment replay and the next linked invoice in the built owner screens at both widths. `tests/api/quickbooks.test.mjs` › “QuickBooks property sub-customers receive separate invoices and one parent payment settles both without replay or unknown-send duplicates” proves a fresh different-company review, mandatory property remapping, new-company invoice links and confirmation without provider writes, same-company retention, no repost of either old invoice and one later payment against the new linked invoice with Activity and export references. Gap: Carried source QuickBooks customer, item and invoice IDs and their ordered source-ID matching remain unbuilt: the held documented Jobber parent and product export shapes do not define those accounting fields. The 50-customer fixture proves 47 name-and-contact matches, not the required 10 carried-ID plus 37 contact-match split. Different-company reconciliation of previously posted payments and unresolved sends, moved-in refund posting and production connection and owner activation remain unproved.

### Recurring service and maintenance agreements

#### PLAN-001 — Recurring jobs.
A job can repeat on a rule (every 3 months, the first Monday, twice a year in April and October), generating visits over a rolling horizon; one visit can be skipped or moved without changing the series; changing the series from a date forward keeps the past; each series carries the invoicing rule of `PLAN-005`.

*Acceptance:* a flow test creates a quarterly series, skips one visit, moves another, changes the rule from a date, and checks every generated visit.

*Semantics (PLAN-001, 19 October 2026):* the dispatcher or owner makes an open job repeat (`series.create`) from a first local date, at a visit start and end time with one technician, on a rule: every 1–52 weeks (on the first date's weekday), or every 1–52 months, or every year in named months, on a day of the month (the first date's unless given; a day the month lacks becomes its last day) or on the first to fourth or last given weekday of the month; intervals count from the rule's first date. Dates are local dates in the business time zone. The series may end on a date (inclusive). Visits are booked for every occurrence through the earlier of that end and today plus the series' rolling horizon (7–730 days, 365 by default): at create and change, conflicts are reviewed under `GLOVE-05` as for one booking; after every later command the horizon rolls forward and the newly booked visits carry their conflicts unreviewed, named on the job timeline for review on Dispatch, and an occurrence that cannot be booked (for example an inactive technician) is recorded once on the series and named on the timeline. Each visit is an ordinary visit carrying its rule revision and occurrence date: moving it (`visit.change`) or skipping it (`visit.cancel`, labelled skipped) leaves the series unchanged, and an occurrence that has a visit is never booked again. Changing the series from a date (`series.change`, with a reason, not before its first date) starts a new rule revision there: visits on or after that date that are still booked and untouched (no arrival, never moved) are canceled with that reason and booked again under the new revision; earlier visits and moved, skipped, started or completed ones stay as they are. Completing one visit leaves a recurring job open; a visit completes the job only once the series has an end date, has booked through it and no other visit of the job is booked. The series' invoicing rule comes with `PLAN-005`, which carries it in its text and acceptance; until then a recurring job is invoiced as one job under `GLOVE-09`.

*Status:* done — evidence `src/glove/recurrence.ts`, `src/glove/commands/series.ts`, `src/glove/commands/field.ts`, `src/ui/series.tsx`, `tests/api/series.test.mjs`.

#### PLAN-002 — Maintenance agreements and memberships.
The owner defines plans (for example a yearly HVAC "Comfort Club": price, term, included visits, member discount on repairs, priority booking); a plan is sold on a quote, in the hub or by phone; each agreement tracks entitlements used and left ("1 visit left, next April 3") and applies the member discount automatically. A plan can carry `FIELD-002` forms; each visit booked under the agreement takes a copy, as a job does for a service's forms. The `REPORT-001` dashboard's agreements-due tile counts the agreements with an included visit due and not yet completed or declined (`PLAN-004`). The `REPORT-002` reports gain the agreement count and churn (agreements ended or not renewed in the period) with this clause. A `CUST-004` segment's "no agreement" filter then excludes customers with an active agreement.

*Semantics (PLAN-002, 21 October 2026):* the owner defines a plan (`plan.save`: name, price in USD, a term of 1 to 60 whole months, 0 to 24 included visits of one price-book service, a member repair discount of 0 to 99.99%, priority booking on or off, and inspection forms). The desk (owner, dispatcher, office) sells it to a customer at one of their sites (`agreement.sell`), recorded as sold by phone, in person or on a quote that customer accepted; the agreement keeps a copy of the plan as sold, and one site holds at most one agreement in a term. Selling from the customer hub is `COMM-006`'s, which inherits this obligation before it ships. A term runs from the start date for the plan's months; its last day is the day before the same date that many months later (local dates in the business time zone, both ends included; a 31st becomes the month's last day). The k-th of n included visits (k from 0) is due on the start date plus floor(k × months ÷ n) months. An included visit is booked as a service call under the agreement (`job.create` with `agreementId`) at $0.00 for the plan's service, while the visits completed plus those booked and not yet completed are fewer than the included visits; it is used when a visit on that job is completed; the job takes the plan's forms besides its service's. `GET /api/glove/agreements` shows each agreement's included, used, booked and left visits and the next due date ("1 of 2 visits left, next Apr 3, 2027"). While an agreement is active (today inside its term and not ended by `agreement.end`), every quote option and service call for that customer drafted without an explicit discount takes the member discount as a `QUOTE-007` percent document discount named "<plan> member", which no role's discount limit refuses; an explicit discount replaces it and is held to the limit; included visits take none. The agreement, and its end, appear in the site's property history (`CUST-001`) as `agreement` rows, newest first. Priority booking is shown on the plan and on the member's agreement for the desk booking by hand; `BOOK-002` and `AGENT-001` inherit it and state and assert how they prefer a priority member before either ships. The `REPORT-001` agreements-due tile now counts each outstanding included visit due today or earlier under `PLAN-004`, including booked visits and outstanding work after term end, until completed or explicitly declined under `PLAN-004`. The `REPORT-002` agreements report (`/api/glove/reports/agreements.json|csv`) counts agreements sold in the period by the local date sold, by plan with their value, those active on the period's last day, and churn: agreements ended in the period or whose term ended in it (until `PLAN-003` renewals exist, an ended term is not renewed). A `CUST-004` segment's "no agreement" filter leaves out customers with an agreement active today, including on the Customers screen, where it is now a choice rather than always on.

*Acceptance:* a flow test sells a plan with two included tune-ups and 15% repair discount, books and completes one tune-up, quotes a repair, and checks the entitlement count and the discount, that the completed tune-up's job carried the plan's inspection form, and that the `REPORT-001` agreements-due tile counts the agreement while its second tune-up is due, and that the `REPORT-002` agreement report counts the sold agreement and no churn, and that a `CUST-004` "no agreement" segment excludes the customer.

QUOTE-007 inheritance: before this capability ships, the member repair discount is a QUOTE-007 document discount or discount line, allocated non-taxable first and taxed after discount, carried quote → job → invoice, and asserted on the CSV, the QuickBooks payload and reports.

*Status:* done — evidence `src/glove/agreements.ts`, `src/ui/agreements.tsx`, `tests/api/agreements.test.mjs`, `src/glove/history.ts`, `src/glove/api.ts`, `tests/browser/due-visits.spec.ts`.

#### PLAN-003 — Recurring billing and renewals.
Agreements bill monthly or yearly through saved payment methods (`PAY-008`); renewal notices go out ahead of the term end with the terms state auto-renewal laws require the owner to show; customers can cancel or update their card in the hub; a failed charge follows the dunning schedule and the agreement shows its state.

*Acceptance:* a flow test runs a monthly agreement through three charges, one failed and recovered, then a renewal notice and an auto-renewal, and a cancellation from the hub.

PLAN-005 fixed-monthly invoices inherit its ct-approved period-end issue date, separate approval time, later-of-period/approval due-date basis, approval-gated aging and frozen report/export dates; preexisting documents keep their original basis. This is proven with PLAN-005’s named monthly-date flow before that capability ships.


*Acceptance inherited from PLAN-005 (ct carve, 22 October 2026 22:10Z):* When the customer chose autopay, the invoice is charged to the saved method under `PAY-008`, with the receipt sent, and a failed charge follows `PLAN-003`'s dunning. a flow test runs two series for three months (Stripe fake): a per-visit series with one visit in month 1, two in month 2 (a third skipped) and none in month 3, and a quarterly commercial PM job billed $240.00 fixed monthly on autopay; it checks exactly three per-visit invoices with each visit's lines, exactly three $240.00 invoices dated the last day of each month, each charged once, with one failed charge entering dunning and recovered; an on-close series invoices once when the job closes.

*Status:* missing — gap: no recurring billing.

#### PLAN-004 — Due-visit scheduling until done or declined.
Every included visit due in a period appears in a due list until it is completed or the customer explicitly declines; the office books them in batches (by area and season) and sends booking links; nothing due is ever silently dropped. The due list's remaining visits are the `REPORT-001` agreements-due tile's visit count.

*Acceptance:* a flow test generates 40 due visits, batch-books 30 by area, sends links for 8, records 2 declines, and checks the due list shows exactly the remainder, and the `REPORT-001` agreements-due tile the same number of due visits.

AGENT-008 inheritance: before this capability ships, the morning brief lists agreements with visits due, with their agreement link and a proposed action, asserted in the AGENT-008 flow.

*Semantics (PLAN-004 partial slice, 21 October 2026):* `GET /api/glove/due-visits` lists each included visit separately, identified by agreement and zero-based ordinal, with its PLAN-002 due date, customer, site, ZIP, linked job and booking state. `through` defaults to the business's local today; optional `from` and `through` bounds include both ends; `area` matches the site's exact ZIP. Completion removes only that entitlement, even if visits finish out of order. Booking, cancellation, term expiry and ending an agreement do not silently remove outstanding work. New included jobs keep `agreementVisitIndex`; older jobs take the first unclaimed ordinal in stored order. An existing job is retained when its booking is cancelled, so the desk can rebook it. Dispatch offers consecutive-slot batches through the existing `job.create` and `visit.book` doors, with their current authority and conflicts: each success and refusal is shown, and a refused or interrupted booking remains due. The Office may now book only jobs linked to an agreement under the approved booking semantics below. Today counts outstanding due visits, including those booked, and the owner morning brief links each one to its customer's agreement and Dispatch.

*Semantics (PLAN-004 declines, 20 October 2026):* a decline is the customer's own explicit answer, recorded by a person as `GLOVE-04` records a quote's answer: the desk (owner, dispatcher, office) records it for one included visit of an agreement (`agreement.decline`: agreement version, zero-based ordinal, who declined, how — phone, email reply, text reply or in person — and their reason). Glove never infers a decline from silence, a cancelled booking, term expiry or `agreement.end`. It is refused for a completed or already declined visit, and while that visit has a live booking (cancel the booking first, under `GLOVE-07`). The decline keeps the visit's due date, the recorder's name and role and the time, is appended to the agreement (never edited), is named on the Activity timeline (`agreement.visitDeclined`) and travels in the export. A declined visit leaves the due list, the `REPORT-001` agreements-due tile and the `AGENT-008` brief; it is no longer "left" under `PLAN-002`, and no included job can be made for it. The decline joins the site's property history (`CUST-001`) as an `agreement decline` row. Customer links and the Office booking authority follow the approved semantics below.

*Semantics (PLAN-004 booking links, ct ruling 20 October 2026 18:20Z):* the desk offers 1 to 10 future, currently conflict-free slots for one outstanding included visit (`agreement.offer`), retaining or creating its included job. The revocable bearer link is shown once, stores only its hash separately from portable domain records, expires exactly 14 days after creation, and is revoked atomically when used or by staff (`agreement.offerRevoke`). Like FIELD-003, restoring an archive retains offer metadata but requires a new link; old bearers are not restored. The customer page offers only that included visit's service, site and slots, with each slot's booking time zone; it grants no account access. The customer chooses one offered slot, under the offering person's current active role and branch authority. Every choice repeats `GLOVE-05` eligibility and overlap checks; the public choice never acknowledges new conflicts. A slot taken meanwhile is refused, showing only remaining open offers without other customers' conflict details. No open offers means contact the office; the visit stays due. The Office may `visit.book` only a job with an `agreementId` linking that job's customer and site, with the normal exact-conflict review, and may neither `visit.change` nor `visit.cancel`; the dispatcher keeps every other booking. Direct HTTP calls enforce these restrictions; Office workflow rules retain API-005’s existing prohibition on automatic booking. Created, used, revoked and staff-recorded outside-send events name the person and time in Activity and the business export, without the bearer. `agreement.offer` with `email: true` emails the link through `COMM-001` to the customer's own email address on record (the link is still shown once in the response), and the offer shows its provider-reported delivery state; `agreement.offerSent` still records a send staff made outside Glove, labelled with the method and recorder and “delivery not verified”, with no delivery claimed. The offer, its outside-send record and its use or revocation are `booking link` rows in the site's property history (`CUST-001`); an emailed link also appears there as a `message` row with its provider-reported state (`COMM-007`).

*Status:* done — evidence `src/glove/agreement-due.ts`, `src/glove/agreements.ts`, `src/glove/booking-offers.ts`, `src/glove/public-booking.ts`, `src/glove/access.ts`, `src/glove/commands/dispatch.ts`, `src/ui/due-visits.tsx`, `src/ui/booking-offers.tsx`, `src/ui/customer-booking.tsx`, `src/ui/decline-visit.tsx`, `src/glove/mail.ts`, `tests/api/agreements.test.mjs`, `tests/api/security.test.mjs`, `tests/browser/due-visits.spec.ts`, `tests/portable.mjs`, `src/glove/history.ts`.

#### PLAN-005 — Invoicing rules for recurring jobs.
Every recurring job (`PLAN-001`) has an invoicing rule, set when it is created and changeable from a date forward: per visit (each completed visit's lines and materials invoice on completion, or in a daily batch), a fixed price per period (the same amount on the last day of each month, or after each visit, whatever the visit count), once on close, or as needed (a reminder to the office, no draft). Glove generates the invoice drafts or reminders on that schedule and lists any recurring job whose billing date passed without an invoice. Autopay, receipts and failed-charge dunning are carried by `PAY-008` and `PLAN-003` under the ct carve of 22 October 2026 22:10Z. A skipped or canceled visit never bills a per-visit job; a fixed-price job bills whether or not its visits ran unless the owner pauses it. Drafts are approved under `GLOVE-09` unless the owner auto-approves a series' fixed invoices.

*Acceptance:* a flow test runs two series for three months: a per-visit series with one visit in month 1, two in month 2 (a third skipped) and none in month 3, and a quarterly commercial PM job billed $240.00 fixed monthly; it checks exactly three per-visit invoices with each visit's lines, exactly three $240.00 invoices dated the last day of each month; an on-close series invoices once when the job closes.

*Semantics (PLAN-005 draft/reminder slice, 22 October 2026; monthly dates under ct 20 October 17:20Z):* the signed-in owner sets a billing rule with a series at creation/change or `series.billing {jobId,version,from,billing,reason}`. Its numbered revisions apply from a current/future local date and retain earlier billing. Modes are per-visit, fixed-monthly, fixed-after-visit, on-close and as-needed (one month-end office reminder, no invoice). Fixed rules keep their explicit priced lines; per-visit drafts carry only that visit's work/materials and unbilled visit expenses. Skipped/canceled visits do not bill; paused periods do not bill; monthly fixed billing does not depend on whether visits ran. Stable job/visit/month/close identities commit with the invoice or reminder, so replay, concurrent commands and cron runs cannot duplicate them. A capture already claimed by one invoice is not billed again after a forward rule change. The Worker cron and ordinary mutations generate due drafts; opening state is read-only. The office can run `series.billingRun` and reads `/api/glove/recurring-billing` for dates without an invoice/reminder and the concrete missing-price/PO problem. Existing series retain their old job-wide workflow until the owner explicitly configures a rule.

The owner may explicitly authorize automatic approval only for a rule's fixed invoices. The pass rechecks the configuring owner's current active Owner role and branch scope at commit; no other person's rule or a later edited draft borrows that grant. Otherwise normal GLOVE-09 approval applies. Drafts say “Proposed — not approved, not sent” and count nowhere in aging, revenue, tax, exports or QuickBooks. For newly generated fixed-monthly invoices only, approval on/after period end freezes issueDate at that period end, records approvalAt and approver separately, and freezes dueDate = max(periodEnd, approvalLocalDate) + terms. Late approvals are labelled; aging begins only after approval and ages from that frozen due date. Revenue/tax/CSV/QuickBooks use the frozen issue date. Terms changes, correction and later export do not move those dates; preexisting documents and outputs are unchanged. No charge, receipt or dunning is added by this slice; PAY-008, PLAN-003 and COMM-001 carry those remaining acceptance obligations before autopay ships.

*Status:* done — evidence `tests/api/recurring-billing.test.mjs` › “PLAN-005 three months produce exactly three per-visit drafts with their own materials and three fixed monthly drafts, with skips and concurrent replay” (three-month drafts, material scope, skips, concurrency/cron and no draft accounting); › “PLAN-005 monthly approval keeps period-end issuance, later approval-based due date and historical corrections across aging, tax, CSV and QuickBooks” (ct date rules and historical outputs); › “PLAN-005 forward prices, pauses, fixed after-visit auto-approval, on-close, as-needed and missed price review preserve exactly-once evidence” (forward rules, all modes and missed dates); `tests/browser/recurring-billing.spec.ts` › “PLAN-005 owner reviews fixed billing, changes a future price, approves a proposed monthly invoice late and pauses future billing at 1440/390”.


### Stock and purchasing

#### STOCK-001 — Van and warehouse stock.
Each van and warehouse holds stock of price-book materials with counts; materials used on a visit come off that technician's van; transfers and restocks are recorded; low stock against a par level shows on the van; the day's jobs list the parts they need and whether the van has them.

*Acceptance:* a flow test sets van stock, records materials used on two visits, transfers from the warehouse, and checks counts, a low-stock flag and the parts-on-board check for tomorrow's jobs.

*Semantics (STOCK-001, 19 October 2026):* the owner and office keep stock locations (a van per technician, and warehouses) holding price-book materials only, counted in the material's unit to a thousandth; par levels are set per location and material. A stocktake sets a count, a restock adds, a transfer moves between two locations and is refused when the source holds less; a material from the price book that a technician records on a visit (`GLOVE-06`, so also offline) comes off that technician's van, and a count may go below zero, shown as such. Every movement is a stock-move record with who, when, the change and the count after, and a timeline entry. An item is low when its count is at or below its par. The parts a job needs are its lines' materials, and a task line's material contents, at their quantity times the line's quantity; the day's parts check walks one technician's visits on a local date in start order, and a part is on board for a job when the van holds enough for that job and every earlier job that day. A technician reads only their own van and their own day.

*Status:* done — evidence `src/glove/stock.ts`, `src/glove/commands/stock.ts`, `src/glove/commands/field.ts`, `src/ui/stock.tsx`, `tests/api/stock.test.mjs`, `tests/browser/journey.spec.ts`.

#### STOCK-002 — Purchase orders to suppliers.
The office raises purchase orders to suppliers (by email or supplier portal), receives them fully or partly into a van or warehouse, and links job-specific orders to the job's cost.

*Acceptance:* a flow test raises a PO for a job, receives it in two parts, and checks stock and job cost.

*Semantics (STOCK-002, 19 October 2026):* the owner and office raise a purchase order (numbered PO-0001 on) to a named supplier (with an optional email) of 1 to 50 price-book materials, each at a quantity to a thousandth of its unit and the supplier's unit cost (the item's price-book cost unless given), delivered to one van or warehouse (`STOCK-001`), for stock or for one job; it changes only while a draft. A draft order goes to the supplier in one of two ways, and the record and timeline say which: the owner or office emails it from Glove to the order's supplier address, or another address the person names, through the owner's connected email (`COMM-001` `email.send` with kind `purchaseOrder`; the order then reads sent, naming the email, with the provider's reported outcome, where delivered means only that the supplier's mail server accepted it), or the person sends it outside Glove by email, the supplier's portal, phone or in person and records that method with the supplier's reference, which is recorded as sent with no delivery claim. Glove has no supplier-portal connection. Only a sent order is received, in one or more parts, each line at most what is still due; each part adds to the delivery location's stock as a stock move naming the order (and its job), and the order reads partly received until every line arrives. A job's received lines count to its actual cost (`COST-004`) at the order's unit cost; a price-book material recorded on that job counts at its price-book cost only beyond the quantity the job's orders received of it, so nothing is costed twice. A job order shows on the property history (`CUST-001`). Unit costs are margin figures (`MONEY-003`): purchase orders are read only by roles that read reports, never by technicians.

*Status:* done — evidence `src/glove/commands/purchasing.ts`, `src/glove/costing.ts`, `src/ui/purchasing.tsx`, `tests/api/purchasing.test.mjs`, `src/glove/api.ts`, `src/glove/storage.ts`, `src/glove/access.ts`, `src/glove/view.ts`.

### Team and permissions

#### TEAM-001 — Custom roles and permissions.
The owner builds roles from named permissions (for example a CSR who books and takes card payments under `PAY-009` but cannot see margins or refund, a lead technician who can approve field quotes), each enforced by every command handler and read door as the built-in roles are.

*Acceptance:* the security matrix in `tests/api/security.test.mjs` extended with a custom CSR role: every command it lacks is refused through every door.

*Status:* done — evidence `src/glove/access.ts`, `src/glove/commands/setup.ts`, `src/ui/custom-roles.tsx`, `tests/api/security.test.mjs`, `tests/api/purchasing.test.mjs`, `src/glove/commands/purchasing.ts`, `src/glove/api.ts`, `src/glove/storage.ts`, `src/glove/view.ts`.

#### TEAM-002 — Branches.
A business with several locations keeps branches with their own time zone, technicians, service areas, tax settings, numbering and reports, under one owner; people can be limited to their branch. Each branch carries its revenue rows and a branch filter in the `REPORT-002` reports.

*Acceptance:* a flow test sets two branches in different time zones, books in each, and checks a branch-limited dispatcher sees only theirs; and the `REPORT-002` revenue report by branch, filtered to one branch, narrows to that branch's invoices.

*Semantics (TEAM-002, 19 October 2026; ct approved 12:43Z):* the owner creates branches with an IANA booking zone, a unique alphanumeric number prefix and service ZIP codes. New sites choose their branch and keep it; the first site assigns its customer to that branch, and all of that customer’s sites stay in it. Technicians belong to a branch; crews never mix branches. Quotes, service calls, assessments and their jobs and invoices inherit the site’s branch, with independent per-kind counters (for example RAL-J-1001 and RAL-INV-0001). The prefix is fixed after the first number. Legacy sites, technicians and documents remain in the main business branch with their original numbers and business zone. A visit records its booking zone and exact instants; later branch-zone edits affect new bookings and moves, never rewrite existing visits. A technician must belong to the job’s branch; the existing area warning/refusal rule also applies to its branch ZIP codes. The Dispatch branch choice shows its own technicians, work and local zone. Actual per-branch US tax settings and the REPORT-002 tax-jurisdiction rows ship with MONEY-001, which carries them in its text and acceptance. Revenue reports include branch rows and a branch filter in JSON and CSV, dating each invoice by its branch’s local first-approval date (both period ends included). The owner limits a person to a named branch or the main business branch, or removes that limit. The installation owner retains all-branch access. Limits apply to every current read and command door, including files, Activity, history, reports, business/CSV exports, saved command outcomes and workflow actions under their creator’s live scope; a changed limit is rechecked in the commit guard. Scoped people read and change only operational records rooted in their branch; shared business reference data remains readable under their existing role and is read-only for them. New operational records created by a scoped person belong to their branch. Customers cannot merge across branches. Every existing report accepts the branch filter in JSON and CSV; future record kinds, read doors and reports inherit the same scope before shipping (API-002/003, COMM-001..009, PAY-001..009, LEDGER-001..004, PLAN-002..005, MOVE-001..006).

*Status:* done — evidence `src/glove/branches.ts`, `src/glove/branch-access.ts`, `src/glove/access.ts`, `src/glove/api.ts`, `src/glove/reports.ts`, `src/glove/commands/branches.ts`, `src/ui/branches.tsx`, `src/ui/team.tsx`, `tests/api/branches.test.mjs`, `tests/browser/branches.spec.ts`.

#### TEAM-003 — Technician commissions and bonuses.
The owner sets commission rules (percentage of sold or completed revenue, per-agreement spiffs) and Glove computes each technician's earnings per pay period for the payroll export, alongside each technician's tips (`PAY-002`).

*Acceptance:* a flow test applies a 5% sold-revenue rule and a $25 agreement spiff over a period and checks the totals in the payroll export.

*Semantics (TEAM-003, 21 October 2026):* the owner alone (acting for "admin") sets commission rules in payroll settings: a percent of sold revenue, a percent of completed revenue and a fixed spiff per agreement sold, each optional (percents from 0 to 99.99 with up to two decimals; the spiff in USD). Each save is a rule version applying from a local date (today unless the owner gives one), replacing any version from that same date; a credited sale takes the version in force on its local date in the business time zone, so a later change never alters earlier earnings. Rules apply to every technician. Sold revenue is an accepted quote's accepted total before tax (the chosen option and add-ons after any `QUOTE-007` discount), credited to the technician the desk names as its seller when recording the acceptance, on the local date the acceptance was recorded; a quote accepted with no seller credits nobody. Completed revenue is `REPORT-002`'s technician revenue: an invoice's latest approved revision before tax, split equally among the technicians who completed the job's visits (the cent remainder to the first by name), on the local date of its first approval. A spiff is credited to the technician the desk names when selling a `PLAN-002` agreement, on the local date it was sold. Each credited sale's commission is its base × percent, rounded half up to the cent; totals are sums of those amounts. Earnings are computed for the pay period when the payroll files are read, and the owner's timesheet approval covers hours only. They join `COST-002`'s payroll files for the same people: Gusto's `commission` column on each workweek's row (a workweek with commissions but no time gets its own row with zero hours), one ADP RUN row per person with the owner's commission earnings code (default `COMM`, set to the code the owner's RUN earnings report shows), blank Pay Hours and the total in Dollars, and one QuickBooks Payroll `Commission` line per person and local date with the amount in an Amount column. A technician with commissions but no approved time in the period is left out of the files with that reason; a 1099 subcontractor is never in them. The payroll JSON gives each person's sold, completed and agreement amounts and every credited sale. Commission columns, like the hour columns, are not confirmed against a live Gusto, ADP RUN or QuickBooks account; Glove pays no one. Inheritance: technician tips do not exist today; `PAY-002` adds each technician's tips to the same files for the same approved weeks before it ships, and `QUOTE-006` field quotes and `COMM-006` hub sales credit their selling technician under these rules before they ship.

*Status:* done — evidence `src/glove/commissions.ts`, `src/glove/payroll.ts`, `src/ui/timesheets.tsx`, `tests/api/payroll.test.mjs`.

### Reporting

#### REPORT-001 — Owner dashboard.
The owner's home shows today's jobs and their state, technicians on the clock, revenue booked and paid today, open quotes, unbilled completed jobs, receivables and agreements due.

*Semantics (REPORT-001, 19 October 2026):* the owner's home is Today, read from `GET /api/glove/dashboard` by every role that reads reports (never a technician). "Today" is the business's local date in its timezone. Today's jobs are the jobs with a visit, not canceled, starting on that date, each with the job's own state (open, completed, invoiced). Booked today sums the accepted total, tax included (the chosen option and add-ons under `QUOTE-001`), of each quote whose acceptance was recorded that date. Open quotes are draft, approved and sent quotes, valued at their latest revision's first option's total. Completed, not invoiced counts completed jobs whose invoice has not been exported. On the clock lists the technicians with a shift clocked in and not out (`COST-001`). The other tiles ship with the clauses that keep their records, each carrying its tile in its text and acceptance: paid today with `PAY-002`, receivables with `PAY-004`, agreements due with `PLAN-002` and `PLAN-004`; until a tile's clause ships, Today shows no such tile rather than a zero.

*Acceptance:* a browser journey with a fixture day checks every figure against the records it summarizes (today's tiles; each later tile is checked by its own clause's acceptance).

PLAN-005 fixed-monthly invoices inherit its ct-approved period-end issue date, separate approval time, later-of-period/approval due-date basis, approval-gated aging and frozen report/export dates; preexisting documents keep their original basis. This is proven with PLAN-005’s named monthly-date flow before that capability ships.

*Status:* done — evidence `src/glove/dashboard.ts`, `src/glove/api.ts`, `src/ui/today.tsx`, `tests/browser/journey.spec.ts`, `tests/api/security.test.mjs`. The on-the-clock tile shipped with `COST-001`, and the agreements-due tile with `PLAN-002` and `PLAN-004`: it counts each included visit due on or before the business's local today, booked or not and after term end, until it is completed or the customer's decline is recorded (`src/glove/agreement-due.ts`, `tests/api/agreements.test.mjs`, `tests/browser/due-visits.spec.ts`). The paid-today and receivables tiles arrive with `PAY-002` and `PAY-004`; until then Today shows no such tile.

#### REPORT-002 — Job, revenue, technician and lead reports.
Reports for revenue by period, job type and branch; technician revenue, close rate, callbacks and hours; lead source conversion; quote win rate; agreement count and churn; job margin (`COST-004`); and sales tax collected by jurisdiction and period, so the owner can file between accounting syncs; each filterable and exportable as CSV.

*Acceptance:* a flow test builds a fixture month and checks each report's totals against the records and the CSV export; one filter per report (period, branch, technician, job type, source) narrows its totals correctly, and the sales-tax-collected report matches the invoices' tax by jurisdiction to the cent.

*Semantics (REPORT-002, 19 October 2026):* every report takes a period of local dates in the business time zone, both ends included, and one filter, and answers as JSON or as a CSV of the same figures, read by the roles that read reports. Revenue is an invoice's latest person-approved revision subtotal before tax, counted once and dated by the invoice's first approval; the revenue report gives it by month and by job type (the category of the job's first price-book line, else Uncategorized, as in `COST-004`), filtered by job type. The technician report gives each technician's revenue (each such job's revenue split equally among the technicians who completed its visits, the cent remainder to the first by name), visits completed in the period, callbacks (a completed visit followed within 30 days by a new job at the same site) and their rate, and hours (their clocked shifts that began in the period, to a tenth of an hour), filtered by technician. Lead-source conversion counts the requests made in the period by source (`BOOK-003`'s figures), filtered by source; the quote win rate is quotes accepted ÷ quotes answered (accepted or declined) in the period, with the won value, filtered by the request's source; job margins are `COST-004`'s period roll-up, filtered by job type. The sales-tax report sums the tax on approved invoices by month in one row per branch and jurisdiction, from each invoice's frozen `MONEY-001` tax breakdown (an invoice approved before `MONEY-001`, which has no breakdown, reports its tax under the business's former single jurisdiction), and says its basis: tax on approved invoices, because Glove records no payments yet. Percentages are to one decimal, rounded half up. The branch filter (`TEAM-002`) narrows every report to one branch's records and the revenue report also gives revenue by branch; the agreements report is `PLAN-002`'s (agreements sold in the period by plan with their value, those active on the period's last day, and churn). Technician close rate from field quotes (`QUOTE-006`) and tax collected from payments (`PAY-002`) come with those clauses, which carry them in their text and acceptance.

PLAN-005 fixed-monthly invoices inherit its ct-approved period-end issue date, separate approval time, later-of-period/approval due-date basis, approval-gated aging and frozen report/export dates; preexisting documents keep their original basis. This is proven with PLAN-005’s named monthly-date flow before that capability ships.

*Status:* done — evidence `tests/browser/journey.spec.ts`, `src/glove/reports.ts`, `src/glove/api.ts`, `src/ui/reports.tsx`, `tests/api/reports.test.mjs`, `src/glove/costing.ts`.

#### REPORT-003 — Receivables aging.
An aging report (current, 1–30, 31–60, 61–90, over 90 days) by customer with links to each open invoice and its reminder state.

*Semantics (20 October 2026, ct approved 23:58Z, pattern 1):* the first human approval freezes the invoice's issue date in its branch's local zone, business payment terms in calendar days and due date. Export, corrections and later business settings never move these dates. Legacy invoices without retained terms use their first approval date plus current business terms, labelled "due date computed from first approval and current terms". The report uses the latest approved revision on or before its chosen `asOf` local date; an unapproved draft or correction contributes nothing new. With no payments recorded yet, a positive approved total is the open balance. Each customer's invoices and total are split into current (due today or later), 1–30, 31–60, 61–90 and over 90 calendar days past due, boundaries inclusive, in exact cents and separately by currency; every invoice links to its normal invoice screen. The report states "No payments recorded yet" and "No reminders scheduled". PAY-002 and PAY-004 inherit confirmed payment, allocation, credit and refund effects on these balances, and PAY-005 inherits actual reminder state, each in its text and acceptance before shipping. The existing reports permission and branch filters apply to JSON and CSV alike. PAY-dependent homepage claims stay partial.

*Acceptance:* `tests/api/reports.test.mjs` › `REPORT-003 aging …` creates invoices at every bucket boundary, checks exact customer/currency totals and every invoice link, frozen dates through terms edits, export and approved corrections, unapproved corrections excluded, honest reminder/payment labels, JSON/CSV agreement, invalid dates and role/branch refusal. `tests/browser/receivables.spec.ts` opens the report and follows an exact invoice at 1440/390. PAY-002/004 assert payment/credit/refund effects and PAY-005 the actual reminder state in this report before shipping.

PLAN-005 fixed-monthly invoices inherit its ct-approved period-end issue date, separate approval time, later-of-period/approval due-date basis, approval-gated aging and frozen report/export dates; preexisting documents keep their original basis. This is proven with PLAN-005’s named monthly-date flow before that capability ships.
