### Move-in from other systems

#### MOVE-001 — Move in from the client-and-property source system.
The owner moves in from the client-and-property source system (clients, properties, contacts, requests, quotes, jobs and visits, recurring jobs, invoices with open balances, the price book of products and services, notes and attachments) through the source API with the owner's own access, or its CSV exports (including the products and services export), under `GLOVE-11`'s dry run, journal and undo; source IDs are kept; what does not map is listed.

*Acceptance:* a flow test previews and commits a documented-shape Jobber fixture, checks open balances, recurring visits and the price book from the products and services CSV arrive, and undoes it; the same journey on a real owner export is recorded when one is available.

*Semantics (24 October 2026):* imported source quotes retain their source status, terms and cent-reconciled lines as read-only history; a source approval creates no Glove approval, signature, send or new job. New customer decisions use a new native quote. Imported invoice lines reconcile with the source amounts and balance report, including document discounts; every job belongs to the same source client, and a multi-job balance counts once. Closed source jobs stay archived. Job-owned notes retain source time, and downloaded job attachments retain exact bytes without fetching source URLs. An unscheduled source visit is explicit pending history on its job, with no invented time or capacity reservation; the dispatcher books native work explicitly. Source recurrence definitions stay visible but inactive until an exact rule mapping exists. Unsupported shapes remain named in preview. No acceptance point is added.

*Status:* partial — evidence `tests/api/movein.test.mjs` › “MOVE-001 Jobber saved responses and product CSV preview, retain recurring visits, repeat without duplicates and undo while keeping native work” (source relationships, prices and balances, exact visits and unscheduled work, inert recurrence, reconciled priced and text-only quote rows, job and non-job notes with source timestamps, inherited-note deduplication, retained original bytes and disclosed missing files, refusal of incomplete or foreign ownership, technician privacy, customer export and erasure, repeat and guarded undo); `tests/browser/movein.spec.ts` › “MOVE-004 fieldpulse, MOVE-001 Jobber and MOVE-002 Housecall Pro: owner previews, commits, reads the imported work and balance, and undoes at 1440 and 390” (Jobber preview and committed customer, request, quote and invoice source notes, originals and balances, undo); `tests/portable.mjs` (exact source records and originals restore, repeat and undo). Gap: separate contact-person mapping, selected optional quote items and future recurring-rule mapping need a current version-labelled Jobber child schema or owner export that this installation does not hold. Public parent references do not define ContactModel fields, optional selection encoding or calendarRule encoding; authenticated GraphiQL access is required. Source recurrence remains inert and unsupported optional priced lines are refused, without invented selections. A real owner export is reconciled when available.

#### MOVE-002 — Move in from the service-plan source system.
The same as `MOVE-001` for the service-plan source system, including service plans and price book.

*Acceptance:* the `MOVE-001` flow against a documented-shape Housecall Pro fixture.

*Semantics:* saved Housecall Pro public API responses use the existing owner-only dry run, reviewed fingerprint, source journal and guarded Undo. Price-book monetary integers are cents; invoice quantities are hundredths. Imported invoices retain dated remaining balances and cannot become new sales. Existing source visits arrive without starting future schedules. Source roles grant no sign-in. Unknown fields and unsupported rules are named; nothing sends or charges. Housecall Pro leads retain source identity, customer and optional site, source and pipeline labels, and open, won or lost state; unknown source stage times stay unknown. Invoice discounts are non-negative source cents represented as negative lines, with each item amount, gross subtotal, discounts, tax and final total reconciled before import.

*Status:* partial — evidence `tests/api/movein.test.mjs` › MOVE-002 Housecall Pro saved responses preview prices, balances and recurring visits, repeat unchanged and undo without removing native work; `tests/browser/movein.spec.ts` › MOVE-004 fieldpulse, MOVE-001 Jobber and MOVE-002 Housecall Pro: owner previews, commits, reads the imported work and balance, and undoes at 1440 and 390. Gap: service plans need a documented source export or read shape with plan identity, terms and included services; estimates, separate contacts and future recurring-rule mapping remain; real owner-export proof is recorded when available.

#### MOVE-003 — Move in from the location-and-membership source system.
The same as `MOVE-001` for the location-and-membership source system, including locations, equipment, memberships and their recurring services.

*Acceptance:* the `MOVE-001` flow against a documented-shape ServiceTitan fixture.

*Semantics (22 October 2026):* the owner loads saved V2 response files from the location-and-membership source system and downloaded attachments through the existing move-in dry run, reviewed fingerprint, journal and guarded undo. Source IDs, locations, contacts, requests, quotes, jobs, appointments and technician assignments, service and material price-book entries, notes, attachment bytes, equipment and warranties remain linked. Invoice remaining balances are dated opening balances, never a new sale or an approved invoice to repost. One source membership remains one agreement, including ongoing or multiple-location terms; each source recurring-service event keeps its own site, template SKUs, quantities, exact due date and completed or source-closed state. Its source billing template is labelled separately from charge permission, and unavailable member benefits are named. Monthly rules with one day and weekly rules with one weekday matching their anchor map to inactive native proposals; a person explicitly chooses a technician and times before activating a mapped rule strictly after exported events, with its original end date. Nothing is scheduled, emailed or charged by importing. A shape without an exact native rule, an unknown field, missing bytes or a rejected parent is named in the preview and its source is retained, as `MOVE-001` requires. `MOVE-005` retains carried QuickBooks references and the cutover checklist; `MOVE-006` retains saved methods, autopay and processor authorization; a real owner-export journey is recorded when available (`MOVE-001`).

*Status:* done — evidence `tests/api/movein.test.mjs` › `MOVE-003 servicetitan: saved exports preview, preserve source records, prices, balances, visits, notes and attachment bytes, repeat once, and undo without touching native work` (dry run, source identities and graph, balances, service and material prices, visits, notes, exact original bytes, unchanged repeat, native work preserved by undo), `MOVE-003 servicetitan: missing parents and invalid amounts are explained in preview; a native edit prevents destructive undo` (rejections and edited-record undo refusal), `MOVE-003 an ongoing multi-location membership keeps one identity and offers each outstanding source event only at its own site` (one membership, per-site and per-service entitlements and Office booking), `MOVE-003 source recurrence activates only after explicit review, never replays exported events, and unsupported rules are named` (mapped rule, no source-event replay, guarded native activation and unsupported-rule disclosure); `tests/browser/movein.spec.ts` › `MOVE-003 servicetitan: owner previews, commits, reads the imported work and balance, and undoes at 1440 and 390`; `tests/portable.mjs` › source records, balances, membership entitlements and original custody restore exactly and undo through the normal command door. No real owner export was available; the documented-shape fixture is synthetic.

#### MOVE-004 — Move in from the recurring-report source system and the additional job source system.
The same as `MOVE-001` for the recurring-report source system and the additional job source system.

*Acceptance:* the `MOVE-001` flow against documented-shape fixtures for each.

*Semantics (22 October 2026):* FieldPulse saved job responses may be accompanied by its documented Recurring Jobs CSV report. The report's Recurring Job ID joins only exact `recurring_parent_id` values, never customer names; conflicting or absent customer/site parents are rejected. One definition remains one inactive job proposal with source frequency, anchor and end condition retained. The report's last generated occurrence and every exported occurrence, including later exceptions, bound explicit activation under the existing `series.create` guard. Exactly matching weekly weekday wording maps to a native weekly proposal; other frequencies are named as unmapped and retained without guessing (FieldPulse's absent-month-date skip must not become Glove's last-day clamp). A person must choose a technician and times and explicitly confirm activation after all exported work, retaining the source end condition. No import schedules, charges or sends anything. Workiz remains partial until its future-rule source encoding is available. MOVE-005 retains accounting references, agreements/equipment and cutover; MOVE-006 retains saved methods, autopay and processor authorization.

*Status:* partial — evidence `tests/api/movein.test.mjs` › `MOVE-004 fieldpulse: saved exports preview, preserve source records, prices, balances, visits, notes and attachment bytes, repeat once, and undo without touching native work`, `MOVE-004 workiz: saved exports preview, preserve source records, prices, balances, visits, notes and attachment bytes, repeat once, and undo without touching native work` and the corresponding missing-parent/native-edit refusal flows; `tests/browser/movein.spec.ts` › provider move-in journeys at 1440 and 390; `tests/portable.mjs` › source records and custody restore and undo. `tests/api/movein.test.mjs` › `MOVE-004 FieldPulse recurring report retains one schedule, requires explicit activation after all exported work, and names unsupported definitions` proves the documented recurring-report import, one inactive group proposal, source frequency/anchor/end and last-generated watermark, existing-exception no-replay, explicit native activation, missing-parent rejection and unsupported-rule disclosure. Gap: Workiz future recurring definitions still need a provider-documented export/read shape containing identity, frequency, anchor and termination. Its public job schemas and documented reports do not provide that encoding. Existing Workiz occurrences arrive unchanged; importing generates no new bookings.

#### MOVE-005 — Open balances, agreements and equipment carried over.
Every kit carries open invoice balances (as opening balances that `LEDGER-*` does not post twice), every accounting reference the source kept (the QuickBooks Online customer, item and invoice IDs its sync wrote, used by `LEDGER-004` to link rather than re-post), active agreements with remaining entitlements and renewal dates, installed equipment, and saved cards, bank accounts and autopay authorizations (`MOVE-006`); a guided cutover checklist tells the owner what to switch off in the old system and when, including the payment processor's export request and its freeze window.

*Acceptance:* a flow test moves in a fixture with open balances and an active agreement, checks balances and entitlements and the carried QuickBooks IDs, and checks the QuickBooks fake receives no duplicate invoice; the cutover checklist lists the processor export request and the freeze window.

*Status:* partial — evidence `tests/api/movein.test.mjs` › ServiceM8 opening balances retain payment custody, reject mismatches, and preserve native edits on undo; `tests/browser/movein.spec.ts` › MOVE-004 fieldpulse, MOVE-001 Jobber and MOVE-002 Housecall Pro (includes the ServiceM8 owner flow at 1440 and 390); `tests/api/movein.test.mjs` › MOVE-003 ServiceTitan saved exports and ongoing multi-location membership; MOVE-004 FieldPulse and Workiz saved exports. The Workiz, ServiceTitan and Housecall Pro saved-export flows additionally prove original invoice-number preservation, exact-total existing-invoice matching and confirmed linking, refusal of a same-number invoice with the wrong total, no provider writes on repeated opening-invoice sync, and original-number custody in the business export. Gap: ServiceTitan carries dated opening balances (not reposted), one agreement per membership with per-event entitlements, and equipment with warranties; FieldPulse, Workiz, Jobber and Housecall Pro carry opening balances. ServiceM8 now reconciles completed sent invoices against active source payments into dated opening balances, retains rejected-job payment custody for customer export and erasure, and proves the owner journey with unconditional phone overflow checks. No kit carries documented source QuickBooks customer, item and invoice IDs for `LEDGER-004` linking, so the carried-ID no-duplicate assertion remains unproved. ServiceM8 agreements, equipment, original due-date mapping and balance-specific restore proof remain unproved, as do agreement and equipment coverage for the other kits beyond ServiceTitan. The owner’s Move in screen and `IMPORT.md` now provide the cutover guide: secure processor export request, the export-to-import freeze window and recollection of intervening card updates, old-system shutdown, final dated reconciliation, confirmed accounting links, and held renewals without a verified method. Workiz serialId, ServiceTitan referenceNumber and Housecall Pro invoice_number remain separate from prefixed Glove display numbers, allowing existing QuickBooks invoices to be reviewed and linked without reposting. These document numbers are not QuickBooks record IDs. Source QuickBooks customer, item and invoice IDs still require a provider-documented export field and remain uncarried; carried-ID matching and its no-duplicate assertion remain unproved under LEDGER-004. The checked ServiceTitan catalog externalId identifies an original catalog item, not an accounting record, and must not be treated as a QuickBooks ID. Saved methods and authorizations remain with MOVE-006. No processor transfer or saved-method activation is claimed.

#### MOVE-006 — Saved cards and autopay carried over.
Each kit carries every moved-in customer's autopay: the agreements and recurring jobs that charged a saved card or bank account. Card and bank numbers move processor to processor, never through Glove: the `MOVE-005` cutover checklist tells the owner to request the old processor's export to Stripe (Stripe's PAN import, about 10 business days) and names the freeze window between export and import, in which card updates made in the old system are lost and must be collected again. When Stripe completes the import, the owner loads Stripe's mapping file (old customer and card IDs to new Stripe customer and payment-method IDs); Glove attaches each imported method to its moved-in customer and to the agreements or recurring jobs it paid, keeping the original authorization reference, consent date and source as the `PAY-008` authorization record. Glove lists every autopay agreement or recurring job with no migrated method, sends those customers a tracked card-update link (`COMM-001`, `COMM-002`) before their next charge, and shows who has updated. A renewal or recurring charge with no method is held as "needs payment method" with a reminder, never charged and never counted as a failed payment for dunning.

*Acceptance:* a flow test moves in a fixture with 10 autopay agreements, loads a Stripe-fake mapping file matching 7, and checks those 7 carry the imported method and original authorization reference and renew with a charge; the other 3 are listed with update links queued before their next charge date, their renewals charge nothing and enter no dunning, and one customer's update link saves a method that the next renewal charges; the cutover checklist shows the processor export request and the freeze window.

*Status:* missing — gap: no saved payment methods or autopay; the ServiceM8 kit carries no payment data (`src/glove/movein/servicem8.ts`).

### Integrations and API

#### API-001 — Documented HTTP API.
Every read and command people use is a documented HTTP door ([API.md](API.md)) that agents and integrations call under the same roles and checks.

*Acceptance:* the API flow suite drives every clause through these doors.

*Status:* done — evidence `API.md`, `tests/api/flows.test.mjs`, `tests/api/security.test.mjs`, `tests/api/subcontract.test.mjs`, `tests/api/push.test.mjs`, `tests/api/suggest.test.mjs`.

#### API-002 — Webhooks and integration tokens.
The owner issues scoped tokens for integrations, each acting as one role, revocable; webhooks fire signed events (request created, quote approved, visit booked, job completed, invoice paid) with retries and a delivery log.

*Acceptance:* a flow test issues a dispatcher-scoped token, is refused an owner command with it, and receives signed webhooks for a booked visit and a paid invoice, with a retried failed delivery.

*Semantics (API-002 tokens, 19 October 2026; ct approved 19:01Z):* only the owner issues (`token.issue {name, role, branchId?}`) and revokes (`token.revoke`) integration tokens. A token acts as one role: Dispatcher, Office, Agent, Viewer or one of the owner's custom roles, never Owner, Technician or Subcontractor (field roles act for a person on their own visits). Without `branchId` it reaches every branch; with a branch id, or `main` for the main business, it is limited like a branch-limited person (`TEAM-002`). The token (`glv_` and 43 base64url characters from 32 random bytes) appears once, in the issuing response; Glove keeps only its SHA-256, never in records, the saved command outcome a replay returns, Activity, exports or the portable archive (restored installations issue new tokens). A request with `Authorization: Bearer <token>` acts as that role through every read and command door, without the same-origin check sessions need. Each request checks the token is unrevoked, and each commit re-checks in its atomic batch that the token is unrevoked, still holds its role, and that a custom role's permissions are unchanged; a revoked token is refused (401) from its next request. A token never manages tokens, roles, role assignments, agent grants, branch limits, provider keys or calendar addresses, whatever its role. What a token changes is in Activity under "<name> (integration token)" with its role; the owner's token list shows each token's role, branch, issue time, last use (every request) and revocation, never the token or its hash. A workflow rule a token created runs as that token's current role and branch, and is logged "not allowed" once the token is revoked. Signed webhooks, the other half of this clause, are not built yet: the delivery host must be checked on its resolved address with no DNS-rebinding gap, which waits on the Base egress door (gr-base-285); `invoice.paid` is carried by `PAY-002`/`PAY-004`.

*Status:* partial — evidence `src/glove/tokens.ts`, `src/glove/access.ts`, `src/ui/integration-tokens.tsx`, `tests/api/tokens.test.mjs`, `tests/api/security.test.mjs`; gap: signed webhooks (owner-only, id-only payloads, Glove-Signature t/v1, commit-time outbox, 5 retries then failed, delivery log and redeliver) wait on the Base egress door with the resolved-address private-range check (gr-base-285).

#### API-003 — Zapier and Crank apps.
Published Zapier and Crank apps expose Glove's triggers and actions (create request, create customer, new paid invoice) for cross-app automation.

*Acceptance:* each app's own test suite runs its triggers and actions against a running installation.

*Status:* missing — gap: no published apps.

#### API-004 — CompanyCam photos.
Photos taken in CompanyCam for a job's address appear on the Glove job, and Glove job photos can be sent to the CompanyCam project.

*Acceptance:* a flow test links a CompanyCam project (fake) and checks photos flow both ways once.

*Semantics (API-004, 19 October 2026; ct approved 12:43Z):* the owner saves or disconnects a CompanyCam Public API access token as an installation secret, excluded from state, exports, logs and archives. Saving it is labelled unverified. The owner or dispatcher links a job to an existing project by numeric id; its recorded address must match the site's address after case, whitespace and punctuation normalization, without claiming postal validation. Imports and exports are explicit actions on that job. Imports consume every cursor page, retain provider photo ids and capture times, and show original photos with captions labelled CompanyCam, never as technician arrival or sign-off. The existing Glove JPEG/PNG/WebP and 1.5 MB photo limits apply, with a visible error for every unsupported, oversized or unready photo; none is silently skipped. Repeating an import creates no second copy. A selected original Glove photo sends only its bytes and caption to that project; no customer identity, contacts, job prices or other fields are sent. A durable, current-authority-checked reservation precedes the upload. Its provider photo id and caption outcome are kept; repeating a send never sends that photo again. An uncertain upload is labelled “outcome unknown; check CompanyCam” and is never resent. The owner can reconcile it to an existing photo in the same project only after its original-byte SHA-256 matches; this confirms the photo without claiming any unconfirmed caption. Exported photos do not loop back into the job; a photo matching an uncertain upload is held with a visible reconciliation error. Link, import and export outcomes and provider ids are audited on the job; whole-business export and the portable archive retain references and upload reservations, never the token. Every command and read door checks the current role and job visibility.

*Status:* done — evidence `src/glove/commands/companycam.ts`, `src/glove/companycam/client.ts`, `src/glove/companycam/uploads.ts`, `src/ui/companycam.tsx`, `tests/api/companycam.test.mjs`, `tests/browser/companycam.spec.ts`.

#### API-005 — Workflow rules.
The owner sets rules of the form "when this happens, do that" on Glove's own events (when a job with the "commercial" tag completes, assign the invoice to a named office person; when a quote over $5,000 is approved, notify the owner; when a request comes from Angi, apply its lead cost), each with a run log and an on/off switch; a rule can do only what its creator's role can.

*Acceptance:* a flow test creates two rules, triggers each, checks the run log and effect, and checks a rule created by an office person cannot book a visit.

*Semantics (API-005, 19 October 2026):* the owner and the office make rules (`rule.save`); a rule's creator or the owner changes it or turns it off. A rule has a trigger among Glove's own events (a request created, a quote's price approved, a job created, a job completed), an optional condition (the request's source; the quote's or job's total over an amount, tax included) and one action: an in-app notice to a desk role or to one named person who holds a desk role , booking a job just created with a technician a number of days later at set times, or assigning a job just completed's invoice preparation to one named person who prepares invoices (the owner or an office person), shown on the job with a notice to them. After every command, each enabled rule whose trigger that command raised runs once, as its creator's current role checked at run time through the same command checks: an action that role cannot take is logged "not allowed" and does nothing, and a booking with a conflict is logged "refused" (a rule never confirms a conflict review). Every run is logged with the rule and its version, the triggering record, the outcome and why, and is shown with the rules on the Rules screen; what a rule does never triggers another rule. A customer-tag condition comes with `CUST-004`, and applying a lead cost (`leadCost`, on `enquiry.created`) with `BOOK-004`, which carry them in their text and acceptance.

*Status:* done — evidence `src/glove/rules.ts`, `src/glove/api.ts`, `src/ui/rules.tsx`, `tests/api/rules.test.mjs`.

### Data, privacy and operations

#### DATA-001 — Customer privacy requests and erasure.
On a customer's request the owner exports everything Glove holds about them, or erases their personal data (name, contacts, addresses, messages, recordings, photos of people), keeping invoices and payment records the business must retain for tax with the person's details removed; each request is recorded with its outcome.

MONEY-001 inheritance: erasure removes every private exemption-certificate original/chunk and its evidence metadata, and the restore fence prevents an older backup from reviving them; assert this through the customer erasure door before shipping.

*Acceptance:* a flow test exports one customer's data, erases them, checks every personal field and file is gone while invoice totals and tax records remain, and checks a restored older backup cannot bring them back.

*Semantics (DATA-001, 21 October 2026):* only a signed-in owner without branch limits, never an integration token or custom role, requests whole-customer export or explicitly confirms erasure. The request closes over every merged identity and its receipt lists every merged ID. Export includes related records, full timeline and base64 original files/certificates; shared CSV/ServiceM8 sources are projected to this customer’s complete source rows/objects, never peer records, with any mixed-ownership source omission explicitly named; its download is shown only in that response, never in saved command outcomes. Erasure removes personal operational records, attached originals and certificate chunks/evidence, customer-link credentials, delivery-recipient/event copies, personal timeline text and command results. Anonymous customer/site/job placeholders, invoice amounts/dates/classifications, job costs and tax totals remain; erased records cannot be edited or unmerged. The Base redaction checkpoint and transfer invalidation commit atomically with the erasure; the destination fence or a trusted later post-erasure backup supplied as the import journal refuses an older checkpoint. A fresh offline destination cannot discover an erasure without that trusted later journal; the owner is told to keep it.

*Shared import custody (DATA-001, ct ruling 21 October 2026):* an import source can hold one person's customer and site in one record and several people's originals in one artifact. Erasure first produces a preview bound to the customer group's current version. The preview gives counts per store and names: each import batch whose Undo/resume will be retired and why, each shared original artifact that will be withdrawn, and the number of peer customers whose source file becomes unavailable. Confirmation repeats the preview's fingerprint, and a stale preview is refused. The subject's records in every affected batch become zero-effect tombstones, so peers' Undo and resume keep working. A batch's whole Undo/source custody is retired only when the import kit cannot tombstone the record or a batch header holds the subject's exact value; its source is then labelled withdrawn. A withdrawn shared artifact is removed whole. Peers keep their per-record source payloads and their exports name the byte-exact file as an omission. Subject values are the group's exact typed identifiers (name, email, phone, address, contact name/phone/email, certificate number). Peer-owned records that mention them have only those values redacted and are never deleted. Rewritten identifiers are keyed or random, never a plain hash of a personal value. No send or remote deletion happens. At the next successful device refresh, value-free fences remove the earlier assignee's cached day, photo cache and queued captures for erased visits; losing an assignment alone never discards late evidence. A disconnected device cannot observe erasure until it reconnects. Only a signed-in owner without branch limits confirms today. A TEAM-001 privacy permission may later delegate the door. Agents may prepare a request for a person's decision under GLOVE-10/AGENT-009. A token or agent never confirms erasure. QUOTE-005 proves its snapshot/attachment erasure in the document flow; future MOVE-001..006, QUOTE-006, COMM-002/005/006/009, PAY-002/004/007/008/009 and LEDGER-001..004 inherit their additional personal stores before shipping. Outside copies and holds are DATA-005.

*Status:* done — evidence `tests/api/privacy.test.mjs` › “DATA-001 owner exports and erases every merged identity, original and personal copy while retaining anonymous invoice and tax totals” (merged owner-only export/erasure, private originals, tax totals, stale preview, typed identifiers, peer field redaction, random IDs); › “DATA-001 a trusted later erasure journal refuses an older backup and a new anonymous backup restores without certificate bytes” (restore fence); › “DATA-001 imported customer erasure removes retained source copies and shared originals without changing a peer customer” (named retirement preview, peer counts and later source omissions); › “DATA-001 erasure tombstones one CSV customer while peers retain source projections and exact Undo” (tombstones, peer projection and Undo); `tests/browser/journey.spec.ts` › “DATA-001 the owner exports and explicitly erases a merged customer with durable outcomes at 1440 and 390” (download, named preview before consent and erased end state); › “DATA-001 a technician reconnects and erases cached originals and queued captures only for explicitly erased visits at 1440 and 390” (device fences); `tests/portable.mjs` (real rer older-restore refusal and anonymous restore); `tests/api/customer-quotes.test.mjs` › “QUOTE-002 / COMM-001 the owner accepts concrete disclosure, the customer chooses the middle option and one add-on, requests a revision and signs its immutable exact copy” (shared signed-original withdrawal, retained peer quote and unaffected byte-exact original); `tests/browser/customer-quotes.spec.ts` › “QUOTE-002 owner enters disclosure; middle option and add-on become a revised drawn signature and exact job; 390px typed approval works entirely by keyboard” (named shared-copy preview and withdrawn customer/desk states at 1440/390).

#### DATA-002 — Every change, message and payment recorded.
Every command, message, call, payment, sync and agent action is on the timeline with who (or which agent or provider), when and what changed, readable in Activity and exportable.

*Acceptance:* named DATA-002 flows perform human and agent commands, workflow rule actions, calendar reads, audience pushes, model responses, CompanyCam photo syncs and Web Push setup/subscription/removal/outcomes, and find each current kind with who, when and what changed in Activity and the business export. A history beyond the latest window remains readable through Activity’s older-page door under the same role checks. The browser journey loads an older provider outcome in Activity at 1440 and 390. Future communication, payment and accounting flows assert their new kinds before shipping under the carriers below.

*Semantics (DATA-002, 19 October 2026; ct approved 13:06Z):* DONE covers every current capability. Every committed Glove command must supply an explicit timeline event; an eventless command is refused before its changes commit. An event keeps its actual person/agent/rule-creator identity, time, command id, subject and effect. The command's first event also keeps the ids, before/after versions and changed field names of its changed records, without copying private field values. Activity's changed-record details follow the same current visibility as the underlying records. Provider response facts identify the provider and a generic outcome, never credentials, endpoints, keys or payloads; current model, audience, CompanyCam and Web Push outcomes are recorded separately so the fact survives a lost final command commit. Web Push records subscription/technician/job ids and HTTP status only, including 404/410 and unreachable services; it never claims delivery to the phone. Subscription setup/removal, marketing disconnects, custom-field/tag/segment edits, stock locations, purchase-order revisions and rule changes/runs have their own explicit events. Activity loads older events by sequence, with current role/job checks on every page; its loaded history is discarded when the installation change version or reader changes. Export keeps the whole append-only timeline and provider references.

Every future clause adding a capability, provider, record kind or door inherits this obligation before it ships. The absent customer-message/call/portal kinds are carried by COMM-001/002/005/006/009, payments/refunds/payouts/office charges by PAY-002/004/007/009, and accounting syncs by LEDGER-001/002/003/004, each named in its text and acceptance. Homepage claim 55 includes those missing call/message/payment capabilities and stays partial.

PLAN-005 fixed-monthly invoices inherit its ct-approved period-end issue date, separate approval time, later-of-period/approval due-date basis, approval-gated aging and frozen report/export dates; preexisting documents keep their original basis. This is proven with PLAN-005’s named monthly-date flow before that capability ships.

*Status:* done — evidence `src/glove/storage.ts`, `src/glove/timeline.ts`, `src/glove/activity.ts`, `src/ui/activity.tsx`, `tests/api/records.test.mjs`, `tests/api/segments.test.mjs`, `tests/api/push.test.mjs`, `tests/api/eligibility.test.mjs`, `tests/api/suggest.test.mjs`, `tests/api/companycam.test.mjs`, `tests/api/rules.test.mjs`, `tests/api/stock.test.mjs`, `tests/api/purchasing.test.mjs`, `tests/browser/companycam.spec.ts`, `src/glove/rules.ts`.

#### DATA-003 — Hosted on the owner's own Cloudflare account.
`rer deploy` puts Glove on the owner's Cloudflare account with their domain, backups and the provider secrets above, and the hosted installation passes the same acceptance flows as a local one.

*Acceptance:* a hosted deployment runs the API flow suite against its own URL and a restore from its backup.

*Status:* partial — evidence `wrangler.jsonc`, `deploy.json`; gap: Glove runs locally; no hosted deployment has been accepted.

#### DATA-004 — Free, open source and run on the owner's own computer.
Glove is MIT-licensed with no per-technician fee; `rer install`, `rer setup` and `rer dev` run a complete installation on the owner's own computer from a fresh checkout, and the owner keeps every record.

*Acceptance:* the browser journeys run real `rer setup` and `rer dev` in a fresh copy and complete the owner, dispatcher, technician and office journey.

*Status:* done — evidence `LICENSE`, `tests/browser/run.mjs`, `tests/browser/journey.spec.ts`.

## Push further

Agents run the front office and chase the unfinished job, beyond today's receptionist and scheduling agents. Each clause names what the agent does, on which data, and under what human control. Every agent works as an invited sign-in with the Agent role through the public commands (`GLOVE-10`); what it may do on its own is only what the owner granted under `AGENT-009`, and every action is on the timeline.

### Agents that run the job

#### AGENT-001 — AI front office for calls and texts.
*What the agent does:* answers the business's calls and texts 24/7 (always, after hours or on overflow, by owner setting), speaks and writes like the business, identifies the caller and their property, qualifies the problem, quotes flat-rate prices from the price book, and books into a real open slot (`BOOK-002`, `DISP-006`), then texts the confirmation; every call and thread gets a written summary, and the lead's details (name, address, problem, equipment, urgency) are extracted onto the request.

*Data:* the price book, technician capacity, skills and areas, the customer's history and equipment, and the owner's call script and policies.

*Human control:* the owner sets what it may book and quote, and the price limits; anything outside goes to a person as a prepared request; emergencies the owner names (gas smell, flooding, no heat in freezing weather) transfer to the on-call person at once; callers hear that they are speaking with an automated assistant and can reach a person.

*Acceptance:* a scripted-call journey (telephony and model fakes) books a tune-up into the right slot, refuses an out-of-policy price and hands it to the office, and transfers a gas-smell call within the owner's limit. An inbound text thread is answered by the agent and books a slot, with the confirmation text sent. With the mode set to after hours, a call inside business hours rings the office and the agent does not answer; with overflow, the agent answers only a call the office leaves ringing past the owner's limit. Every call transcript and text thread opens with the automated-assistant disclosure; saying or texting "talk to a person" transfers the call, or hands the thread to the office inbox. The request carries the extracted name, address, problem and urgency, and the call, transcript, thread, summary and booking are on the customer timeline. `AGENT-005` drafts a quote from a request the agent took with the call's transcript in its evidence (model fake). It also carries `DISP-006`: a booking the front office makes is assigned automatically.

COMM-007 inheritance: its sent and received messages (or agent conversations) appear in the COMM-007 stream in order, with direction, channel and author.

*Status:* missing — gap: no telephony or answering agent.

#### AGENT-002 — Ready-to-dispatch check the day before.
*What the agent does:* for each next-day visit, confirms access (gate codes, pets, who will be home) with the customer by text, checks the parts the job needs are on the van or at the supplier (`STOCK-*`), and that permits, deposits and equipment history are in place; marks each visit ready or not ready with the reason.

*Data:* tomorrow's visits, customer threads, van stock, purchase orders and the job's quote lines.

*Human control:* the agent texts only from owner-approved templates; anything not ready goes to the dispatcher with the agent's proposed fix; the agent books and cancels nothing.

*Acceptance:* a flow test with five next-day visits (provider fakes) gets one access confirmation, one missing part and one unanswered customer, and checks the ready board and the three proposals.

*Status:* missing — gap: no ready-to-dispatch agent: no next-day access confirmation by text (no SMS, `COMM-002`), no ready or not-ready marking with its reason, no ready board and no proposals to the dispatcher; the agent can read `STOCK-001`'s parts check and job purchase orders (`STOCK-002`), which are those clauses' doors, not this workflow.

#### AGENT-003 — Rebook around delays.
*What the agent does:* watches the day (technician locations, running timers, supplier delays, cancellations) and when a visit is likely to be missed, prepares a feasible reschedule that respects windows and skills, with options to offer the customer.

*Data:* the dispatch board, `DISP-005` locations, `COST-001` timers, purchase orders and customer preferences.

*Human control:* the dispatcher approves moved appointments (`GLOVE-07` review applies); the customer confirms a changed window by reply; with an `AGENT-009` grant, the agent may move a visit within the same day and window and text the customer itself.

*Acceptance:* a flow test runs a job long, the agent proposes moving the next visit to another technician, the dispatcher approves, and the customer's confirmation reply lands on the visit.

*Semantics (AGENT-003, 20 October 2026; ct approved as pattern-1 DONE):* `GET /api/glove/delays?at=` (the desk and agents; never field roles) watches today's signals that exist. A booked visit not yet arrived is at risk when its technician is still on an earlier visit that day after that visit's booked end (a running `COST-001` job timer, or arrival without completion): Glove expects them free at the moment of reading plus the overrun so far, at least 15 minutes, and flags their later visits that day starting before then. A visit is also at risk while a purchase order for its job (`STOCK-002`) is not fully received. For an overrun, the watch offers each other active technician free at the same planned time (same branch, skills, areas, time off, calendars and other visits checked; cancelled visits free their slots), then the same technician at the expected free time rounded up to 15 minutes. The agent (or the owner or a dispatcher) prepares a move as a proposal (`proposal.prepare {kind: "move", visitId, plan}`); someone other than the preparer approves it with `GLOVE-07` conflict review, and a visit changed since preparation needs a new proposal. A move that keeps the planned time, or keeps the start inside the visit's arrival window (`DISP-006`) on the same day, keeps that window; any other move drops it and is labelled "customer not yet asked". Under an `AGENT-009` grant the agent moves a visit itself only within its day, for the same technician, with the start inside its arrival window, and never changes the window. The customer's confirmation by reply and the agent's text are carried by `COMM-002` and `COMM-005`, technician locations by `DISP-005`, each naming `AGENT-003` in its text and acceptance; customer preferences join when a clause records them.

*Status:* done — evidence `src/glove/delays.ts`, `src/glove/commands/dispatch.ts`, `src/glove/commands/grants.ts`, `src/ui/dispatch-cards.tsx`, `tests/api/delays.test.mjs`.

#### AGENT-004 — Chase every invoice until paid and reconciled.
*What the agent does:* compares accepted scope, technician evidence, invoices, payments and the books; prepares invoices for completed unbilled work, sends payment nudges within the reminder policy, and prepares specific fixes for anything that does not reconcile (a payment in Stripe but not in QuickBooks, a refund without a credit).

*Data:* jobs, invoices, payments, payouts and `LEDGER-*` sync state.

*Human control:* the office approves every invoice and every credit or refund; nudges follow owner-approved templates and stop at zero; the agent never posts to the books or moves money.

*Acceptance:* a flow test with an unbilled job, an overdue invoice and an unreconciled payment checks one invoice draft, the reminder sequence and one reconciliation proposal, and that nothing posts without approval.

*Semantics (26 October 2026, current collection doors):* an agent, owner or office person explicitly requests an invoice chase through the existing owner-approved PAY-005 email sequence. It cannot change wording or schedules, create a payment link, bypass consent, disputes or quiet hours, repeat a reserved step, or continue after zero balance. Owner authority and consent are rechecked by the same scheduler that handles ordinary reminders. Each request records its actual initiator. Reconciliation preparation compares a confirmed provider payment or refund with its approved invoice, accepted job total, field-evidence references and retained accounting outcome. It proposes verification of an unrecorded payment or inspection of a refund's possible missing credit; it never claims a fresh provider lookup or infers that a credit is absent. An uncertain accounting operation explicitly requires inspection, never resending. A separate signed-in Owner or Office person records approval or decline of the inspection proposal; the preparer cannot decide it. Even approval posts nothing, creates no credit and moves no money. Existing separately authorized accounting, credit and refund commands retain their human boundaries. Proposals follow existing branch, Activity, export and personal erasure custody.

*Additional preparatory semantics (26 October 2026):* an unscoped signed-in Owner may use `reconciliation.prepare` with a payout reference to compare its retained automatic payout snapshot or staff-recorded deposit batch against current source amounts, fees, invoice postings and payment outcomes. PAY-007 provider-wide visibility remains Owner-only; agents, tokens, custom roles and branch-limited people receive no payout inspection authority. A missing retained deposit or fee posting is an inspection question, never proof of a missing bank entry. No provider read or write occurs. Repeated current preparation reuses the proposal, changed records require a new comparison, a different Owner reviews it, and even approval has no accounting or payment effect. Customer erasure removes personal aggregate proposals. ADOPTED pending CT: retain Owner-only access for provider-wide comparisons; AGENT-004 remains PARTIAL until CT resolves agent access and the remaining complete comparison is proved.

CT scope ruling, 26 October 2026: The adopted provider-wide payout comparison is inspection by a signed-in Owner without branch limits. Agents, integration tokens, custom roles and branch-limited people receive no provider-wide payout facts or comparisons through any read, command result, proposal, model input, Activity, history or export. Existing payment-specific preparation retains its current role and branch limits. Comparing retained snapshots is not fresh provider verification. A recorded inspection decision grants no posting, credit, refund, allocation, provider connection or money-movement authority. Existing consequential commands retain their separate authorizations and approvals. The complete invoice, reminder and reconciliation target remains binding; this ruling does not authorize future agent reads or reduce that target to Owner inspection. AGENT-004 remains PARTIAL.

*Status:* partial — evidence `src/glove/collection-agent.ts`, `src/glove/reminders.ts`, `tests/api/flows.test.mjs` — “AGENT-004 prepares unbilled work, chases only approved reminder steps and proposes reconciliation without posting or moving money”. Gap: Owner-only retained payout comparison is implemented; agent access to provider-wide payout facts awaits CT review. Complete current-books discovery and fee and deposit posting readback remain unproved; retained outcomes are not fresh provider verification. The implemented command flow proves drafts, policy-bound email chasing, confirmed payment and refund inspection proposals, human decisions without posting, a subsequent explicit owner posting exactly once, stale and uncertain accounting refusals, branch isolation and personal erasure; it does not prove the entire agent target. Gap retained after CT ruling: The present Owner-only policy is decided; separately scoped future agent payout reads remain unapproved and unproved. Complete current-books discovery and fee and deposit posting readback remain unproved, and retained outcomes do not prove fresh provider verification.

#### AGENT-005 — Quote writer from the request.
*What the agent does:* from a request's description, photos, call transcript and the property's equipment age and history, drafts a good, better and best quote from the price book with the reasoning for each option.

*Data:* the request, photos, equipment, past jobs at the property and the price book.

*Human control:* a person approves each option's price (`GLOVE-03`); the agent never sends a quote.

*Acceptance:* a flow test drafts a quote for a 15-year-old furnace request (model fake) and checks three options from the price book with reasons, held as a draft until approved.

*Semantics (AGENT-005, 19 October 2026; data flow as ct approved 19 October 11:36Z):* the agent, the owner or a dispatcher asks for a draft on a new or contacted request that names its site, once the owner has connected the model provider (`FIELD-006`). The owner's provider receives, with store off and a strict answer schema, exactly: the request's description as entered and its source; the site's equipment (type, make, model, install and manufacture dates, age in whole years from the install date, else the manufacture date, to today in the business time zone, refrigerant) with each unit's earlier completed visits' dates and work summaries; the property's past job titles with their completed visits' summaries; and the active price book (code, name, the description customers see, unit, price before tax). Never costs or markups, the customer's name, contacts, billing or site address, technician names or serial numbers. Request photos are added separately only under BOOK-001’s consent, owner, metadata, training and withdrawal controls. The answer is a job title and three options, Good, Better and Best, each one to 20 lines of an active price-book code and a quantity (more than 0, up to three decimals) and a reason. Any other code, quantity or shape refuses the whole answer, with nothing saved, repaired or retried. Glove saves it as a draft quote on the request (the request becomes quoted) at the price book's prices, with each option's reason and the label "Drafted by the model from the request. A person approves each option's price before it is sent." A person approves the prices (`GLOVE-03`), and the agent can neither approve nor record a send. Requests may retain private office photos under BOOK-001’s partial intake slice, but only its separately consented sanitized copies join model evidence today; no call transcripts join: `BOOK-001` (request photos under its ct-approved consent, owner, metadata, training and withdrawal rules) and `AGENT-001` (the call transcript) add them to this evidence before they ship, in their text and acceptance.

*Status:* done — evidence `src/glove/commands/suggest.ts`, `src/ui/quotes.tsx`, `tests/api/suggest.test.mjs`, `src/glove/provider.ts`.

#### AGENT-006 — Repeat-callback diagnosis.
*What the agent does:* when a property calls back about equipment serviced recently, gathers the equipment history, earlier photos, readings, materials and the manufacturer's manual, and prepares a diagnosis checklist and parts plan for the technician.

*Data:* equipment records, form results, photos and materials at the property.

*Human control:* advisory only; the licensed technician diagnoses and decides the work, and the agent never certifies safety.

*Acceptance:* a flow test raises a callback on a condenser with two prior visits and checks the prepared checklist cites both visits' readings.

*Semantics (AGENT-006, 19 October 2026):* a new job (from a quote or a service call, which can name the site's equipment) is a callback when a unit it names had a visit on another job completed within the 30 days before the job was made, counted on the server's received completion time, the window `REPORT-002` counts callbacks in; the job carries that flag and its timeline says so. The agent (or the owner or a dispatcher) prepares the checklist with the owner's model provider (`FIELD-006`'s connection): Glove gathers the unit's record, manual, photos and every earlier completed visit on it (form readings with their ranges and out-of-range flags, refrigerant, notes, materials), oldest first, each visit and reading with a reference; the model gets the equipment and visit evidence and photo captions only, never customer names, contacts or addresses. Every checklist step and part must cite references the pack holds, and Glove resolves each citation to the recorded reading; an answer that cites nothing or anything else is refused and nothing is kept. The prepared checklist is labelled advisory (the licensed technician diagnoses and decides the work; it is not a safety certification), decides no work, and is read by the desk and the technicians assigned to the callback job.

*Status:* done — evidence `src/glove/callbacks.ts`, `src/glove/commands/callbacks.ts`, `src/glove/provider.ts`, `src/ui/nameplates.tsx`, `tests/api/callbacks.test.mjs`.

#### AGENT-007 — Keep every maintenance agreement fulfilled.
*What the agent does:* tracks agreements' due visits and entitlements, offers customers booking slots for due visits, chases unanswered ones, checks the promised maintenance was actually recorded, and prepares renewal outreach for agreements nearing their end.

*Data:* agreements, due visits, capacity, completed forms and customer threads.

*Human control:* the owner sets booking and renewal policy; customers choose their slots; technicians record completion; the agent changes no price or term.

*Acceptance:* a flow test with 20 due visits checks offers sent, 12 booked by customer reply, chases for the rest, and a flag on a completed visit with no maintenance form.

*Status:* missing — gap: no agreement agent: agreements, entitlements, the due list and booking-link offers ship under `PLAN-002` and `PLAN-004` for the desk (an agent reads them but cannot offer slots: `agreement.offer` is a desk command), and there are no agent offers, chases of unanswered customers, flags on a completed visit missing its maintenance form, or renewal outreach.

#### AGENT-008 — Morning brief and margin watch.
*What the agent does:* shows the owner a short morning brief when they open Glove: jobs at risk today, unbilled completed work, jobs over their cost estimate, overdue invoices, agreements due and leads waiting too long, each with one proposed next action. Nothing is pushed; the brief is emailed only when the owner turns on `COMM-001`'s morning email, which is off by default.

*Data:* `REPORT-*`, `COST-*`, `PAY-*` and `BOOK-*` records.

*Human control:* read-only; each proposed action opens the normal screen for a person to act.

*Acceptance:* a flow test with a fixture day checks every item in the brief matches its records and links to its action, and opening it sends no email or push. A browser journey opens Glove and reads the brief. `COMM-001` inherits morning email delivery with a provider-outcome assertion.

*Semantics (AGENT-008, 20 October 2026):* Glove prepares the brief itself from its records (no model), for the owner only, at `GET /api/glove/brief` and on the owner's Today screen, for the business's local date at the moment it is read (`at=` reads it as of another UTC moment); it writes nothing. At risk today: visits on that local date, not cancelled or completed, with no technician, not arrived 15 minutes after their start, or arrived and still open at or after their planned end (action: Dispatch). Unbilled: completed jobs whose invoice is missing (Prepare invoice) or still a draft (Approve invoice). Over estimate: open jobs whose actual cost exceeds a non-zero estimated cost as `COST-004` computes them (Review job costs); `COST-005` margin warnings stay the live watch. Leads waiting: requests still new or contacted 24 hours or more after entering that stage (Contact the customer / Quote the request). The brief is shown when the owner opens Glove and sends nothing by being opened; `COMM-001` emails it to the owner once per business-local date only when the owner turns that on (`mail.morning`, off by default), with the delivery outcome asserted in its flow, and nothing is pushed. The brief says overdue invoices are not tracked until `PAY-004` adds them; PAY-004 adds its linked section and assertion before it ships. PLAN-004 now lists outstanding due included visits with their agreement and Dispatch links, asserted in its named flow.

PLAN-005 fixed-monthly invoices inherit its ct-approved period-end issue date, separate approval time, later-of-period/approval due-date basis, approval-gated aging and frozen report/export dates; preexisting documents keep their original basis. This is proven with PLAN-005’s named monthly-date flow before that capability ships.

*Status:* done — evidence `src/glove/morning-brief.ts`, `src/ui/morning-brief.tsx`, `tests/api/morning-brief.test.mjs`, `tests/browser/morning-brief.spec.ts`.

#### AGENT-009 — Agent policy and autonomy grants.
*What the agent does:* nothing on its own; this clause is the owner's control. The owner grants named actions to a named agent (for example send reminder texts from a template, book into open slots for listed services under a price limit, move a visit within its window), with a daily budget and quiet hours; every granted action records the grant it used; one switch pauses every agent.

*Data:* the grant register and the timeline.

*Human control:* only the owner grants and revokes; a revoked grant applies to the next command; an agent can never grant itself anything or approve its own preparation.

*Acceptance:* a flow test grants an agent "book tune-ups under $200", checks a booking succeeds and records the grant, a $250 booking is refused, revocation refuses the next one, and the pause switch stops all agents.

*Status:* done — evidence `src/glove/commands/grants.ts`, `src/glove/api.ts`, `src/ui/agent-grants.tsx`, `tests/api/grants.test.mjs`, `tests/api/security.test.mjs`.

## Honest boundaries

Glove does not do these, and this is how a US buyer gets them.

| Glove does not | How buyers get it |
| --- | --- |
| Act as a telecom carrier, register numbers itself or run a full business phone system | Twilio (or another provider) holds the numbers, A2P 10DLC registration and delivery; RingCentral, Dialpad or the owner's system keeps extensions and IVR; Glove records consent and opt-outs and answers on its own numbers (`COMM-002`, `COMM-009`) |
| Process raw card numbers, underwrite credit or lend | Stripe holds card and bank data under its PCI scope, and saved cards move from the old processor to Stripe directly (`MOVE-006`); Wisetack decides and funds financing; Glove records provider-confirmed outcomes (`PAY-*`) |
| Keep the general ledger, file sales tax or run payroll | QuickBooks Online, Xero or Pantry keep the books; Avalara or the owner's accountant files sales tax; [Envelope](https://github.com/just-every/run-envelope), Gusto, ADP RUN or QuickBooks Payroll pay people and file W-2 and 1099 forms (`LEDGER-*`, `MONEY-001`, `COST-002`) |
| Operate maps, GPS hardware or lead marketplaces | Google Maps and routing providers, Linxup, Samsara or Verizon Connect for fleet GPS, Google, Angi, Thumbtack and Yelp for leads (`DISP-002`, `DISP-005`, `BOOK-004`) |
| Replace trade licensing, diagnosis or safe-work judgment | Agents are advisory on technical work; the licensed technician remains responsible for the work and equipment release (`AGENT-006`) |
| Give legal advice on consumer, auto-renewal, surcharging or recording-consent law | Glove carries the notices, consents and settings; the owner sets the wording their state requires |
| Run route optimization or media processing unbounded inside a request | Bounded background jobs and external providers (`DISP-003`, `FIELD-006`) |
| Become a marketing suite or a construction project manager | Mango or Mailchimp for campaigns; Plinth or Buildertrend for multi-month builds; related sibling: [Pegboard](https://github.com/just-every/run-pegboard) |

Physical receipt, completed repair, safe equipment and accepted work require human evidence; a database state, a provider acknowledgment or a restore is not that evidence. A real owner export from each incumbent has not yet been reconciled; each move-in kit is proven against documented-shape fixtures until one is. "Glove" is the working name.

## Homepage claims

Every capability the homepage states (the design desk's page-s1 structure, owner-approved 17 October 2026: one job, Ellen Brooks's water heater, from the call to a year of Comfort Club) maps to a clause above. Status is judged from the clauses' statuses at the judged commit: `done` when every backing clause is done, `missing` when none has started, otherwise `partial`. Code names the path that backs the claim today, "partly" with what exists, or "contract, not yet in code". A claim ahead of the code stays on the page; its clauses are what the build rounds deliver.

## Status

Judged from the code and tests at commit `a38248e45e342b14c9adb9d7f1a88148a790c5c8` (21 October 2026).

The heading retains the historical baseline. COMM-009 is updated to PARTIAL in this revision from its named local proofs; the handoff report identifies the exact candidate and its CI outcome. No new DONE is claimed.

`done` means the behavior ships and a cited test exercises it; `partial` means some of it ships and the Gap says what is missing; `missing` means there is no implementation. Status is a build-planning judgment, not acceptance; [ACCEPTANCE.md](ACCEPTANCE.md) decides acceptance.
